Anything else is not learned and is reported as `unverified_first` (critical when it hands out another router, as a hijack of the traffic path); register a legitimate one in `dhcp.allowed_servers`. When no comparison is possible (the gateway is unknown, an answer on another interface, DHCPv6 and so on), the first server is learned as before. **A rogue server that hands out the same router as the real gateway and only rewrites the DNS cannot be told apart from a real second server from outside. Use `dhcp.allowed_servers` to pin it.** A baseline learned before this change is not judged again.
- **Added: the Sensor deb and rpm are now built for arm64 too** (Raspberry Pi and others). The README and the description mentioned arm64, but the deb and rpm were amd64 only. The rpm release now carries the distribution name (`fedora` / `opensuse`) and both are built (the release stayed `1` and went to `rpm/1/`, so `dnf install roamswitch-sensor` did not work).
- **Changed: the deb and rpm now depend on the `ip` command (`iproute2` / `iproute`).** `ip` is used for the LAN range and for the IPv6 neighbor table and default route. Without `ip`, detection of a rogue IPv6 router becomes "not visible". nmap stays recommended.
- **Changed: dependencies were updated** (tokio 1.54.1 for advisory RUSTSEC-2026-0386, and the yanked `yoke-derive`). The README now explains installation with apt and dnf, arm64, and the rpm variant.
- Checks: all 670 workspace tests pass.

### 1.0.0

- **Changed: the version is now 1.0.0.** PersonalSOC, PathScope and AccessScope move to 1.0.0 together with it.
- **Fixed: an automatic feature that had stopped showed up neither in `status` nor in a notification.** The periodic audit, the periodic audit of unlisted devices and the automatic update of the device list (finding quiet devices) silently did nothing when nmap was not installed, when there was no address to audit, or when there was no identity binding. nmap is a recommended package, so this happened on hosts installed without it. The reason is now recorded as a condition that lasts until it is resolved, and `roamswitch-sensor status` shows it as a warning under "What is visible". If a notification destination is configured, one `coverage_gap` arrives, and `coverage_restored` arrives when it is fixed. During lockdown and outside the time window it is not a warning; `status` shows one line. A failure of the periodic audit of unlisted devices is now logged at warn instead of debug. The automatic update also logs once at warn that nmap is missing.
- **Added: `status` shows whether nmap is installed and the state of the automatic features** (CLI and TUI, 10 languages). It has an "nmap: not installed" line, the state of the periodic audit, the audit of unlisted devices and the notification destinations, and the last delivery result of notifications. Sensor installs without nmap, but without it the periodic audit, the periodic audit of unlisted devices and the active scan of the device list do not run.
- **Fixed: you could not tell that notifications had stopped arriving.** For each webhook and syslog destination it now keeps the last delivery result (success or failure, the time, the error). While failures continue, `notify_delivery_failed` appears in `status` and in notifications. The result of `roamswitch-sensor notify-test` goes to the same place. A failure record has no expiry; it clears when a delivery succeeds or `notify-test` passes.
- **Fixed: an audit that did not reach the device, and an empty list, were shown as "no problem".** When an audit finds no open port and gets no ARP reply either, it now says "may not have reached the device" instead of "no problems found". That is not "no problem". The nmap NSE line now tells "not run (nmap is not installed)" from "no match". The "no events" display of network-threats now carries a note about what is not visible.
- **Fixed: a passive-capture setting takes effect only after a restart.** After adding the environment variable you need `sudo systemctl restart roamswitch-sensor`; `systemctl edit` alone does not reach the running daemon. This was not written anywhere, and the unit name `roamswitch-sensor-daemon` in the setting's description does not exist. The name is now `roamswitch-sensor` (10 languages), and the README and the service file state the restart. Check that it took effect in `roamswitch-sensor status`: "passive capture: receiving on eth0". If it still says "passive capture is disabled", you have not restarted.
- **Defaults to check**: a missing nmap has severity medium (it is notified). A host installed without nmap gets one `coverage_gap` at start. A "no answer" failure of the audit of unlisted devices is also medium. Detection thresholds and defaults are unchanged. No automatic blocking was added.
- **Scope of verification**: in the lab (Docker containers) I checked the `status` line, the `coverage_gap` notification and the log in a container with nmap hidden, the delivery failure shown for a dead webhook, and the restart hint when passive capture is disabled. I did not look at it on a real screen. The added texts are based on machine translation and have not been reviewed by native speakers (other than Japanese).
- Verification: all workspace tests pass, and the lab checks (62 PASS and 22 PASS) pass.

### 0.2.1 - 0.4.6 (summarized)

The full text of each item is in this file's git history.

- **Control API and pairing hardened** (0.3.14): signatures gained a v3 bound to the Sensor's public key (sent by Mac 1.10.17 and later and Linux 1.10.18 and later), and a new install refuses v1 and, on a plaintext connection, v2. We tested that a downgrade from v3 is refused. When pairing, the client sends a proof of possession of its key (PoP, `control.require_pairing_pop`) so that, over a connection without a certificate pin, nobody can register another party's public key (confirmed on a real Mac). A new install defaults to required TLS, and a corrupted configuration file keeps the previous configuration. The local IPC is authorized by the uid and groups seen at connect time (fixing a race on a reused PID), and write operations and active scans are limited to root or the `roamswitch-sensor` group. A read timeout, limits on requests and simultaneous connections, a per-IP lockout, a per-key nonce limit and an expiry for pending audits were added, and `last_addr` is updated only over TLS, with changes recorded.
- **Collector hardened** (0.3.14): an endless pre-authentication body read is now bounded by a 32 MiB shared read budget and a 20-second deadline per body, and a stalled, timed-out or short body counts as a failure for the source IP. Per-IP connection limits, trusted-proxy support, replay rejection, a push limit, a default retention and streaming body reads were added, and new `http://` settings are refused. The push signature gained a v2 bound to the receiving collector, which stops a replay to another collector (`--collector-id` or ROAMSWITCH_COLLECTOR_ID on the collector, `collector.collector_id` on the Sensor; `--require-collector-binding` refuses unbound pushes).
- **Secret exposure and persistence fixed** (0.3.14): a failed webhook notification could put the URL, which carries a secret token, into the error text; only the kind of error is shown now. State files are written atomically and a corrupted one is moved aside, the private key is created exclusively with mode 0600, control characters are stripped, and a failed append to the audit log is reported. The TUI settings screen has rows for the settings above (10 languages).
- **Audit report dates** (0.3.13): HTML and Markdown reports showed raw UTC strings such as `2026-09-19T08:00:03.415602838+00:00`; they now show this machine's local time in each language's order, with the weekday and the UTC offset (English: "Sat, 2026-09-19 17:00:03 (UTC+9)"). The "Generated (UTC)" heading is now "Generated". CSV is meant for machines and stays in UTC.

- **Better network inventory** (0.2.1 to 0.2.7): always-on tracking of the network
  layout, plus extended detection (botnet/DDoS participation, DNS tunneling). An active
  ARP sweep equivalent to `nmap -sn` closed detection gaps (and a bug that dropped the
  Sensor itself from the list was fixed), and the network layout and ARP events were
  reworked to carry enough information to act on (refreshed hourly). Device notes were
  added, and TUI display bugs were fixed.
- **Audit operations features for security teams at larger organizations** (0.3.0):
  scheduled audits, diffs against the previous run, notifications (webhook and syslog),
  a tamper-evident operation log, export, retention, and a central collector that
  aggregates several Sensors. Everything is opt-in, and nothing is sent anywhere by
  default.
- **Fixed automatic updates of the CVE maps and the NSE script DB failing silently**
  under systemd's write restrictions (0.3.1 to 0.3.2).
- **Added a diff tab, an operation-log tab and export to the TUI** (0.3.4).
- **Broader active audits** (0.3.5): Elasticsearch, CouchDB, VNC, RDP and SMB added.
- **Slow ARP sweeps detected** (0.3.8): a long window notifies once 64 distinct targets are reached within 24 hours (a 2.5 s-per-address sweep fired at the 64th target). Requests for hosts known to be alive do not count toward it, and the number of tracked MAC addresses, targets per MAC and the live-host table are capped, so a flood of spoofed sources cannot grow memory without bound.
- **Repository size** (0.3.7): the apt and rpm repositories had kept every past version, so they were cut to the latest one (1.10.5 and later keep the latest three).
- **Control API TLS support, replay protection, and a fuller TUI** (0.3.9): the control API now speaks TLS 1.3 with certificate pinning (`control.tls`, default `optional`). Signed requests carry a timestamp (±300s) and a one-time value, with pairing brute force locked out per source and globally. The TUI now covers every function of the CLI and daemon (config editing, TLS management, manual pairing, event views, 10 languages). Added a bind-address setting, a switch to stop the CVE-map fetch, and a distinction between unpaired and unknown devices.
- **Pairing command guidance fixed** (0.3.10): the pairing command shown by the TUI and `sensor-cli issue-code` did not include the Sensor's own fingerprint (`--fingerprint`), so it always failed with Linux 1.9.90 and later clients. This is fixed.
- **Manual pairing screen explanation improved** (0.3.11): it now says that the fingerprint field is for the GUI (Mac) input field.
- **False-positive and alert-fatigue audit** (0.3.12): fixed 6 issues, including detections that spike in count (destination spread, destination concentration flood, malicious-destination contact, IoT behavior) sending an empty destination identity, so that the same kind of detection on another device was taken to be in cooldown and silently suppressed.
- **Data-protection fix** (0.3.15): when a state file could not be read or was corrupt, it was treated as empty and saved, wiping other data (the list of trusted devices, scan history, device inventory, settings, the ARP and passive-monitoring baselines, the audit log, the collector's per-Sensor state and the daemon's send position). Now only a missing file counts as empty; an unreadable file is not rewritten, a copy is kept as `<file>.corrupt-<time>` (0600, newest 3), and changing operations fail with a clear error. If the list of trusted devices cannot be read, nobody is trusted and pairing is refused (`sensor_storage_unavailable`; the pairing code is used up, so issue a new one after repair). Silently regenerating the TLS certificate when the private key was corrupt, and the collector accepting replays when a Sensor's state was corrupt, were fixed too. While a file is corrupt, ARP spoofing detection and new-device detection from passive monitoring stop (they resume after repair or deletion). The new errors are shown in 10 languages in the TUI and in Japanese and English in the CLI.
- **Data-protection and crash-resistance fixes** (0.3.16): the collector's retention prune stopped reading at a single line with invalid UTF-8 and replaced `scans.jsonl` and `events.jsonl` with the shortened content, losing every later record; only the damaged line is skipped now. The collector reported success even when appending a record failed, and since the Sensor did not resend, the record was lost; a failed append now returns a storage error and does not advance the state. After a crash in the middle of an append, the next record was glued onto the torn fragment and became unreadable (the collector's records and the audit log); fixed. In the audit log, one line with invalid UTF-8 hid all later entries and made a sequence number get reused, and an unreadable log "verified" as empty and fine; only the bad line is skipped now, an unreadable log counts as a failed verification, and a new chain is never started. An evidence export came out empty, like a clean "no scans, no devices" report, when a stored file was damaged; it is now aborted with an error (the IPC has a new `strict` parameter that reads strictly; the TUI shows it in 10 languages and the CLI in Japanese and English). `tls rotate` (the key and certificate update) writes the new key and certificate to temporary files, checks that they match and only then puts them in place, and at startup an interrupted rotation is completed or discarded so that the pair is consistent, so a crash in the middle can no longer leave a mismatched pair that stops the Sensor from starting (when it cannot be recovered, the Sensor stops with an error as before and keeps the files).
- **Keeping configuration and data across a downgrade** (0.3.16): when an older version saved a configuration or data written by a newer version, the newer fields were dropped. The configuration, trusted endpoints, scan history, device inventory, the ARP and passive baselines, the collector's per-Sensor state and the push cursor now keep unknown fields when saved (the audit log is unchanged because of its hash chain). When unknown fields are present, the keys of the configuration file are written in alphabetical order. The collector's state, `enrolled` and push-cursor files are written pretty-printed (the content is the same).
- **Safer file writing** (0.3.16): the CLI's `export --out` and `scan report --out` followed symbolic links, kept an existing file's mode, and defaulted to 0644. A symbolic link or a non-regular file is now refused (with the reason shown in Japanese or English), the file is written to a temporary file and then replaced, and the mode is 0600. Under sudo, a link planted in a shared directory could have redirected the write. In the TUI, overwriting a credential file (`collector-token.txt`) did not tighten its mode to 0600, and a crash while creating the identity file could stop the next start; both fixed.
- (0.4.0) **Added: detection of rogue DHCP servers (IPv4 and DHCPv6)**, on by default. Every hour (and on each `refresh`) the Sensor sends a single DHCP discover packet (a SOLICIT for DHCPv6) from a random MAC and counts every server that answers. It sends no request, so no address lease is taken. The first server it hears is learned as the legitimate one, and a server that appears later is reported. A server that hands out a gateway or DNS the legitimate one does not takes over where traffic goes, so it is critical. If several servers answer the first probe at once, none is learned and all are reported. `dhcp.allowed_servers` names the legitimate servers.
- (0.4.0) **Added: IPv6 detection and support**, on by default. It reports router advertisements from anything other than the IPv4 gateway, an IPv6 address changing hands (NDP spoofing), and devices visible only over IPv6. The device inventory now holds IPv6 addresses (the `ipv6` CSV column, `ipv6_addrs` in JSON). The control API (50543) can also listen on IPv6 with `control.listen_ipv6` (off by default, applied on restart). Connection limits and lockouts count IPv6 per /64. A network whose IPv6 router is a different device from its IPv4 gateway is a normal case, so list that router in `ipv6.allowed_routers`.
- (0.4.0) **Added: a light audit of devices without RoamSwitch**, opt-in and off by default. Only for devices inside the ranges the operator authorized, it makes TCP connections to a fixed set of 54 ports and reads what the services announce themselves. It does not attempt logins, exploit vulnerabilities, run nmap NSE or scan every port; deep diagnostics in the style of OpenVAS are done only to endpoints that agreed (paired). To enable it, first set `unmanaged_audit.authorization` (who approved it) and `unmanaged_audit.allowed_cidrs` (the ranges; IPv4 wider than /16 and IPv6 wider than /48 are refused). The device's MAC is checked before and after, and the run stops if the address changed hands. The authorization text is written to the operation log and the exports with every run. New: `audit-unmanaged <IP|MAC> --confirm` and `unmanaged-audits`, and in the TUI an "Unmanaged audits" tab and the `a` key.
- (0.4.0) **Added: revocation and lockdown for a Sensor that is lost, stolen or retired.** After `revoke --reason` at the collector, that Sensor's uploads are refused with `403 sensor_revoked` (given only to requests with a valid signature), and the Sensor stops itself on its next upload. Locked down, it stops pairing, audits, uploads to the collector, notifications and probing of the network, and the control API answers only `sensor_locked_down`. The stop is stored in a file, so it survives a restart, and it stays in force if that file cannot be read. `security.on_revoked` chooses `lockdown` (default), `wipe` (overwrite with zeros and delete the keys, settings, paired endpoints, audit results and inventory, keeping only the operation log) or `none`. `security.lockdown_after_offline_days` stops a Sensor that has not reached the collector for that long (off by default). New: `lockdown on|off|status`, `decommission --confirm` and the `K` key in the TUI. `revoke` and `decommission` print the endpoints that were paired with the Sensor and how to unpair them.
- (0.4.0) **Note: the endpoints that were paired with a revoked Sensor keep trusting it until each one removes the pairing.** A Sensor has no way to notify them (Linux: `roamswitch sensor unpair <this Sensor's public key>`; Mac: Settings > Sensor > Unpair). A Sensor carried off while powered down never receives the instruction to stop; disk encryption is the real protection.
- (0.4.0) **Added: an `unmanaged` table in the evidence export** (CSV) and a light-audit section in the JSON and HTML reports. The inventory CSV has an IPv6 column. The collector dashboard lists revoked Sensors.
- (0.4.0) **Verified with real packets.** On a throw-away Docker LAN, 35 checks cover DHCP and DHCPv6 servers, a rogue router advertisement, NDP spoofing, the light audit, the IPv6 listener, and revocation, lockdown, wiping and the offline stop (`scripts/lab/run.py`). It has not yet been checked under a real systemd (.deb) or on a physical switch. DHCP probing does not work on a Sensor connected over Wi-Fi; wired operation is assumed.
- (0.4.1) **New: detecting changes from each device's usual behavior** (on by default, `behavior.enabled`). The Sensor learns the hourly behavior of each device (identified by MAC, grouped by the existing device class) and reports unusual changes. It looks at bytes sent to public addresses, the number of public address ranges contacted for the first time, the number of LAN peers, DNS queries, data-carrying packets, and the ports used (including admin ports the device has never used). After the learning period (14 days by default, `behavior.learning_days`) it compares each hour with the past values of the same weekday-or-weekend hour (median and spread) and explains in words what changed compared with usual, such as "this camera used port 22 for the first time" or "outbound volume is 40 times the usual". Severity is low or medium, and **this finding alone never triggers lockdown or any other automatic blocking**. Sensitivity is `behavior.sensitivity` (low / normal / high).
- (0.4.1) What it learns is stored in a state file (0600). Destination addresses are not stored, only a keyed Bloom filter. A new device that is still learning borrows the history of devices of the same class and reports only large changes. General-purpose devices such as PCs and phones are left out by default because their behavior changes by the hour (`behavior.include_general_purpose`). The CLI gained `behavior [--detail]`, `behavior reset <MAC>` and `network-threats`; the TUI gained a "Behavior baseline" tab. TUI text is in 10 languages and the CLI in Japanese and English.
- (0.4.1) **Measured effect**: measured on simulated devices (cameras, printers, plugs, speakers, NAS boxes, IoT modules) and simulated anomalies. It has not been checked on real devices or real traffic. False alarms were about 0.05 to 0.09 per device per week at normal (about 0.44 for NAS boxes, caused by cloud-sync days). Detection at normal was 100% for a new port, 97% for LAN fan-out, 94% for a DNS surge, 83% for 3 a.m. activity and 100% for a surge of new destinations. Outbound volume increases were caught only 40% of the time (10%, 35% and 76% at 3x, 10x and 40x the usual) and are harder to see on low-traffic devices. A slowly growing upload was caught during the ramp 68% of the time.
- (0.4.1) **Notes**: the thresholds were fitted to the simulated noise, so a real LAN may produce more false alarms; if so, set `behavior.sensitivity` to `low`. A device that was already compromised while it was learning has that behavior as its baseline. A Sensor on an access port sees only broadcast and multicast traffic. IPv6 and daylight-saving changes were not checked.
- (0.4.2) **New: the light audit of unmanaged devices can now check whether a device is a product with vulnerabilities in the CISA KEV catalog** (`unmanaged_audit.product_check`, **off by default**). It reads the product name from the web page a device normally returns and tells you whether that product has KEV-listed vulnerabilities. Covered: Fortinet FortiGate, Ivanti Connect Secure, Citrix NetScaler, Cisco (the ASA SSL VPN and the IOS XE web UI), UniFi OS, GeoVision, TerraMaster, Check Point Security Gateway and MikroTik RouterOS (Aruba devices have no entry in the KEV and are not covered).
- (0.4.2) **All it sends is a `GET /` to the web ports that accepted a connection (80, 8080, 8181, 443, 8443, 10443), the redirects the device itself points to at the same address (at most two), and a read of the HTTPS certificate's issuer and subject names.** It sends no logins, no password attempts, no guessed paths and nothing that exploits a vulnerability. The limit is 12 requests per device, 150 ms apart. The consent conditions (`enabled`, `authorization`, `allowed_cidrs`, `exclude`, the exclusion of paired devices and the MAC re-check) are those of the light audit, and nothing is sent while the light audit is off.
- (0.4.2) **Verdicts**: a product is named only when its on-screen markers all appear in the same response. It is reported as affected (high) only when the version the device displays can be read and falls in an NVD affected range; when the version cannot be read it stays a notice (medium): this product has KEV-listed vulnerabilities, the version could not be read, so it is unconfirmed. Today only MikroTik RouterOS shows its version, so other products normally get the unconfirmed notice.
- (0.4.2) A copy of the KEV ships with the product and is refreshed, only when both `product_check` and `updates.fetch_cve_map` are on, by fetching one public CISA feed file every 24 hours (nothing about your LAN is sent).
- (0.4.2) **Limits**: the markers are based on public detection signatures and have not been checked against real devices; a marker that does not match errs on the side of detecting nothing. The traffic with and without consent was checked against dummy web servers in Docker.
- (0.4.2) The new texts are in 10 languages (based on machine translation, not reviewed by native speakers). The design notes are in `docs/SENSOR.md`.
- (0.4.2) Fixed: the update-settings initializer now matches the latest RoamSwitch for Linux core (it no longer built after the Web Rules fields were added).
- **Following the core** (0.4.3): the update settings are initialised again to match RoamSwitch for Linux core 1.12.7, which added the Ubuntu package CVE map. Sensor does not fetch that map, just like the other package CVE maps. Sensor's network behaviour is unchanged.
- (0.4.4) **Added: the Sensor now counts what it could not see and shows it in `status` and in notifications.** Before, not seeing something left either one log line or nothing at all, and could not be told apart from "nothing was found". What is counted: passive capture that could not be opened (before, only a log line, and passive monitoring stopped silently), receive errors (before, a busy loop, so it now waits 100 ms), frames cut off above 2048 bytes (detected with `MSG_TRUNC`), packets the kernel dropped (`PACKET_STATISTICS`), frames that could not be parsed, and checks that failed (the ARP table, the IPv6 neighbour table and routes, the baseline files). It shows in `status` of the CLI and TUI, in the push to the collector, and on the collector's screen.
- (0.4.4) **Fixed: an unreadable ARP table was treated as an empty table.** An empty table could replace the baseline and blind the spoofing check for that cycle. A cycle that could not be read is now skipped and counted. The IPv6 neighbour table and routes (when `ip -6` fails) are likewise counted instead of looking empty.
- (0.4.4) **Added: it tells a quiet capture from a broken one.** The capture loop wakes every second, so a stale wake-up time means it is stuck. A capture that is quiet because the link is down is reported as a loss (`capture_silent` after 5 quiet minutes, `capture_link_down` when the link is down). The Sensor's own transmissions are counted separately, so a deaf capture cannot look alive because it hears itself.
- (0.4.4) **Added: events for the start and end of a gap** (`coverage_gap`, `coverage_restored`). They go only to the notification destinations the operator has already configured. Notes about quiet stretches stay on screen and are not notified. **No new network traffic is added.** The push to the collector gains the items `capture` and `coverage_notes` (additions only, length-limited, control characters removed).
- (0.4.4) Checked with: a lab using real packets, real webhook delivery and a downed link (`scripts/lab/coverage.py`, 11 checks), and the existing lab (`run.py`); both pass. A capture that stayed quiet for over 5 minutes was reported as `capture_silent`, and then taking the link down gave `capture_link_down`. On a Docker bridge it reported that the kernel dropped 263 of 1484 packets (about 15%), which looks like real loss under bursty traffic; the receive buffer was not changed. On a network without IPv6, the IPv6 check now shows up as a skipped check (before, only a debug log line).
- (0.4.4) Limits: the part where the collector silently skips broken lines is unchanged this time because it would change the shape of its responses. The Sensor was built and tested only on Linux (it does not build on macOS). The screens were checked through tests only, not on a real display. The TUI texts (12) are in 10 languages (other than Japanese and English they are based on machine translation and have not been reviewed by native speakers).
- (0.4.4) The RoamSwitch for Linux core it depends on is now 1.12.8 (`Cargo.lock`).
- **The collector counts and shows what it could not read from its history files** (0.4.5): in the scan history (`scans.jsonl`) and the event history (`events.jsonl`), files that cannot be opened, broken lines, the position where a read error stopped the read, and failures of the retention pass are shown on the dashboard and in the startup log. `/api/overview` gained `storage_health` (an added key). No new network traffic is added.
- **Fix: webhook delivery no longer follows redirects and treats them as a failure**. The destination is a URL the operator typed in, so a 3xx response never sends events to another host. `ureq` returns a 3xx as a success once redirects are off, so it is now converted to a failure. Tests confirm that a redirect target listening on another port is never contacted and that a 204 counts as success. (0.4.6)
- **Change: the "please wait" wording is removed from the notice shown while a scan is running** (TUI text, 10 languages). (0.4.6)
- Follows the RoamSwitch for Linux core 1.14.0 (`Cargo.lock`). Sensor is built and tested on Linux only. (0.4.6)


### 0.2.0

- **Added: IoT-device-security-focused ARP events, in four tiers.**
  Previously an ARP event was just a bare MAC/IP diff with nothing
  actionable in it.
  - Tier 1: MAC-vendor classification against a curated, verified subset
    of the IEEE OUI registry (camera/smart-plug/etc. categories), a
    persistent per-device inventory (`sensor-cli device-inventory`), and
    detection of a single MAC issuing ARP requests for an unusual number
    of distinct IPs (possible LAN-internal reconnaissance).
  - Tier 2: reads self-announced mDNS/SSDP/DHCP identifiers (extends the
    existing passive packet-capture path) for more precise, model-level
    device identification than the OUI alone.
  - Tier 3: cross-references classified IoT devices' traffic against
    known-malicious destinations and admin-port contact on other LAN
    devices, with device context attached (`sensor-cli iot-events`,
    requires passive-capture to be configured).
  - Tier 4: a small, hand-verified reference table of real public CVEs
    for a handful of IoT vendors (e.g. Hikvision camera auth-bypass/RCE
    flaws) via `sensor-cli iot-advisories` — static, not a
    network-refreshed feed; that's out of scope for this release.
  - All four tiers are detection/reference only — Sensor never blocks
    traffic. Device classification is also shown inline in the
    `sensor-tui` ARP events tab.

### 0.1.0 - 0.1.7

- **Distributed as deb/rpm packages** (0.1.0), running as a systemd service. Extended
  passive LAN visibility (opt-in) was added too.
- **Pairing moved from mDNS discovery to a pairing code** (0.1.3). mDNS works only
  within one LAN segment, broadcasts constantly, and has no strong authentication by
  default. The Sensor sits at a fixed IP and issues a one-time code (8 characters,
  expires in 10 minutes), authenticated with Ed25519 signatures. Accepting audit
  requests from clients and making the nmap NSE supplementary scan always-on came in
  the same release.
- **Audit status badges for paired endpoints, and viewing the latest report** (0.1.4).
  Manual pairing was removed in favor of the code method, and the IP address is always
  shown.
- **Fixes** (0.1.5 to 0.1.7): several audits running in parallel against the same
  endpoint; the daemon crashing on `Too many open files` and losing in-flight audits
  (the file descriptor limit is now 65536); the detailed description missing from
  audit findings.

## RoamSwitch for Linux

The Linux edition (systemd + nftables), distributed via apt / dnf / zypper
(GPG‑signed). See <https://lafine.net/linux>.

### 1.17.11

- **Added: a notification when the item "Exploitable vulnerabilities (installed software)" newly fails** (Client Edition and Server Edition). It notifies once in any of these cases: a vulnerability that the last notification did not list is added, a vulnerability that is already listed gets a KEV mark, or 24 hours pass with the same contents. The Server Edition sends an alert to the operator (with the host name and IP address); the Client Edition sends a desktop notification. When an update brings the item back to pass, no notification is sent and the state is cleared. The same contents are not notified again at every restart (the state is kept in `/var/lib/roamswitch/cve_exposure_notice.json`). There is no setting key for the notification. On the Client Edition, `finding_history_enabled=false` stops the diagnosis itself.
- Note: the daemons already ran the diagnosis every hour, but nothing was notified when this item failed (only a stopped eBPF guard was notified). It was visible only to whoever opened the diagnosis report.
- Checks: the 4037 workspace tests, except the eBPF XDP crate, pass (Docker). A real daemon was run in a Debian 12 container with PHP 8.2.32 before the fix: the Server Edition sent the notification at its first diagnosis, 90 seconds after the start, did not repeat it after a restart, and cleared its state without a notification after the update to 8.2.35. The Client Edition also sent the notification at its first diagnosis.

### 1.17.10

**Changes in behavior come first.**

- **Changed: an action that asks for confirmation stops with exit code 2, without reading any input, when there is no terminal and no `--yes`** (`roamswitch install`, `uninstall`, `sensor repin`, `fleet unenroll`). Before, `install` with a closed standard input went on to run the install scripts without asking (they run inside the sandbox). `uninstall` also went on without asking. `sensor repin` and `fleet unenroll` quietly aborted and returned exit code 0. `emergency-restore` still goes on without asking when the cause is not in doubt and there is no terminal (it is meant for an emergency over ssh).
- **Changed: the manual scan and the quick scan of the GTK app count a file that neither clamd nor clamscan answered for as "could not be checked", and do not announce "clean"** (10 languages). This covers ClamAV being unavailable or returning an error (for example when it cannot load its signature database). Before, the app announced that all files were scanned and no threat was found.

- **Added: `run_active_vuln_scan` takes a `format` argument** (`json` by default, or `sarif`). It returns SARIF 2.1.0. The rule ID is the probe name, and the log carries `evidenceKind`, the checks that could not be made (`inconclusive`) and whether the scan was complete (`complete`). A rule that SARIF rule IDs are never renamed is written down, with a test that fails when a published ID is removed or renamed.
- **Added: the dependency source scan (`dependencyConfusion` of `run_package_lifecycle_script_scan`, the CLI and the window) reports `release_age_gate_missing`.** For a project with an npm, pnpm, yarn Berry, bun or uv lockfile, it reports with Low severity when neither the project nor the home folder sets a rule that keeps out versions published a moment ago. The settings it looks for are npm `min-release-age` or `before`, pnpm `minimumReleaseAge`, yarn Berry `npmMinimalAgeGate`, bun `minimumReleaseAge` and uv `exclude-newer`. It does not say that a bad package is installed. pnpm 11 and later is on by default, so it is not reported. Environment variables and settings elsewhere are not read. It uses no network (10 languages).
- **Added: `get_incident_timeline` returns `incidents` (events put together per incident) and `openIncidentCount`.** Events in the same chain of cause, or about the same process within 15 minutes, become one incident. `events` stays the record, and the grouping is not proof of one attack. A saved evidence bundle, a Fleet event and an expired risk acceptance do not count as detections, and an incident made only of them is `recorded`.
- Checks: the workspace tests, excluding the eBPF XDP crate, pass: 4023 tests (15 ignored; Docker). `cargo audit` reports 0 vulnerabilities.

### 1.17.9

**Changes in behavior come first.**

- **Changed: `roamswitch php-check` is removed** (added in 1.17.2). The PHP judgment is now part of a new item of the periodic diagnosis (see "Added" below).
- **Changed: the text of "Automatic security updates" now matches what is checked.** The item looks at whether an automatic update mechanism such as unattended-upgrades or dnf-automatic is set up and enabled. The flat statements "applying critical patches" and "the configuration is optimal" are gone, and so is the text that said "unattended-upgrades is enabled" on dnf-based machines (10 languages).

- **Added: the periodic diagnosis has a new item, "Vulnerabilities likely to be exploited (installed software)"** (Client Edition 30 to 31 items, Server Edition 40 to 41). It compares the installed OS packages with the known-CVE data on the machine. It fails when a vulnerability that CISA has confirmed as exploited (KEV), or whose probability of exploitation (EPSS) is 0.9 or higher, is still unfixed. With no data, or on an OS it does not cover, it shows "cannot be confirmed" and takes no points off. It uses no network. The result is kept for 30 minutes.
- **Added: PHP installed outside the package manager is checked by the same item** (official Docker images, builds from source and the like). The version and the configuration are read directly, to judge the mod_php version, `session.upload_progress`, and CVE-2026-115560, which depends on a combination of settings.
- **Added: a KEV list is delivered every day.** It is a signed file of about 30 KB that holds only CVE numbers, and it is added to the KEV marks in the data that is delivered once a week. A vulnerability that entered KEV after the data was built gets its mark by the next day. On the Server Edition it follows the operator's switch for outside communication, like the other data.

- **Fixed: on Debian-based systems an unpatched PHP was reported as "none found".** The package check did not match the binary package name (`php8.2-cli`) with the source name in the data (`php8.2`). It now matches by source name and source version. It also looks only at the fixed versions for the machine's own Debian release, which removes false failures of `git` and `xz-utils` on patched machines. This also changes the results of `scan-packages`, MCP and the window's check, so results can change.
- Checks: 3,988 tests of the workspace pass.

### 1.10.0 - 1.17.8 (summarized)

Everything in these releases, grouped by topic (Client and Server Edition unless marked). The full text of each entry is in the git history of this file.

- **Stronger Web Access Guard** (1.17.8, Server Edition): it detects brute-force attempts on login pages (`/login`, `/wp-login.php`, `/xmlrpc.php` and others) with `login=off|warn|block` (default `warn` = notify only; `login_limit` defaults to 20 and `login_spray_ips` to 10). A run from one source is `login_burst`; when 10 or more sources have 3 or more attempts, it is `login_spray`. Redirects (3xx), server errors (5xx) and "no such page" responses (404, 405, 410) are not counted, and a source whose login succeeded is excluded. A source that was blocked again and again is blocked 4 times longer than the previous block, up to `ban_max_secs` (default 7 days; on by default; the history is `/var/lib/roamswitch/web_guard_strikes.json`; `roamswitch unban` removes the pending hold but the count stays). A source that appears in both the Web guard and the SSH guard is remembered for 24 hours, the SSH failure threshold drops to a third (at least 2), and the block becomes one step longer (only the first time it appears in both is notified; of the sources detected on the web, only those that can be blocked, meaning not shared, private or excluded addresses, are recorded). In a disposable Ubuntu 24.04 virtual machine, a source detected on the web was blocked for 4 hours at the second failure (the control: the sixth failure, 1 hour).
- **`Wants=nftables.service` was removed from the Server unit** (1.17.8): starting `nftables.service` runs `flush ruleset` from `/etc/nftables.conf` and deletes the `DOCKER` chain of the NAT table of a running Docker (the next `docker run -p` fails until Docker is restarted). The start of `roamswitch-server` was waking a stopped `nftables` through `Wants`. The daemon installs its own table with `nft -f -`, so `nftables.service` is not needed (the ordering `After` was kept). `scripts/install.sh` also no longer starts `nftables` immediately while Docker is running. In a virtual machine, `docker run -p` failed with `Wants` and worked without it.
- **Display of the table of suspended processes** (1.17.8): the PID shown on the rows of children, siblings and parents suspended together is the PID of the detected process, not of that row's parent. The display now says "suspended together with the detected process", and the field in the table is named `stopped_with`. The key in the table file is still written as `parent`, and `stopped_with` can be read too, so old tables and old versions both work.
- **Six more MCP tools** (1.17.8): `get_honeytoken_status`, `get_ransomware_entropy_guard_status`, `get_lan_sensor_overview`, `get_lan_network_threats`, `get_privilege_escalation_paths` and `get_access_log_report` (45 in total, 42 of them read-only). PathScope and AccessScope run only root-owned executables at fixed absolute paths. Sensor is asked only through two read methods. Paths of other users are not shown to anyone but an administrator. Checked by unit tests only.
- **Record of entropy-based ransomware detections** (1.17.8, Client Edition and Server Edition): it is saved to `/var/lib/roamswitch/entropy_incidents.json`. The Server Edition also writes it to the incident timeline.
- **EXR-002 (execution from a temporary folder) is reliably detected for short-lived processes** (1.17.8, Server Edition): in a loaded virtual machine, running a copy of `ls` placed in `/var/tmp` 300 times as `www-data` left about 18% ending before `/proc/<pid>/exe` was read, giving a record with an empty executable, and EXR-002 could not be judged. A notify-only `FAN_OPEN_EXEC` is set on the file systems that hold `/tmp`, `/var/tmp` and `/dev/shm`, and the path the event carries is remembered for 5 seconds per process. Only a record whose executable could not be read looks it up, and an event of a dynamic loader never overwrites it. With this, the missing 18% went to 0%. Where `fanotify` is unavailable, nothing changes.
- **Bundled apps** (1.17.8): updated to AccessScope 1.0.3, PersonalSOC 1.0.2 and PathScope 1.0.2.
- **Publishing the apt and rpm repositories failed at the rpm signature** (1.17.8): the CI runner for publishing became Ubuntu 26.04 (rpm 6), and the signing call did not work with rpm 6. Signing now works with both rpm 6 and the older rpm 4.x. The 1.17.7 tag was not published to apt and rpm because of this failure, so the same content was released as 1.17.8 (the GitHub release 1.17.7 still holds packages with the same content).
- **Probing detection in the web access guard** (1.17.5, Server Edition): it finds the probing of secret and configuration files even on a site that answers 200 to every path (`probe_walk`). Paths such as `.env`, `.git`, `.aws`, `actuator` and `phpmyadmin` are counted by kind, whatever the answer. A client that asks, within `window_secs`, for 6 or more different kinds (`probe_walk_limit`, default 6), or for 12 or more different paths of the secret kinds (`path_walk_limit`), is treated like `path_walk`: a notice, and a block when `recon=block`. It is never reported together with `path_walk`. A WordPress administrator who opens many paths under `/wp-admin/` still shows one kind. About 2 weeks of the logs of 3 real sites gave 49 scanners that had been missed; search-engine crawlers and the operator's own IP were not matched. The setting is `probe_walk_limit` in `web_guard.conf` (3 to 10000). The bundled apps became AccessScope 1.0.1 (PersonalSOC 1.0.0 and PathScope 1.0.0).
- **Bundled apps** (1.11.0 to 1.11.2): 1.11.0 bundled AccessScope 0.2.0 and PersonalSOC 0.1.9, 1.11.1 AccessScope 0.3.0 and PathScope 0.1.7, and 1.11.2 AccessScope 0.3.1 (see each app's entry). From 1.11.2 the Server Edition deb and rpm also carry the `personalsoc` and `pathscope` commands next to `accessscope`; all three are commands only, with no window app and no WebKitGTK. The commands themselves do not use the network. Only when you pass `personalsoc --llm`, a summary of the diagnosis can be sent to an external service through the LLM command the operator chose (off by default, and outside Zero Telemetry). `pathscope` does not call an LLM. A standalone package with the same name is replaced. The client deb, rpm and tarball also carry the window apps.
- **Server Edition: a default network use** (1.11.0): the kernel CVE map update is now on by default (`cve_kernel_map_updates_enabled`). It used to connect only when the operator turned it on. `roamswitch-updater` checks a signed manifest every day and, only when there is a newer version, fetches the data itself from `lafine.net` about once every 30 days. Both are anonymous HTTPS GETs (no query string, cookie or identifying header; nothing about this host is sent), and only data whose Ed25519 signature verifies is used. It is receive-only. An explicit `false` in `server.conf` is respected, and a value that cannot be read turns it off so that nothing connects by accident. To stop it, use `roamswitch server config set cve_kernel_map_updates_enabled false`, the setup wizard (the default answer is Y) or the server TUI. The diagnosis keeps working without it, judging only from the data in the package. Apart from OS package updates, this is the only outside connection the Server Edition makes by default. The description of the update frequency was also corrected (the manifest check is daily; the kernel, OS package and language CVE maps are fetched only when a newer version exists, about once every 30 days).
- **MCP and diagnosis additions** (1.11.0 to 1.11.2): the MCP tool `verify_security_findings` re-evaluates the diagnosis items (27 on the client, 33 on the server) in their current state and returns, per item, still present (stillPresent), fixed (resolved) or cannot confirm (inconclusive), with a language-independent reason. It is read-only. An item that could not be measured is never treated as safe: running as non-root, a stopped daemon, Docker not reachable, a missing command and a timeout all become inconclusive, and so do items such as `entropy_ransomware_guard` that were placeholders always returning pass. The MCP config audit (`audit_mcp_configs`, `roamswitch audit-mcp-configs` and a dedicated GTK screen) checks the MCP config files of AI tools for free, without any network access and without printing secret values (auto-approve, plain http, shell one-liner launch, a connection to HexStrike, writable configs, `enableAllProjectMcpServers`, launchers without a pinned version, container host privileges). The MCP skill `closing-out-roamswitch-findings` shows how to end each finding as confirmed, denied with a reason, or undetermined, and to lower the severity without deleting the finding even when there are mitigating circumstances. The active vulnerability check gained `hexstrike-noauth`, which finds an API that accepts arbitrary execution by AI without authentication (HexStrike AI) by sending a single `GET /api/processes/list` to a Python listener; it is not shown by the passive diagnosis. The process execution log gained two rules that find chains of attack tools, EXR-009 (one process looks up 8 or more kinds within 30 seconds) and EXR-010 (a script runtime starts 3 or more kinds within 2 minutes), notification only, and a person typing under sshd, login or a terminal is excluded. The server diagnosis gained "sensitive files exposed in the web document root" (34 items, 1.11.2): it works out the document root from the Apache, nginx and Caddy configuration, requests `.git`, `.env`, `.DS_Store`, phpinfo, SQL dumps, backups and private keys from 127.0.0.1 with `curl`, and reports "exposed" only when the response matches the start of the file on disk. An SPA that returns 200 for every path, or a path already answering 403, is not a false positive. File contents are never reported, only the host name and path, and symbolic links are not followed. With no web server it is "not applicable", and a check that could not be made costs no points. The result is reused for 5 minutes, and the request is recorded in the local access log.
- **Detection defaults and coverage** (1.11.0): the Bluetooth guard is now on by default (on an untrusted network it only turns "discoverable" off; it does not power the radio down or disconnect connected devices). Credential access monitoring (`credential_access_watch_enabled`) is also on by default (notification only: it tells you when an unexpected program opens SSH keys, `~/.aws`, gcloud, or the browser password and cookie stores; backup tools and indexers are excluded from the start, others can be added to `credential_access_watch_allow` in `config.json` and take effect in about 30 seconds). The default of the DNS threat guard was aligned across the daemon, the diagnosis, the screen and MCP (on by default, and only on untrusted networks; a plain Linux install goes from 88 to 92 in the diagnosis score because the treatment became the same). The Bluetooth guard and the DNS threat guard respect an explicit `false` in the settings. Listener detection was widened (the interception proxy Caido is not reported when it listens on loopback only; the MCP Inspector ports 6274 and 6277 are now known). Link guard also detects URLs that put a fake destination before an `@`, such as `https://apple.com@evil.example/`. The fake Anthropic, OpenAI and PyPI keys of the honeytokens now match the default gitleaks rules (they were only 23 characters, so a tool that harvests keys could discard them in its format check).
- **External command waits and wrong verdicts** (1.11.0): about 190 waits for external commands now have a limit, and a timeout is never turned into "not applicable", "clean" or "released". Fixed: a `docker info` timeout removed a working Docker guard (it is now treated as "not ready" and retried); when the release check (`nft list`) hung, `emergency-restore` and similar reported success and deleted the isolation record (the record is kept until the check succeeds); files that `clamscan` could not scan because of a timeout were counted as scanned (they are rescanned after 10 minutes and you are notified); and when listing packages or reading logs failed, the CVE scan result said "not applicable" (the result now says it is incomplete and why). Half-built interfaces left by a killed `wg-quick up` are cleaned up, the time limit now works even when a grandchild process holds standard output, and on a timeout the whole process group is stopped.
- **Diagnosis parsing and display fixes** (1.11.0): `sshd_config` is parsed by its real rules (`Include`, the first value wins, everything after `Match` is out of scope, `Match all`, sshd started by `ssh.socket`, and sshd on hosts without systemd). When the file integrity monitoring (FIM) baseline could not be read without root it was reported as "deleted"; it now shows that it cannot be confirmed. A stale `state.json` left after the daemon stopped was shown as running, and several passing states of ssh, Wi-Fi, browser and Secure Boot were merged into one passing sentence; both are fixed.
- **Container and Docker fixes** (1.11.1, 1.11.3): 1.11.1 fixed the container exec guard, which had stopped working on Docker 29. Docker 29 removed the deprecated `Status` from the `docker events` message, so the subscription failed at once and the guard kept reconnecting every 5 seconds. It now uses a format based on `Action` (the same value on older Docker), each line is parsed strictly, and when the subscription keeps failing the reconnect interval doubles from 5 seconds up to 5 minutes, with a warning for the same cause logged only once (the same interval handling was added to the dangerous-container and crash-loop detections). Also in 1.11.1, the decoy `~/.docker/config.json` reacted to RoamSwitch's own `docker` runs (the daemon's monitoring and `roamswitch status --server`) and wrongly logged 75 "honeytoken access" events in 6 minutes; every `docker` that RoamSwitch runs now starts with an empty private directory as `DOCKER_CONFIG` (`/run/roamswitch/docker-config` as root, mode 0700). The decoy itself is unchanged and still detects reads by other programs. 1.11.3 fixed a false positive in which `docker build` on a server caused a host-wide network isolation (Air-Gap). To Falco, each step of a Docker image build has a `container.id` that is a systemd unit name such as `system.slice`, and an empty image name. It used to be treated as a real container, so the many new executables that the build's `apt-get` runs became "Drop and execute new binary in container" (Critical) and, once the repeat count passed the limit (3), led to a host-wide Air-Gap (a public site going down was confirmed on a real machine). Now a detection whose `container.id` is a unit name and contains no container ID is only logged and notified: no process isolation, no automatic Air-Gap, and it is not counted for repeats. Detections for real containers (hex IDs) and for the host are unchanged.
- **A Falco detection could not stop the process it found** (1.11.25, Server Edition, `install.sh`): Falco's stock rules put the process name and command line in the output, but not the process ID (`proc.pid`). So when RoamSwitch received a Critical detection (for example `Fileless execution via memfd_create`), it could not tell which process to stop, ended with "Logged & Monitored (no PID in event)", and neither froze nor isolated anything. The Falco setting (`99-roamswitch-optimized.yaml`) now adds `proc.pid` to the JSON output fields with `append_output` (the message text is unchanged). In a real virtual machine (Ubuntu, Falco 0.45, `modern_ebpf`), a fileless execution with `memfd_create` ended as "Logged & Monitored" before the fix and as "Pinpoint Process Isolated + SIGKILL" after it. On existing machines the deb upgrade replaces this file; the rpm replaces it if you have not changed it, and if you have, a `.rpmnew` is left beside it, so add the `append_output` entry yourself. Falco needs a restart. Not covered: Falco's stock rules have no rule for a reverse shell on the host (only in containers), and this version does not add one. Mac is not affected (it does not use Falco).
- **Other fixes and corrections** (1.11.0 to 1.11.2): Falco's web server (`0.0.0.0:8765`) is disabled (1.11.2; the Falco package publishes `/healthz` over unencrypted HTTP on all interfaces by default, RoamSwitch receives events over a UNIX socket and does not use the port, so `webserver.enabled: false` was added to the deb and rpm configuration and to the configuration embedded in install.sh). The systemd journal warning `Got notification message from PID n, but reception only permitted for main PID`, logged about every 12 seconds, is gone (1.11.1; child processes started by the daemon inherited the notification socket `NOTIFY_SOCKET`, so the variable is removed and the shared code that starts external commands no longer passes it on). The port-scan block display showed blocks under one minute as `0m`; it now shows seconds, such as `BLOCKED 40s` (1.11.1; command and server TUI, 10 languages). On hosts without iptables the `DOCKER-USER chain protection could not be armed` warning is no longer printed (1.11.1; when there is really no protection, for example Docker on nftables without the RoamSwitch guard, it still warns). The `ping` used to pin ARP/NDP (it was sent to each DNS server in `resolv.conf` to create neighbour entries) is sent only when the destination is loopback, private, link-local or ULA, never to a public DNS server (1.11.1; with a threat-protection DNS configured, every pin used to send ICMP to the public server). Credential access monitoring may not report very short reads by programs that exit before RoamSwitch can identify the executable, such as `cat` or `head`; this is now written in the knowledge base (10 languages), the README and `ADVANCED_OPTIONS.md` (1.11.1; the detection method is unchanged). The ransomware detection description in the README and the app introduction now matches the behavior (on a write burst, the process is frozen with SIGSTOP and notified; Air-Gap and shared-service shutdown are not automatic, while a canary tamper still isolates as before; the diagnosis threshold now says 7.92 as implemented instead of 7.75; the setting `entropy_freeze_enabled`, which the daemon never read, was removed from the screen switch and the MCP settings view) (1.11.1). The manual's FIM baseline path is `/var/lib/roamswitch/fim.db` (it said `fim_baseline.db`), and the Server Edition deb and rpm now recommend `socat`, which Falco's output configuration uses to send to RoamSwitch's socket (1.11.1). The description of the update request (setup wizard, knowledge base, EULA) now matches the real header: GET only, no query string, no cookie, no per-device identifier, and the only header that names the software is the fixed `User-Agent: RoamSwitch-Updater`; what is sent did not change (1.11.1).
- **Packaging and documents** (1.11.0): a package upgrade no longer re-enables a service or timer that the operator stopped (they are enabled only at the first install; an upgrade only does `try-restart`; deb, rpm and Server Edition). The description of automatic sharing-service shutdown (`sharing_service_control_enabled`) now matches the implementation (it had been on by default since 2026-09-02, but the README and help (10 languages) still said opt-in, off by default). The AUR package enables the update timer and gained a hook that refreshes the FIM baseline after pacman, and the RPM includes the autostart entry (`/etc/xdg/autostart`). The Server Edition `server.conf.example` lists the guards that are on by default (honeytokens, harden_userns, container_exec_guard, port_scan, dns_tunnel, resource_guard_cpu) and a test checks that it matches the defaults. The Zero Telemetry display of the server TUI and the setup wizard no longer mention "ARP spoof protection", which the Server Edition does not have. The `nfq` tests build on Rust 1.99 too.
- **AccessScope bundled** (1.10.27): a separate tool that finds attack attempts in web server and SSH access logs and shows the evidence is now bundled. The Server Edition gets only the command (`accessscope`); the client edition gets the command and a window app (see AccessScope 0.1.0).
- **PathScope bundled** (1.10.23 to 1.10.25): a separate app that works out the paths by which an ordinary user could reach root and shows where to fix first (a privilege-escalation path analysis tool) is now bundled in the Client Edition deb, rpm and tarball (and the AUR package). It opens from the application menu as "PathScope"; the command is `pathscope`. It is licensed under the Apache License 2.0, and Section 4-3 was added to the EULA. It is updated together with the RoamSwitch package, and replaces a standalone `pathscope` package of the same name if one is installed. The Server Edition does not include it (see PathScope 0.1.4). 1.10.24 checks the versions of installed packages and of the running kernel against known vulnerabilities (0.1.5). In 1.10.25 the added explanation is for macOS, so the Linux screens hardly change (0.1.6).
- **False ransomware detection** (1.10.25): Copying many compressed or media files, or copying in a file manager, no longer gets the writer frozen as ransomware. A file whose first bytes are a genuine compressed or media format (gzip, zip and its relatives, JPEG, PNG, mp4/mov/heic, xz, zstd, 7z, lz4 and so on) is left out of the burst check. Encryption destroys those first bytes, so detection of real encryption is not weakened; documents such as docx and xlsx are still tracked. File managers (Nautilus, Nemo, Thunar, Dolphin, Caja, GVfs, KDE's KIO) are now on the allowlist, like `cp`: a GUI copy is written by the file manager itself, so it could be frozen before. `.heic`, `.avif`, `.mov`, `.webm`, `.m4a`, `.rar`, `.whl`, `.lz4` and others were added to the skipped extensions. Checked on a real Linux kernel, before and after: ciphertext without a header, and `cp` run under another name, are still detected after the fix.
- **Bundled PersonalSOC** (1.10.21, 1.10.22, 1.10.26): The white margin around the app icon is gone (see PersonalSOC 0.1.6). On Linux, the drop-down lists no longer have a white background that made their text unreadable in dark mode (0.1.7). In 1.10.26, the details of a CVE (known vulnerability) finding began to show the package name and version (0.1.8).
- **Autostart at login** (1.10.27): addressed the app not starting automatically at login on some desktop environments. Looking into a report from a real machine, the `NoDisplay=true` in the autostart `.desktop` file was suspected: some environments, such as lxqt-session, treat it as a reason to skip autostart. The file already lives where the application menu never lists it, so the unneeded setting is removed. Re-checking on a real machine is still to be done.
- **MCP `run_package_cve_scan_languages`** (1.10.27): when called with an empty folder, it now scans the registered project folders (`pkgcve_watched_folders`). This matches the Mac tool of the same name, so the watched folders can be used without passing a path.
- **Contact details and product-page URLs** (1.10.27): the contact details in the EULA and README changed from an address that cannot receive mail to the contact form (with an email as a fallback); the terms of the EULA themselves did not change. Product-page URLs in the app, the command line, the screens and the package notes now point to roamswitch.com.
- **Detections and features added** (1.10.0, 1.10.7, 1.10.11): DNS tunneling and exfiltration detection (only the host's own DNS queries; name resolution is never blocked). A forensic evidence bundle (with a SHA-256 manifest) when containment fires. Honeytokens. The age of the last proven vulnerability check (`roamswitch vuln-status`) and a mapping of diagnostic items to NIST CSF 2.0 and CIS Controls v8. Detection of LockBit-style partial encryption (16 of every 32 bytes, 1.10.7). Detection of the ransomware-recovery snapshots being deleted or thinned by anything other than RoamSwitch or the known managers snapper and timeshift (T1490, 1.10.11).
- **Honeytoken and evidence-bundle fixes** (1.10.4, 1.10.5, 1.10.8): so that real tools are not broken, the AWS decoy uses a separate profile name and the Docker decoy a non-existent registry name. Mode is 0600, and access by OpenSSH and by RoamSwitch itself is not flagged (1.10.8 also fixed a false positive from RoamSwitch's own process). The 1.10.0–1.10.4 packages did not include `roamswitch-honeytokens` and `roamswitch-incident-capture`, so decoys and evidence bundles never ran. 1.10.5 ships both in the client and server packages, and CI fails a build that lacks them.
- **Isolation (Air-Gap) and host defense** (Server Edition, 1.10.5): fixed host isolation cutting the management SSH it should keep, port 22 being opened even with `preserve_ssh_on_isolation=false`, and the operator's acknowledgement (`roamswitch server ack`) being ignored. Evidence is now collected before blocking on more paths, and the settings `honeytokens_enabled` and `harden_userns_enabled` were added.
- **Security fixes** (1.10.4–1.10.7): state-changing IPC can be called only by root or a user with a local login session. The root daemon no longer reads `~/.config/roamswitch/config.json` without checking ownership or follows symlinks when writing. The daemon runs inside a systemd sandbox. `rustls` was updated to 0.23.45 (RUSTSEC-2026-0285). Fixed: treating a gateway MAC change alone as "moved to another network", isolation release trusting the current gateway unconditionally, and the ransomware burst detector excluding processes that carry an allow-listed name. Link-guard packet parsing runs in a sandboxed child process outside root.
- **Malware detection** (1.10.0–1.10.6): fixed a fallback hiding `clamdscan --fdpass` failures, a USB scan treating exit code 2 (failed to run) as malware and running `umount -f`, and the daily update not being able to update ClamAV signatures. An EICAR self-test of the detection path was added. fanotify now uses permission events only for execution.
- **Network, DNS, Docker, link guard** (1.10.1–1.10.4): DNS enforcement no longer flushes the DNS cache every 3 seconds (it runs only when the state changes). The Docker protection self-heals every 60 seconds. The link guard's packet wait and queue length were revised, and it reassembles the ClientHello from TCP sequence numbers. An option to block UDP/443 (QUIC) was added (`linkGuard.blockQuic`, off by default). Fixed: a `clamav.net` false positive in DNS tunneling detection, the Sensor control port (50543) being blocked permanently on a co-located host (Server Edition), and an endless repeat of notifications for programs that bind a different UDP port on every restart.
- **Notifications, diagnostics, UI** (1.10.3, 1.10.4, 1.10.9, 1.10.11): identical notifications are only recorded in the history for 10 minutes. On Arch-based systems the "automatic security updates" check is "not applicable". Fixed: port blocking reporting success even when nftables was not updated, Wi-Fi safety always being "unknown" on iwd systems (RoamSwitch OS), guidance for Validity fingerprint sensors, and tabs cut off at 1280 px. The tray language switcher gained Italian and Portuguese, and the ransomware-recovery screen now describes its real scope (the logged-in user's home only, 1.10.11).
- **Recovery, uninstall, upgrade** (1.10.7, 1.10.10): a dedicated tab in the main window, and `roamswitch emergency-restore` plus a tray menu entry undo isolation, firewall, gateway pinning, DNS and connection tracking. `roamswitch uninstall` does this first and then removes the decoys and files it added. The license signing key was regenerated (both old and new public keys are accepted). Three GUI upgrade problems were fixed: stopping on one unreachable mirror, reporting success when nothing changed, and not restarting the app. The restore path is now a real file picker (1.10.10).
- **PersonalSOC bundled** (1.10.14): a separate app that gathers this machine's logs and defense status read-only into a report is now bundled in the packages (deb, rpm, tarball, AUR). 10 languages, Apache-2.0. Its LLM integration is optional and off by default. The Server edition does not include it.
- **Packages, tests, other** (1.10.5–1.10.10): apt and rpm kept the latest three versions of each package, but since 1.10.27 they keep only the newest one. Three versions used to be kept, so `apt install roamswitch=<version>` or `dnf downgrade` could roll back; to save storage that is no longer possible. If a release turns out to be defective, a fixed version will be published instead. The pentest suite was re-run (Server 19/19, Client 11/11). External commands called by the resident daemon now have a time limit (a `bluetoothctl` that did not answer for 1 hour 41 minutes on a system without bluetoothd stalled IPC). RoamSwitch OS hides the uninstall entry points. The Server investigation-agent report now includes the host name and IP address.
- **Activity log and false-positive audit** (1.10.13): a Security Activity Log tab to search and filter past detections (ARP, FIM, port anomalies, recorded process execution, notification history and more). An alert-fatigue audit fixed 44 findings in the desktop edition and 7 in the server edition: lockfile monitoring's "unreadable" verdict is no longer treated as tampering, notifications for link protection, isolation-release checks, gateway-MAC changes and exec detection now carry more to judge them by (the triggering signal, old and new MAC, the download source), and the server edition's Docker notification is deduplicated.
- **Resume from suspend, diagnostics, feed** (1.10.12): fixed a false "protection is not responding" alert right after resuming from suspend (a gap far beyond the monitor loop's interval is treated as a resume, and every guard's timestamp is reset). Also fixed the order and explanation of the active-verification status, a UDP-listener notification record that grew without limit, and the popular-npm-packages feed's manifest URL not matching the published file name, which showed a false SHA-256 mismatch warning.
- **Decoys and notification tuning** (1.10.15): the honeytokens now include `~/.pypirc` and `~/.env.backup`. Log-audit notifications are limited to security-relevant anomalies such as authentication failures and malware detections. For about 25 minutes after a Sensor audit is requested, the warning is worded calmly (Client edition).
- **Security fixes** (1.10.16 to 1.10.19): when the daemon placed decoys (canary files, honeytokens) in a user's folder it followed symbolic links, which could lead to privilege escalation; it now creates them without following links. `get_canary_status` returns only the caller's own entries to non-admins, `kill_process` refuses processes the caller does not own, an IPC line is capped at 1 MiB and non-root users at 16 concurrent connections. Event-forwarding file output is limited to locations only root can write. Values passed to DNS settings and rfkill are validated, ClamAV and YARA scans validate the path and put `--` before it. Webhook `curl` is limited to http and https, and a new option (`webhook_block_private_addresses`, off by default, with DNS-rebinding protection) refuses internal addresses. The quarantine store moved to a root-only `/var/lib/roamswitch/quarantine` (migrated automatically from the old location at start-up), and restore and delete verify the store path and ownership.
- **Blocking file execution (fanotify)** (1.10.18): it was not effective when run as a systemd service because executions never reached the daemon. It now watches per filesystem, and we confirmed under the real service that a detected suspicious script is refused. Scan exclusions can no longer be bypassed by spoofing a process name or matching part of a path.
- **Data protection and integrity** (1.10.16 to 1.10.19): firewall profile switches are atomic in one batch (also for Air-Gap), so rules can no longer disappear midway or on failure. Concurrent updates to the pending approvals record (`approvals.json`) no longer lose data. When the trusted-Sensor file cannot be read, other pairings are no longer erased by overwriting with an empty list; a copy is kept (`<file>.corrupt-<time>`, 0600, latest 3) and the operation fails with a clear error. The audit-results file got the same protection.
- **Secret masking in the execution record** (1.10.19, same rules as Mac): command lines in the execution record hide `mysql -ppassword`, `sshpass`, `docker login -p`, sensitive flags, URL credentials, bearer and basic headers, known API key and token formats, and cryptocurrency private keys and seed phrases (only when the checksum matches). Wrappers such as `sudo` and `env`, interpreters such as `sh` and `python`, and the contents of `sh -c '…'` are seen through. It covers MCP, the CLI, the TUI and incident evidence, and search runs on the masked text.
- **Working with Sensor** (1.10.16 to 1.10.19): pairing sends a proof of key possession (PoP). Control-API signature v3 (bound to Sensor's public key) is sent and never downgraded to v2 once it has worked. The reason "Sensor's own storage file is corrupt, so pairing is impossible" returned by Sensor 0.3.15 is shown in 10 languages. The GUI no longer says "removed" when removing a Sensor failed. The calmer warning during a Sensor audit returns to the normal warning as soon as the report arrives.
- **Ransomware detection and notifications** (1.10.18): a stale verdict left over when a process ID (PID) was reused could freeze a legitimate `cp`; the process start time is now checked as well. A process released with `roamswitch frozen resume` is not frozen again for 600 seconds. The notification for a failed isolation shows the reason in 10 languages when it was refused for lack of a login session. `roamswitch forward status` puts a translated heading before the reason a forwarding target was refused.
- **OS Hardening** (1.10.18): fixed the "do not use Secure Boot" checkbox missing when re-enrolling TPM2, and the contradictory fingerprint (Validity 138a:0090) guidance.
- **Bundled PersonalSOC** (1.10.16 to 1.10.20): 0.1.2 to 0.1.5 in turn (see each PersonalSOC release). In 1.10.20, Settings is split into tabs by function and the message after a report is created is shorter (0.1.5).

- **Network block fix** (1.11.4): the egress block of malicious IPs (`roamswitch_egress_block`) and the block of port-scan sources (`roamswitch_portscan`) only took effect for five packets per minute. Each rule used to be written as one rule, "log, rate-limit to 5 per minute, drop". In nftables, `limit rate` stops matching once the rate is exceeded, so only the first five packets per minute were dropped and the rest went through (measured: 25 of 30 packets passed). A retrying connection got through after a few seconds, so the block was only a delay. Each target now has two rules: a rate-limited log rule and an unconditional drop.

- **Pointer to the first-hour page after an isolation** (1.11.5): the site has a new page, "First Hour After an Incident" (<https://roamswitch.com/en/incident-first-hour.html>, in 10 languages), and the app points to it. The client edition shows a link in the dialog that releases Air-Gap. The Server Edition adds one line with the page address to the end of the notifications sent while the host is still isolated (when it enters degraded mode, and when you acknowledge with ack), and to the output of `roamswitch server ebpf`. The link opens only when you click it; RoamSwitch never connects to this page by itself.

- **Bundled apps** (1.11.6, 1.11.8, 1.11.9): 1.11.6 bundled PersonalSOC 0.1.10, 1.11.8 AccessScope 0.3.2 and PersonalSOC 0.1.11, and 1.11.9 PersonalSOC 0.1.12. PersonalSOC 0.1.10 lets you consent to automatic log collection from the command line alone with `personalsoc consent`, even without the app window (including the Server Edition); 1.11.6 has no other change. AccessScope 0.3.2 tidied its terminal output, PersonalSOC 0.1.11 removed the "consent" to reading logs automatically, and 0.1.12 added an example with `sudo` to the message for a log that could not be read (see each app's entry).
- **Recovering files from ransomware (snapshots)** (1.11.7, client edition): the client package now includes `roamswitch-ransomware-rollback` and the resident service `roamswitch-ransomware-rollback-watch.service` (not in the Server Edition). It takes snapshots of btrfs subvolumes and LVM logical volumes and copies only the files you need to a separate place, without overwriting the current files. By default it takes a pre-damage snapshot every 6 hours regardless of any damage (4 generations on btrfs, 2 on LVM), and also takes one when a burst of writes or canary tampering is detected (after a detection-time snapshot, periodic snapshots pause for 7 days so that already-encrypted data cannot push out the last clean generation). The full rollback, `restore`, refuses a detection-time snapshot, which contains encrypted files, without `--accept-tampered`. The screen existed only in the Incident Response tab of the RoamSwitch OS image (the standard edition got a "Ransomware Recovery" tab in 1.11.21). The service is enabled on first install, restarted on upgrade and stopped on removal. It is documented in section 24 of the manual and on the CLI page.
- **Ransomware detection by write entropy now also cuts the network** (1.11.7, client edition): until now it only froze the offending process with `SIGSTOP` and notified you. The isolation policy already defined a `RansomwareEntropyBurst` trigger, but nothing called it, and in 1.11.1 the README, intro text and knowledge base were rewritten to say "does not cut the network" to match the implementation. It now engages the Air-Gap after the freeze. It is treated like canary tampering, a high-confidence trigger: a timer alone never releases it silently; it lifts only once the cause is verified gone, or when you release it from the tray menu or with `roamswitch emergency-restore`. The notification now says the network was cut as well (10 languages). On a real kernel (Ubuntu, fanotify) I checked the whole chain from a 40-file write: the freeze, the isolation record, the outbound block and the evidence bundle. The texts describing this detection (app intro (GTK), knowledge base, diagnostic item) now say both the freeze and the network cut, and the description of the diagnostic item "entropy-based ransomware detection", which still said a 7.75 threshold in French, Spanish, Italian and Portuguese only, now says the implemented 7.92.
- **Suspicious autostart detection** (1.11.7, 1.11.9; client edition): 1.11.7 added a check of autostart entries that were newly created or rewritten (`persistence_watch_enabled`, on by default, notify-only; it corresponds to the Mac app's `PersistenceMonitorGuard`). It covers `/etc/systemd/system`, cron (`/etc/cron.d`, `/etc/cron.{hourly,daily,weekly,monthly}`, `/etc/crontab`, `/var/spool/cron`), `/etc/xdg/autostart`, and each user's `~/.config/systemd/user` and `~/.config/autostart`. It notifies only about entries that look like how malware persists: a reverse shell, decoding data and running it directly, downloading and running it directly, or running a program from a temporary or hidden folder; ordinary-looking entries are only logged. The switch and the notification text are in 10 languages. On a real kernel, a suspicious systemd unit was detected within 10 seconds of being placed. 1.11.9 fixed the app's own autostart entry being reported as suspicious: the link that `systemctl --user enable` creates (`~/.config/systemd/user/default.target.wants/roamswitch-app.service`) was judged as "runs a program from a temporary or hidden folder" only because its target is inside a hidden folder (`.config`) (in a real virtual machine, installing and starting the app made it report its own service). Now a link that points into one of the places where systemd keeps units (`/etc/systemd`, `/usr/lib/systemd`, `/lib/systemd`, a user's `~/.config/systemd/user`) is judged by reading the unit and its `ExecStart`, and links to anywhere else are judged by the path they point to, as before.
- **An "admin-only" mode for shared machines, and permissions in RoamSwitch OS** (1.11.8): added an "admin-only" mode (off by default). Until now, anyone with a local login session could, even as an ordinary user, do things that affect the whole machine, such as changing the security level or engaging the Air-Gap, so on a machine used by several people another user could loosen the firewall. Only when the operator creates `/etc/roamswitch/admin-only` are changes to the firewall, DNS, VPN, USB, kernel and mount hardening, radios, Sensor trust and the like limited to root or users in the sudo, wheel or roamswitch group. The file counts only when it is owned by root and not writable by others (so a user cannot create it and switch the mode themselves). Actions tied to your own files and processes (isolation, kill, freeze, scans) work as before. The refusal messages are in 10 languages (the CLI in Japanese and English). It is off by default because a setup where an ordinary, non-admin user runs the app on their own PC would stop working. Also, the mutating methods of RoamSwitch OS are now limited to root or members of wheel. Among the `os_hardening_*` methods, destroying files (shred, rollback restore), blocking the network (lockdown), rewriting where logs are sent (SIEM output) and switching Tor or DNS could be run as root with only a `confirmed` flag written by the request itself and a local login session. The wheel check that SecureBoot enrollment and TPM2 LUKS setup already did now applies to these too (reading state and dry runs work as before).
