The app edition (apt, rpm, AUR) does not enable `os_hardening_integration`, so it is not affected.
- **ARP spoofing confirmation screen** (1.11.10): fixed the screen sometimes showing "attacker" and "legitimate router" the wrong way round when the gateway's entry briefly went missing. When the gateway's entry disappears from the kernel's ARP table (the "pin the gateway" setting was turned off, an interface reset, expiry), the reference MAC address RoamSwitch remembered disappeared with it. If a forged reply arrived in that gap, the forged address was learned as the reference, and when the real router appeared later, the real one was reported as the sudden change (in a real virtual machine, the isolation confirmation screen showed the two the wrong way round). Now the earlier reference is kept while the gateway is missing from the table, and a fresh reference is made only when a move to another network is confirmed. The labels "Attacker MAC" and "Legitimate router MAC" on the screen are now "MAC now answering (suspected spoof)" and "Earlier gateway MAC (the reference)", because the reference is not always known to be right (10 languages).
- **Build and wording** (1.11.8): the Japanese text in the app now uses one name for the guide page, "インシデント発生後の最初の1時間". The build (CI) was hardened: third-party Actions are pinned to commit SHAs instead of tags that can move, and write permission (`contents: write`) is given only to the job that publishes the release. There is no change you can see.
- **Fixes to URL checks and vulnerability scanning** (1.11.12): `audit-url` (and the URL check in the window) showed short URLs such as `bit.ly` as fine. The check does not use the network, so it cannot see where they lead; it now warns "this is a short URL (the destination is unknown)" (penalty 25). Domains such as `paypa1-secure-login`, where brand-name letters were replaced with digits or look-alikes and words like "secure" and "login" were added, were also shown as safe; they are now judged a serious danger (penalty 45). Official domains and names without a brand stay safe. `scan-packages` compared Ubuntu packages (a version containing `ubuntu`) with Debian fixed-version numbers and could show an already fixed `curl` as CVE-2024-2398; Ubuntu versions are now not compared and the number left out is shown (check Ubuntu's own notices separately). Part of the window stayed in English after a language change (tab names, window subtitles, headings of the dashboard, ports and decoy screens; 24 items in Traditional Chinese, Korean, German, French, Spanish, Italian and Portuguese and 2 in Simplified Chinese); these were translated (based on machine translation).
- **The notification history file itself was detected as a "reverse shell"** (1.11.11): the notification history, incident timeline, evidence bundles, execution logs and the notification queue quote what was detected (command strings and paths). A quote is not a program, but inspected as a file it matches the very signature it quotes; for example `notification_history.json` was reported as "suspected malware (WebShell-ReverseShell)". Only the data files inside `/var/lib/roamswitch` and `/run/roamswitch` are now left out of inspection (executables there are still inspected). Reproduced on the published version and gone in the fixed one in a real virtual machine. Mac is not affected.
- **Air-Gap confirmation screen for ransomware detection** (1.11.13): when ransomware encryption was detected and the network was cut, the Air-Gap confirmation screen did not open. Detection by write randomness (entropy) has frozen the process and cut the network with Air-Gap since 1.11.7, but the app opens the confirmation screen only for notifications of the kind that open it; this detection sent an ordinary notification, so the network was cut without the screen. Now, when it cuts the network, it opens a confirmation screen with the reason (ransomware), the process, the number of rewritten files, whether the process was frozen and the command to release it. It opens even when the process cannot be identified, because the network is cut anyway. The Air-Gap section of the diagnosis screen shows the same reason. The headings are in 10 languages (machine translation other than Japanese and English; not reviewed by native speakers). Checked in a real virtual machine (Ubuntu, btrfs home): the published version did not show it and the fixed one opens it at the moment of detection. Mac is not affected.
- **The Air-Gap title staying in the app** (1.11.14): after the Air-Gap was released, the app's title ("Air-Gap emergency full isolation") and the isolation notice in the diagnosis screen sometimes stayed. When the app opens the Air-Gap confirmation screen (it opens for canary (decoy) tampering, ARP spoofing, and, since 1.11.13, ransomware detection), it sets an internal flag. That flag was cleared only by the app's own release action. So when the daemon released the isolation (the "emergency network recovery" screen, `roamswitch emergency-restore`, or the automatic release by the timer), the "isolated" display stayed until the app was restarted, even though the network was back. Now the flag is cleared when the daemon's marker (`/run/roamswitch/airgap.active`) is still missing 15 seconds after it was set (right after it is set the marker may not exist yet, so the flag is trusted for that time). With simulated ransomware in a real virtual machine (Ubuntu, btrfs home), releasing with "emergency network recovery" now brings the title back; the published 1.11.13 did not, with the same steps. Mac is not affected by this: it refreshes the isolation display by polling the privileged helper for its state.
- **Ransomware rewrite classifier (advisory score) and bundled AccessScope 0.3.3** (1.11.15, client edition classifier): an advisory score from a rewrite classifier in ransomware detection. When a burst happens (20 or more files rewritten in 30 seconds), a logistic regression computes a "ransomware-likeness" from 0 to 1 over the content of up to 20 recent files (4 KiB each from the head, the middle and the tail) and prints it next to the burst notification. Nothing is sent over the network. **In this version it is advisory only and the response does not change**: snapshots, freezing and Air-Gap behave as before. On simulated scenarios that we wrote, it added nothing to the existing entropy rule on kinds of activity it had not been trained on (no benign window was lowered, and no attack window was wrongly called benign). If the model cannot be loaded, too few files are informative, or the score is invalid, the previous behavior stays. A burst that touches a canary or honeytoken is never lowered, whatever the score. The watcher now also counts file deletions and rename-from events (never as rewrites; burst detection is unchanged). The bundled AccessScope is now 0.3.3 (it gained detection of clients that behave very differently from the other visitors). Mac was not covered at this version (Mac 1.12.0 ports it, as an advisory score only): Mac ransomware detection uses FSEvents and did not include this classifier at the time.
- **Bundled AccessScope 0.3.4, and a change in the source of the link guard's seed data**(1.11.16): The bundled AccessScope is now 0.3.4 (the command line can run LLM analysis as root too, and the terminal output is now Markdown tables; see the AccessScope 0.3.4 entry). If you run an LLM as root, `agy` and `codex` run with root's rights. Changed: the source of the link guard's bundled seed data. The initial data shipped in the package used to be host names (about 280) from OpenPhish's free feed. To match OpenPhish's terms of use (commercial use and passing the data to third parties need its written consent), OpenPhish is no longer used; the seed is now 2,000 randomly chosen currently active phishing domains from Phishing.Database (MIT licence). The seed only bridges the time until the first update (within 24 hours). The full threat feed received with the daily update never contained OpenPhish, so it is unchanged.
- **Server Edition diagnosis (first FIM baseline, telnet wording) and the rustls update**(1.11.18): Fixed: installing Server Edition in the same `apt` transaction as Apache (or similar) could make the next diagnosis show a "critical path tamper monitor (FIM)" warning (changes detected). The daemon builds the FIM baseline (file fingerprints) when it starts. When it started in the middle of an `apt` transaction, files dpkg was working on (such as `/etc/cron.daily/apache2.dpkg-new`) went into the baseline, and the check right after the transaction showed them as "deleted" and "new". dpkg and apt use `fcntl` record locks, not `flock`. The two are independent locks, and the old "is the package manager working?" test, which looked only at `flock`, always answered "not working", even in the middle of `apt install` (measured on a real machine: `flock` showed a transaction in 0 of 199 probes, `fcntl` `F_GETLK` in 182). It now checks the `fcntl` lock too, and when the first baseline is built during an `apt`/`dpkg` transaction, it waits up to 120 seconds for it to finish (if it does not finish, a warning is logged and the baseline is built anyway). `roamswitch-server.service` is `Type=simple`, so waiting does not hold up the `apt` transaction. The "postpone FIM verification during an upgrade" logic uses the same test, and it now works as well. In a real virtual machine (Ubuntu 24.04), installing Apache and this version's deb in one `apt` transaction gave 91/100 with no FIM warning, and the journal recorded "wait, then build the baseline". The published 1.11.16 gave 88/100 and "2 changes detected" with the same steps. Fixed: the wording of the Server Edition diagnosis "Legacy cleartext services (Telnet/RSH) absent". Merely having the `telnet` command (a client) installed was reported as "legacy service detected", which read as if a service accepting connections was running. It now separates services that accept connections (`telnetd`, `rshd`, `rlogind`, `tftpd`, `ypbind`) from clients (`telnet`, `rsh`, `rlogin`, `tftp`), and shows the paths found (for example "cleartext client found (not a service that accepts connections): /usr/bin/telnet"). The recommendation now says how to find the package (`dpkg -S <path>`, `rpm -qf <path>`) and that on Ubuntu the real `telnet` package is `inetutils-telnet` (10 languages). What counts as a finding is unchanged, so a client alone still lowers the score (CIS 4.8: keep legacy programs that send credentials in cleartext off the host). Updated: the rustls dependency is now 0.23.46 (used by `roamswitch-updater` to fetch the signed data). 0.23.45 has four advisories published on 2026-10-14 (RUSTSEC-2026-0346 to 0349: data sent to the outer server after an ECH rejection, memory allocated for Brotli certificate decompression, a key-exchange downgrade through an unauthenticated ServerHello, and TLS secrets exposed in `Debug` output). Severity is low to medium, and RoamSwitch only makes anonymous GET requests to the fixed host `lafine.net`.
- **Server Edition diagnosis wording (how to find the telnet package)**(1.11.19): Fixed: the recommendation of the Server Edition diagnosis "Legacy cleartext services (Telnet/RSH) absent" gave a way to find the package that does not work on Ubuntu. It told you to find the package with `dpkg -S <path>`, but on Ubuntu `/usr/bin/telnet` is an alternatives link, and `dpkg -S /usr/bin/telnet` fails with "no path found" (the real file belongs to `inetutils-telnet`). It now says `dpkg -S $(readlink -f <path>)` (10 languages). What counts as a finding and the score are unchanged. In a real virtual machine (Ubuntu 24.04) with the published 1.11.18, I confirmed that `dpkg -S /usr/bin/telnet` fails.
- **Ransomware Recovery screen and the QR code on release** (1.11.20 to 1.11.21): in 1.11.20 the release confirmation window began to show a QR code for the first-hour guide page (while isolated, the machine cannot open the guide; a phone on its own connection can. The code holds only the public, fixed address in the window's language: no host name, no query string. Nothing is sent over the network and no image library is used. In a real virtual machine I read the address back from an image of the window, and a test makes the QR code for the address in each of the 10 languages and reads it back. Mac got the same display (Mac 1.11.10)). In 1.11.21 the standard edition of the Linux desktop app gained a "Ransomware Recovery" screen (tab). The screen for taking files out of a snapshot used to exist only in the RoamSwitch OS image; in the standard edition it was available only as a command (`roamswitch-ransomware-rollback`). The 1.11.7 changelog and chapter 24 of the operation manual described the screen as if the standard edition had it, but it did not (a correction). In "Ransomware Recovery" in the sidebar you see the snapshot list (★ recommended; a detection-time snapshot carries a warning that it contains encrypted files), select one, and choose "Restore Files" or "Restore a Folder"; files are extracted to `<target>/RoamSwitch-Recovered/<ID>/`, never over the current files. Rolling everything back with `restore` is not on the screen and is still a command. The daemon got a new path for this screen (`ransomware_rollback_run`) that lets through only the list, reading the settings, opening the snapshot for browsing and closing it, and extracting files; changing the pre-damage snapshot interval, taking snapshots and a whole-volume `restore` are not let through (so a process running as the user cannot stop the snapshots), and the path to extract has to belong to the caller. The screen's strings moved into the standard edition's string table (they used to be only in the OS image's table, so the string keys showed as they were; 10 languages). In a real virtual machine (Ubuntu, btrfs home), from 20 files encrypted by simulated ransomware, I picked the pre-damage snapshot and extracted a file and a folder (40 files) and confirmed the contents were original, the owner was the user and the current files were untouched; I also confirmed the daemon refuses forbidden operations and another user's paths. The same screen in the OS image lacked "open the snapshot for browsing" in the daemon's allowlist, so extraction failed at its first step (not fixed or checked as of 1.11.21). Mac already has "Ransomware Recovery…" (Pro) in its menu.
- **Web application layer rules (Web Rules), MCP config audit, new commands, recovery screen notice** (1.11.22 to 1.11.23; 1.11.22 was never published): detection rules for PHP, JavaScript, JSP and ASP web shells and injected malicious code were added to both the Client Edition and the Server Edition (an `eval` plus Base64 chain, one-line web shells that pass request values straight to execution, executable PHP placed in an upload directory, PHP disguised as an image, `.htaccess` abuse, injected scripts, in-browser mining libraries; 18 rules built only from publicly documented techniques, with no signatures taken from real malware samples). The rules come in three layers: bundled with the product, a signed daily update (receive-only), and your own rules in `/etc/roamswitch/rules.d/*.json`. The Server Edition scans the document roots it finds in the web server's configuration right after a file is written (the server edition had no path that watched writes before, only the periodic scan) and also scans every 6 hours. Automatic quarantine is by default only for `critical` (`quarantine` in `web_rules.conf`); `high` only notifies and calls the hook, and `medium` only reports. The commands `roamswitch web-scan` and `web-rules`, the after-detection hook (`/etc/roamswitch/hooks/post-detection`) and an account action that is off by default (`account_action=revoke_shell`) were also added. On the genuine code of WordPress, phpMyAdmin and npm dependencies there were 0 detections. Limits: only the first 256 KB (on write or execute) and 4 MB (scan) are read, and splitting such as `'ev'.'al'` is not followed. Real-machine checks found and fixed a defect where `web-scan --quarantine` did not run the after-detection hook. The MCP config audit gained an environment variable item (`code_loading_env`; it flags settings such as `LD_PRELOAD` and `BASH_ENV` that load extra code at start, and never shows the value). The command `roamswitch kernel-modules` checks, by reading files only, whether the modules that were the way in for Copy Fail, Dirty Frag and `act_pedit` can be loaded, writes `install ... /bin/false` to `modprobe.d` for unused ones with `--apply`, and undoes it with `--remove` (not part of the diagnosis score). `bluetooth-audit` checks the BlueZ configuration for entries that let unpaired devices in. The ransomware recovery screen used to say "no snapshots yet" on an ext4 home where snapshots cannot be taken; it now says "Snapshots cannot be taken on this home's filesystem (ext4). ..." (`list --json` now includes each home's filesystem). The recovery screen of the RoamSwitch OS image failed at its first step because the daemon's allowlist lacked `mount-for-browsing` and `unmount-browse`; they were added. The bundled PathScope is now 0.1.8 (high-EPSS CVEs added and kept once added). Mac is not affected (its same features are in Mac 1.11.11).
- (1.11.24) **New: the Server Edition TUI (`roamswitch-server-tui`) has `kernel-modules` and `bluetooth-audit` in its Tools screen.** "Risky kernel module check" and "Bluetooth keyboard-injection check" run with Enter (read-only). On the first row, `a` (stop the unused risky modules from loading) and `u` (undo the block) are also available; both need root and go through a confirmation screen. The wording and logic are now shared with the CLI (the CLI's output is unchanged apart from losing its colours).
- (1.11.24) **New: the man page now documents `kernel-modules`, `bluetooth-audit` and the new MCP config audit finding for environment variables.**
- (1.11.24) The new texts are in 10 languages (based on machine translation, not reviewed by native speakers).
- (1.11.24) Not checked: operating the TUI for real (the `a` key through the confirmation screen to the action). Only the tests and an end-to-end run of the CLI in Docker were done.
- (1.11.26) **Fixed: the ClickFix clipboard detection now starts the moment you copy (Linux desktop app).** It used to check only once a second, so after you copied a malicious command it took up to about a second for the clipboard to be emptied, and it could be pasted in the meantime. The app now checks the moment the clipboard's owner changes (`owner-change`); the once-a-second check stays as a fallback for setups where that signal is not delivered. In a real virtual machine (Ubuntu, X11) I measured the time from copy to empty 12 times: 0.06 to 0.91 s on the published 1.11.25, 0.03 to 0.04 s on this version. I have not checked Wayland (the once-a-second check works there).
- (1.11.26) **Fixed: the detection reason in the ClickFix notification is now in the screen's language.** A Japanese notification carried an English reason (`base64-decoded content piped straight into a shell ...`). The reason is now returned as a kind, not a sentence, and shown from the 10-language strings (based on machine translation; not checked by native speakers).
- (1.11.26) Mac gets the same review (Mac 1.11.12).
- (1.11.27) **Added: local AI service diagnosis, AI agent setup audit, an SSRF check in the active scan, a notice for connections to cloud metadata addresses (Server Edition), and blocking/matching of the 3 attack-server IPs published by JPCERT/CC.** The AI service diagnosis (`ollama-exposed`, `jupyter-noauth`, `mlflow-noauth`, `gradio-noauth`) sends one read-only GET to the listener on 127.0.0.1 and reports only when it is reachable without authentication. The AI agent setup audit (`roamswitch audit-agent-setup`, MCP `audit_agent_setup`) checks instruction files, skills and OpenClaw settings read-only, without any network access. The metadata notice (`metadata_watch_enabled`) records and notifies when a non-root program or container connects to `169.254.169.254` and the like (it does not block).
- (1.11.27) **Improved: CVE matching results now name the project (repository) lockfile they came from.** It is in the command output, the CSV, and the JSON of MCP and the terminal UI, and results are grouped by project.
- (1.11.27) **Fixed: the daily update of the link guard's threat feed had been stopped since October 13.** The step that builds the bundled seed data piped the output of `sort -R` into `head` under `pipefail`, so the upstream failed with SIGPIPE. The shuffled result is now written to a file before it is read.
- (1.11.27) Mac has the AI service diagnosis, the AI agent setup audit, the SSRF check, attack-server blocking and the CVE project display too (Mac 1.11.13).
- (1.11.28) **Fixed: false positives in the OS package CVE matching on Ubuntu.** An Ubuntu host was also matched with Debian's data, so it was compared against Debian's fixed version numbers (`3.18.2-1`, `3.2.7~ds-1+deb13u1` and so on), and CVEs that do not apply to Ubuntu or are not yet assessed showed "fix available" (for example fuse3 and node-* packages). It now uses a map made for Ubuntu.
  - The map holds, per release (20.04, 22.04, 24.04, 26.04), the CVEs with a CVSS v3 of 7.0 or more for which Ubuntu has published a fix. A CVE that only Ubuntu Pro fixes is marked "Pro required". The kernel is handled by the kernel CVE map, as before.
  - Matching uses the source package name and source version (because Ubuntu's data is by source package; the binary name is shown too).
  - "Not applicable" does not mean "no known CVE". A CVE whose fix is not yet published is not included (the CLI and the screen carry a note).
  - In a check that assumed a real Ubuntu 24.04 package set, the old method's 1,033 false positives became 0 for those that do not need Pro. On a set that was not updated, 1,341 real fixes are detected.
  - An unsupported Ubuntu (25.04 and so on) and derivative distributions (Linux Mint, Pop!_OS and so on) are shown as "unsupported". They are not switched to Debian's map. **On Linux Mint and Pop!_OS, findings that used to appear from Debian's map no longer appear.** Debian, Arch, RHEL-family and SUSE behave as before.
  - The map is signed and delivered only to Ubuntu hosts (about 4.4 MB). The output (MCP `run_package_cve_scan`) gained `ecosystem` and `release` (existing fields are unchanged).
- (1.11.28) **Changed: the Server Edition also receives the Ubuntu package CVE map.** It follows the server's outbound-connection switch (`cve_kernel_map_updates_enabled`); if you turn the switch off, nothing is fetched (then the OS package CVE matching on an Ubuntu server shows "data not fetched"). Maps for other OS packages are not fetched by the server edition. The outside connections the Server Edition makes by default are the anonymous receiving of signed data (kernel CVE data, web rules, and on an Ubuntu server the Ubuntu package CVE data). The explanation of item 8 of `roamswitch server setup` was changed to match.
- (1.11.28) **Changed: the bundled AccessScope is now 0.3.6 and PersonalSOC 0.1.13** (including the fixes listed above).
- (1.11.28) **Known limit: the Debian package CVE map does not tell Debian releases apart.** On Debian 12 and 13 hosts, fixed version numbers that exist only in Debian 14 can appear (on Ubuntu this is resolved by the new map).
- **SSH brute force blocking** (1.12.0, Server Edition and the Linux client edition): until then SSH failures were only summed by the log audit (every 30 minutes, the latest 500 lines) and reported, and the "RoamSwitch's own SSH rate limit" in the specification was, in the implementation, only a check that fail2ban or CrowdSec is running. Now the sshd log is followed in real time from journald (auth.log / secure is only the fallback without systemd, because the file method can be abused to block a third party where the log can be forged), and a source that reaches 6 failures in 600 seconds (default) is dropped for new connections to the SSH port with nftables (`inet roamswitch_sshguard`): 1 hour first, 4 times as long for a repeat offender, 7 days at most; `Failed publickey` is not counted. The IP comes from the `from <IP> port <N>` at the end of the line, and journald's `_UID` and `_COMM` exclude anything written by something other than sshd. Exempt: the gateway, DNS, the machine itself, loopback, `whitelist_ips`, `ssh_guard_allow`, sources of authenticated sessions, sources that logged in with a public key in the past 30 days, and paired Sensors (**private IPs are not exempt**). Existing SSH connections of a banned source are cut too (checked once more just before cutting; failure lines that got through after a ban fell from 16-24 to 0-4). On by default; it yields blocking to fail2ban / CrowdSec when an sshd jail is enabled. Notifications only for a ban and for a login that succeeds after failures (Critical, the session is not cut), at most 10 an hour. Bans are restored after a reboot with their remaining time; `emergency-restore` keeps them, `recover` and uninstall remove them. Added `roamswitch server bans` / `unban` (`roamswitch bans` / `unban` on the client edition), the read-only MCP tool `get_ssh_guard_incidents`, an incident screen in the server TUI and 10 `ssh_guard_*` keys. The client edition works only while sshd is running. Measured on an Ubuntu 24.04 VM: about 0.2 seconds from the 6th failure to the ban, and a real hydra run (4 parallel) stopped after 6 failures.
- **FIM notifications carry the intrusion path** (1.12.0): who made the change (account, executable, command) and from which SSH login (source IP and port, login time, authentication method, key fingerprint); short-lived processes that have exited are filled in from the exec recorder; unknown is written as unknown, and "a local login" is told apart from "could not be determined". It works with the SSH guard disabled and does not rely on values a process can change itself (comm, argv, environment variables). Recorded in `fim_intrusions.json`, MCP `verify_fim` gained `recentIntrusionContext`, setting `fim_intrusion_context_enabled` (default true). Replace-style edits (`install`, `sed -i`, `mv` within the same directory) are detected too; **`mv` from a different directory is still caught only by the periodic scan (a limitation).**
- **eBPF isolation timing, heartbeat and reverse shell watch** (1.12.0, Server Edition): the default rules of Falco 0.45.0 (29 rules) have none that detect a reverse shell on the host. The isolation delay is recorded in three parts (detection to receipt, receipt to after the action, total) in `ebpf_incidents.json` and MCP `get_ebpf_incidents`, and in the notification (measured: 42 to 64 ms for process isolation plus SIGKILL, 109 ms for host Air-Gap). A RoamSwitch rule `96-roamswitch-heartbeat.yaml` is added to Falco and the daemon measures every 10 minutes how long Falco's output takes to come back; if it does not come back twice it warns "the rule is not loaded, or the output is not arriving" (measured: 2 to 6 ms round trip, a warning about 164 seconds after the rule was removed); heartbeat lines never cause isolation, notifications or incident records, and diagnostic K-04 fails when Falco runs but the heartbeat does not come back (the diagnostic stays at 34 items). Reverse shell detection looks every 2 seconds in `/proc` for a shell or interpreter whose standard input and output are connected to an external TCP socket (external addresses only by default; about 2.4 seconds after the connection; ssh, ProxyCommand and git over ssh are not detected). A non-root process is isolated and killed; **a root-owned one fails process isolation and goes on to the host-wide Air-Gap** (the existing Critical policy). The ways out are `reverse_shell_watch_notify_only=true` and `allowlist add "RoamSwitch Reverse Shell" <process name>`. 4 setting keys (`ebpf_heartbeat_interval_secs` and others).
- **Fixes found on a real kernel** (1.12.0): (1) the FIM (fanotify) notification thread panicked when it called the notification function and stopped at the first notification; (2) the process exec recorder discarded the end-of-message marker (`NLMSG_DONE`) and recorded nothing; (3) with fanotify, a watch target reached through a symbolic link (such as `/bin/su`) did not match the real path the kernel returns and was ignored.
- **AI service diagnostic** (1.12.0): "usable from the network without authentication" was shown even for services listening only on 127.0.0.1 (confirmed on a real machine with Ollama); the results for Ollama, Jupyter, MLflow and Gradio are now shown only when the listener is reachable from the network.
- **MCP config audit** (1.12.0): a new finding `injection_exposure` (medium) in `audit_mcp_configs`, once per file where the same config file has both a server that makes third-party text readable (web fetch, browser, search, chat, email, GitHub and so on) and a server that can run commands or change files, the arrangement that indirect prompt injection (ATT&CK T1698) depends on. It is only a match against known package names; it does not look at what the tools do or whether an AI follows instructions, combinations across files are not added up, and arguments and environment variables are not included in the result.
- **Bundled apps and counts** (1.12.0): AccessScope 0.3.7, PathScope 0.1.9 and PersonalSOC 0.1.14 (see each app's entry), including the PersonalSOC fix for results matched with Ubuntu's data being lowered on Ubuntu machines (on an Ubuntu machine running 1.11.28, updating shows the OS package CVE findings with the correct severity and confidence). 146 setting keys (109 in `server.conf`, 37 in `guard.yaml`) and 38 MCP tools; the added wording is in 10 languages (based on machine translation, not reviewed by native speakers).
- **Honeytoken watcher fix** (1.12.1, Linux client edition and Server Edition): when a decoy credential file is read, related decoys (the AWS config, the SSH config, the Kubernetes config) are planted, but every time that happened the watcher (fanotify) was closed and rebuilt, so events later in the same batch and events during the rebuild were lost. A stealer that reads credential files milliseconds apart lost track after the first one: on a real host, 3 of 5 reads were not detected. New decoys are now added to the running watcher instead of rebuilding it; on a real host (Ubuntu 24.04), all 5 reads and the 3 additional decoys are detected.
- **Web shell account response fix** (1.12.1, Server Edition): revoking the login shell of the account that planted a web shell (`account_action=revoke_shell`) did not work inside the service. The service runs under systemd's protection (`ProtectSystem=strict`), where `/etc` is read-only, so `usermod` could not lock `/etc/passwd` (`cannot lock /etc/passwd`): the web shell was quarantined but the account was left as it was. It only worked when `roamswitch web-scan --quarantine` was run directly as a root command. When the direct call fails, it is now run again as a transient unit (`systemd-run`) outside the service's protection. On a real host, from inside the service, the shell became `/usr/sbin/nologin`, was recorded in `web-rules locked`, and `unlock-account` restored it.
- **Notification repeat fix** (1.12.2, Linux client and Server Edition): the lockfile and critical-path tamper notifications repeated with the same content every time the daemon restarted. The record of already-announced changes lived only in memory and was lost whenever the daemon started again, for example after a package update or a reboot. The baseline is deliberately never advanced on its own (so a possibly tampered change is not silently accepted), so the same notification came back at every start until `lockfile-fim update` or `fim update` was run. The record is now saved under `/var/lib/roamswitch/` (`lockfile_fim_notified.txt` and `fim_notified.txt`) and carried across restarts. A change that has been resolved drops out of the record, so the same file changing again is announced again. Detection itself is unchanged: `verify` keeps reporting the violation until the baseline is updated. If saving fails, the only effect is a repeated notification after the next restart; detection does not stop.
- **Server Edition forwarding fix** (1.12.3): a Docker container port published with `-p` could not be reached from outside, even when it was in `allowed_ports` or `ssh_ports`. The Default-Deny `forward` chain accepted only established connections and traffic that did not arrive from the external interface, so a new connection forwarded after DNAT was dropped there although the `DOCKER-USER` guard let it through (a host that publishes containers only on `127.0.0.1` was not affected). A new DNAT-ed TCP connection whose original destination is an allowed port is now accepted too; every other published port is still dropped by the same `policy drop` and the `DOCKER-USER` guard (in a network-namespace test of ports 80 and 8080, both were dropped before the change; after it, 80 passes and 8080 is still dropped). The Client Edition has no such chain and is not affected.
- **Reports of SUID / SGID files that appear or change, and of logs that are cleared** (1.12.4, Server Edition): `suid_watch_enabled` (on by default) walks the local filesystems every `suid_watch_interval_secs` (default 600; 60 to 86400) and records the mode, owner, size and SHA-256 of every SUID / SGID executable in `/var/lib/roamswitch/suid_baseline.json` (0600). The first inventory is the trusted state; after that a new file, a bit added later, a changed mode or owner, or changed content is reported once as Critical and recorded in the incident timeline (ATT&CK T1548.001). A change a package upgrade explains (the file belongs to a package and its change time is not later than the package database) is taken into the baseline silently. Container image layers (`/var/lib/docker` and the like), snap, and network, FUSE, overlay and squashfs mounts are not walked. `roamswitch fim verify` also prints the differences (exit status 1) and `sudo roamswitch fim update` accepts the current state. `log_tamper_watch_enabled` (on by default) checks `auth.log`, `secure`, `syslog`, `messages`, `kern.log`, `audit/audit.log`, `cron`, `dpkg.log`, `wtmp`, `btmp` and `lastlog` every 30 seconds and reports one that became smaller (it was 1024 bytes or more), disappeared or was replaced by a different file (ATT&CK T1070.002), unless log rotation explains it (the old file kept under another name, a fresh copy or compressed file next to it, or a logrotate status file updated within 2 minutes). Archived journal files (`system@….journal`) are reported when their total, at least 4 MiB before, falls below a fifth (for example `journalctl --vacuum-time=1s`); active journal files are left out because journald preallocates 8 MiB for each. Both follow the BeyondTrust Remote Support zero-day intrusion (Mandiant, October 2026: web shell, SUID enabled, root, logs cleared). On an Ubuntu 24.04 VM, `chmod u+s` on a copy of bash, a copy of a system binary and a package-owned file were all reported on the next walk (about 17 seconds); a package-owned file given the bit and followed by a package database update was taken in silently; `auth.log`, `syslog` (9 MB) and `dpkg.log` emptied or removed and the journal vacuumed were reported on the next 30-second look; a real `logrotate -f` was not. The first walk took 2 seconds for 20 SUID files. Not seen: files inside container image layers, a bit set and cleared again during a walk, changes made while a package manager is running (the walk is postponed up to 3 times), records of `wtmp` / `btmp` / `lastlog` rewritten without changing the size, and a decoy sibling file placed just before a wipe to look like a rotation. There are 149 setting keys (112 in `server.conf` and 37 in `guard.yaml`).
- (1.12.5) **Fixed: the Linux client's autostart watch compared files by modification time and size, not by content** (Linux client edition). A file rewritten with another payload of the same length, with its modification time put back (`touch -r`), looked unchanged and was not examined again, and touching a file without changing it counted as a change. The watch now compares the SHA-256 of the content (files up to 1 MiB; bigger or unreadable ones still by size and modification time; links by where they point). Checked by unit tests: a same-length rewrite with the time put back is now a change, and a touch alone is not. The Mac edition had a related bug, fixed in Mac 1.12.3: a reported LaunchAgent path was never reported again while the app ran.
- (1.12.5) The Server Edition is unchanged. The bundled apps are unchanged (AccessScope 0.3.7, PathScope 0.1.9, PersonalSOC 0.1.14).
- (1.12.6) **Added: the AI agent setup audit gains four detections and change detection against an accepted state** (the Client Edition's "AI Agent Setup Audit" screen, `roamswitch audit-agent-setup`, the Server Edition's command and TUI, and the MCP tool `audit_agent_setup`). It closes the gap that the instruction files, skills and MCP settings an AI agent reads could be swapped later without anyone noticing. The idea was taken from reading NVIDIA's SkillSpector (a scanner for AI agent skills); the rules are RoamSwitch's own, and no rule or regular expression was copied.
- (1.12.6) **Four new findings**: `homoglyph_name` (a skill folder name, file name or front-matter `name` that mixes Latin letters with lookalike Cyrillic, Greek or Armenian ones; Cyrillic is high, the others medium), `whitespace_padding` (text after 100 or more blanks on a line, or after 30 or more empty lines, so that the rest sits out of view), `hidden_encoded_text` (a base64 run of 60 characters or more that decodes to readable text, or `data:text/…;base64,`; the decoded text is never written into the result, only the name of the rule it would trip, such as instruction-override wording; hashes and images are left out), and `skill_broad_tool_grant` (a front-matter `allowed-tools` that permits arbitrary code: all of `Bash`, `*`, or `Bash(curl:*)` and the like; a narrowed grant such as `Bash(git status:*)` is left alone). A comment of the form `[//]: # (…)` is now treated like an HTML comment, a place that is not displayed, and a finding inside one is rated high.
- (1.12.6) **Change detection**: pressing "Mark this state as reviewed" (the button on the screen, or `roamswitch audit-agent-setup --accept`; `--accept-with-findings` when findings remain) records the instruction and skill files and the MCP server definitions as SHA-256 digests only (`~/.config/roamswitch/agent_setup_baseline.json`, 0600; no file content and no MCP argument or environment value is stored). Later audits report a file whose content changed (`agent_file_changed`), a new file (`agent_file_added`), an MCP server whose settings changed (`mcp_server_changed`; high when the launch command, arguments, address, environment and the like changed, otherwise medium; the names of the changed keys are shown) and a new MCP server (`mcp_server_added`). **Only a person can move the baseline.** Neither an audit run nor the MCP tool moves it, so an AI agent cannot mark its own tampering as reviewed. A removed file is not reported. A new file is reported only inside a folder that was already covered when the state was accepted, so registering another project folder does not turn everything in it into "new". The MCP tool's result gained the time the baseline was recorded (`baselineAcceptedAt`, null if none).
- (1.12.6) Run read-only over a real home folder (6 skills, 363 files), the four new rules gave 0 false positives, and a re-run right after recording the baseline showed no change. The core unit tests (1758) and the MCP, CLI, TUI and GTK tests pass. The GTK screen was checked only as far as build and tests; its look on a real screen will be checked after release.
- (1.12.6) The number of finding ids went from 22 to 30. The added wording is in 10 languages (based on machine translation, and not reviewed by native speakers).
- (1.12.6) The bundled apps are unchanged (AccessScope 0.3.7, PathScope 0.1.9, PersonalSOC 0.1.14).
- **Running AI agents under a dedicated account** (1.12.7, Server Edition): for the accounts listed in `agent_users` in `server.conf`, an outbound allowlist (`agent_egress_allow`; new outbound TCP and UDP to anywhere else is dropped; empty means "no limit"), a baseline held by root (`/var/lib/roamswitch/agent_setup/<account>.json`; the accepted state of instruction files, skills and MCP server definitions, which that account cannot rewrite) and decoy credentials in that account's home. These cover the items of the 16 October 2026 NIST, NSA and CISA guidance that one server can implement. An agent that can become `root` is not stopped. The exec-record hash chain now also covers the segment being written, through a checkpoint every 10 seconds (`roamswitch events verify`).
- **Quarantine, Web rules and forwarding** (1.12.7): the process section of the evidence bundle was empty when a quarantine killed the process; the process state is now saved before the kill. The Web rule files have a JSON Schema and mistakes are reported at load time (`roamswitch web-rules example schema`, `web-rules validate`). The JSON Lines forwarding file can be made readable by a chosen group (`forward_file_group`). A Docker risk notice mentions when the container is an Exegol one (an Exegol container alone raises no notice).
- **A warning for text that reads like an instruction to the AI, and how detection is shown** (1.12.8): when an MCP result contains text aimed at the AI ("ignore the previous instructions", fake system tags, impersonated tool calls, data smuggled out through an image link, claims of authority), a separate block at the end of the result warns about it. `audit-agent-setup` findings carry OWASP LLM Top 10 and ATT&CK tags and can be output as SARIF 2.1.0. Local AI runtimes are told apart with a confidence level (`aiRuntimes` in `get_exposed_ports`). The new command `roamswitch detection-coverage` and MCP tool `get_detection_coverage` report, for ATT&CK techniques, whether RoamSwitch can find them, what it cannot see and whether the guard in charge is running. Places where a guard silently lost its input (the SSH log, ARP, the exec record, Falco, the fanotify-based watchers) are now counted and shown in `get_guard_status` and in notifications. The Web rules watch also adds the public folders of Docker containers automatically.
- **More diagnostics** (1.13.0): the Server Edition diagnosis gained the security headers the web server really returns (HSTS, nosniff, framing protection, cookies), an optional check of your own domain (SPF, DMARC, registration expiry), TLS certificate expiry and Docker's `daemon.json`. Both editions gained backup recency and name-resolution exposure (LLMNR, NBNS). The diagnosis now has 30 items on the Client edition and 40 on the Server Edition.
- **Accepting, EPSS, output and import** (1.13.0): package CVE findings can be accepted with a reason and an expiry (`roamswitch vuln-accept`; not from MCP). The CVE map can carry EPSS (a rough probability of exploitation). Detection coverage can be output as an ATT&CK Navigator layer. A static check of where dependencies are fetched from (`extra-index-url` and the like) and import of Sigma rules (`process_creation`) were added. Operators can add processes that containment never touches (`protected_processes`, `roamswitch server allowlist test`).
- **Reverse shells and the exec record** (1.13.0): the Client edition also got reverse-shell watching (on by default, notify only) and it also sees the shapes with pipes, FIFOs, ptys and connected UDP. The exec-record rules gained reverse-shell-shaped command lines (EXR-011) and debugger attachment by a service account (EXR-012), and EXR-003 and EXR-004 match more. Also added: watching of network configuration changes, more kinds of credential-file reads, and read-only annotations on the MCP tools.
- **Web access guard, Web gate and `web-id-check`** (1.14.0, Server Edition): from the web server access log it notifies on requests that walk through receipt or order numbers one after another (a request for a reserved number `canary`, a run of 404s for non-existent numbers from one source, many kinds of numbers on the same place, several sources walking the numbers in turn). The default is notify only (`mode=warn` in `/etc/roamswitch/web_guard.conf`); with `mode=block` the matching source is blocked from new connections to 80/443 for a limited time (the allow list, the gateway, this host itself, shared addresses where three or more browsers are seen, and private addresses are excluded). `roamswitch emergency-restore` lifts the block. It also looks for probing of the site structure in four shapes (`path_walk`: 12 or more kinds of non-existent paths; `asset_harvest`: 40 or more `.js`/`.mjs`/`.css`/`.map` requests without a Referer; `api_probe`: 20 or more different paths under `/api/` answered 401, 403 or 404; `param_fuzz`: 30 or more different queries on one path with more than half of the responses 4xx). The limits can be changed with `path_walk_limit`, `asset_harvest_limit`, `api_probe_limit` and `param_fuzz_limit` (3 to 10000); `recon=off|warn|block` defaults to `warn`, and blocking happens only when both `mode=block` and `recon=block` are set. Crawlers and monitors can be excluded with `exempt_path` (prefix match) and `exempt_ua` (substring, lower case, 3 characters or more). Measured on a real nginx log, one crawling source was found at request 12 (`path_walk`), 70 (`asset_harvest`), 110 (`api_probe`) and 145 (`param_fuzz`), and 120 requests of ordinary browser use produced none. The Web gate (`roamswitch-webgate`, optional) is a small component that answers nginx `auth_request`, works over a Unix socket only, and limits attacks that keep changing IP with a per-location budget of new-number requests per minute (when it is stopped, requests pass; `roamswitch web-gate-config <path>` prints the nginx configuration and changes nothing; it cannot see the Referer or the response code, so it does not look for the four site-structure shapes). `roamswitch web-id-check` asks your own site for consecutive numbers (up to 10) without logging in and checks whether each number returns different content. It also notifies, from the error log, when nginx or Apache workers exit on a crash signal 3 times in 5 minutes (the parent restarts workers, so no monitor saw this before).
- **Web rules and the client's scanned-file memory** (1.14.0): fixed the Web rules watcher not checking a web shell written over the same place right after a harmless file (within 2 seconds); it now checks again after the wait (Server Edition). The client's memory of scanned files is now decided by the nanosecond modification time (a file of the same size overwritten within the same second could be treated as already scanned).
- **Expiry for the guard allow list** (1.14.0, Server Edition): `roamswitch server allowlist add` now requires a reason (`--reason`), and the expiry defaults to 30 days, at most 365 (`--days`). An expired entry stops working (it stays in the list as "expired") and `roamswitch server allowlist renew <rule> <process> [--days N]` counts again from now. One notification goes out 7 days before and one when it expires; the server TUI list shows the days left and the `e` key renews (adding from the TUI asks for no reason and sets 30 days). Turning the wall clock back does not revive an expired entry (the last seen time is kept, and a move back of a day or more is notified), and an unreadable expiry counts as expired. Only the allow list gets an expiry: blocks, isolation and bans are not released by it, and neither the guard nor the MCP tools add or renew entries. Old entries from before the update stay allowed, show as "no expiry (old entry)" and are notified once a day; the Sensor-companion preset has no expiry and shows as "preset". **Limit**: if root can also rewrite the time file, this is bypassed. The aim is to stop a mistake in configuration or a clock error from extending an allow, not to stop an attacker who took root. The Mac allow list and `whitelist_ips` in `guard.yaml` still have no expiry.
- **Notification retry, and sturdier evidence and updates** (1.14.0, Server Edition): Telegram, LINE and Webhook notifications used to be sent once with a single log line on failure (`curl` returns a success exit code even for HTTP 500, so they counted as sent). Now the HTTP status decides and only 2xx counts as delivered; 408, 429, 5xx and connection failures are retried (other 4xx such as a wrong token are not), 3 tries at once (1 s and 5 s apart), and if still undelivered the message goes to `/var/lib/roamswitch/pending_deliveries.json` (root-owned, 0600, at most 50) and is resent every 5 minutes (stopping after 24 hours or 24 tries, with a log line, and "[Delayed]" in front of the text). The emergency notice just before Air-Gap is tried once and queued for when the connection returns. The log never contains tokens, `user:pass@` of a webhook URL or Authorization. The incident-capture manifest and each file are written to a temporary file, fsynced and then renamed, and `chain.log` lines are fsynced too, so a power loss leaves no empty or half-written manifest. `roamswitch-updater` checks the shape of the content after verifying the signature (threat feed: at least one domain; CVE maps: a JSON object with `mapVersion` and the expected members); if it does not fit, it is not installed and the current one stays.
- **`roamswitch doctor [--json]`, `scan-frontend` and execution record rule EXR-013** (1.14.0): `doctor` (client and server; changes nothing, no network) checks the tools the defence uses (`nft`, `systemctl`, `journalctl`, `ip` required; `curl`, `dig`, `ss`, `clamscan` optional), that those and RoamSwitch's own files are root-owned and not writable by others, the package manager verification (`dpkg --verify` or `rpm -V`), whether it runs as root, manually installed CA certificates under `/usr/local/share/ca-certificates` and similar, whether the daemon is running, and whether ufw, firewalld, fail2ban or crowdsec-firewall-bouncer are running (a caution if so). Exit code 1 if anything failed, 0 if only cautions; it is also in the server TUI "Tools". `roamswitch scan-frontend <folder>` (also in the server TUI) reads the files a front end publishes (`dist`, `build`, `out`, `.next/static`, `.output/public`, `.svelte-kit/output`, `public`, `static`, or the folder itself if none exist) for embedded API keys, private keys, crypto keys, JWTs and source maps that should not be in production. `audit-secrets` skips `dist` and `build` to avoid double reports on generated output, so the published side had not been checked. A found value is shown only as its first 4 characters and length; exit code 1 if anything is found; limits are 5 MB per file, 200 MB in total, 20,000 files, and symbolic links are not followed. The MCP `audit_secrets` gained the optional argument `include_build_output` (the result adds `buildOutput`; the Mac version has the same argument). Execution record rule EXR-013 notifies when a process started from a service (nginx, Apache, php-fpm and so on) or running as a service account runs with `LD_PRELOAD` or `LD_AUDIT` pointing to a library outside the system directories (high), or `LD_LIBRARY_PATH` pointing to a writable place (`/tmp`, `/var/tmp`, `/dev/shm`, `/home`, `/var/www`; medium). It matches the trick of bypassing PHP `disable_functions` with `putenv("LD_PRELOAD=…")` and `mail()`, and covers the earlier step that EXR-007 did not see. It only notifies and is never a quarantine candidate, root and ordinary login users are excluded, and only the values of those 3 variables (paths, at most 200 characters) are read from `/proc/<pid>/environ`. There are now 13 rules. The attack tool names of EXR-009 and EXR-010 were also increased (chisel, ligolo, ligolo-ng, suo5, stowaway, iox, venom, mimikatz, certipy, rubeus, kerbrute, bloodhound, sharphound, sliver, havoc, godzilla, behinder, antsword, weevely, fscan, gogo, ehole, oneforall, ksubdomain, dnsx, subzy, tlsx, cdncheck and the impacket family; frp, nps, ngrok and ssh are left out because they are used daily), and EXR-010 now also covers programs whose executable is under `/home`, `/root`, `/tmp` and similar.
- **MCP configuration audit, active scan, CSV and MCP result warnings** (1.14.0): the MCP configuration audit gained `traffic_interception_env` (medium): it flags `env` entries for proxies (`HTTP_PROXY`, `HTTPS_PROXY`, `ALL_PROXY`), extra trusted authorities (`NODE_EXTRA_CA_CERTS`, `SSL_CERT_FILE`, `SSL_CERT_DIR`, `REQUESTS_CA_BUNDLE`, `CURL_CA_BUNDLE`, `GIT_SSL_CAINFO`) and turning certificate verification off (`NODE_TLS_REJECT_UNAUTHORIZED=0`); values are not shown (host only for proxies), and the finding says a corporate proxy can be legitimate. The Mac version has the same finding. The client active scan gained `webvuln-unauth-admin`: it sends one GET each, with no cookie or authentication, to 7 fixed paths (`/admin`, `/api/admin`, `/api/users`, `/api/v1/users`, `/api/v1/user`, `/actuator/env`, `/swagger-ui/index.html`) on the dev server port at `127.0.0.1`, and reports only a 200 that returns data (a non-empty JSON array, a JSON object that is not an error, HTML with a user table, Swagger UI); login pages, redirects, SPA shells and `{"error":"unauthorized"}` are not reported, it never logs in, guesses or fuzzes, and the 7 requests stay in the log. The Mac active scan has the same check. Fixed CSV exports where a spreadsheet could run a cell starting with `=`, `+`, `-`, `@`, a tab or a carriage return as a formula (such cells get a leading `'` in exports of notification history, log audit, activity log, CVE scan and so on; the same on Mac). More patterns were added to the warning for AI-instruction-like text in MCP results (requests to list tools or print API keys, tokens or environment variables `system-prompt-leak`; self-certification requests that make the model confirm it has authority `authority-claim`; "report as safe", "report no threat", "mark as resolved" `instruction-override`; no new categories; the same on Mac). The bundled apps now include AccessScope 0.4.1 (PathScope 0.2.0 and PersonalSOC 0.2.0 unchanged).
- **The Web access guard did not read the per-site Apache access logs** (1.14.1, Server Edition): it read only the two default files (`/var/log/apache2/access.log` and `other_vhosts_access.log`) and the `access_log` files named in the nginx configuration, so with Apache a site that has its own `CustomLog` was not seen at all. It now looks, every 10 minutes, for the files named by `CustomLog` and `TransferLog` in the Apache configuration (`apache2.conf`, `sites-enabled`, `conf-enabled`, `httpd.conf`, `conf.d`) and reads them (piped logs and `/dev/` are not read). On a real server with 14 per-site `CustomLog` files, all 14 were found, and the 145 requests from one IP that were in its log were found as `path_walk` at request 48 and `api_probe` at request 56. **Limits**: configuration placed elsewhere with `Include` is not read; lines that cannot be parsed are skipped; if one request is written to several logs, it can be counted twice.
- **Ransomware detection** (1.15.0): partial encryption (only the head of a file, or every few blocks, rewritten) now counts towards the burst. The content is split into 4 KiB windows, and a file of 16 KiB or more with two or more windows at 7.9 bits/byte or higher counts as a high-entropy write. Before, the mean of the whole file looked low, so nothing was frozen and no alert was shown. The burst condition (20 files in 5 seconds) is unchanged, and setting `ransomware_window_entropy_enabled` to `false` in `config.json` turns off this rule alone (the same rule as on macOS). Rewrites finer than a window are not visible to it. Also fixed: writes by allowlisted programs (gpg, openssl, age, 7z, python3, node, pip, ...) were all skipped on the name alone, so a Python script that overwrote existing files with random bytes, or a loop of `openssl enc` and `rm`, was not frozen. A program that only creates new files is still not counted; when it rewrites a file that already existed, the write counts like any other process's. The name counts only when the executable is in a place only root writes (`/usr`, `/opt`, `/snap`, ...). A program that creates 20 or more new high-entropy files while deleting as many originals in the same folders within 5 seconds is frozen too. cp, mv, rsync, tar, git, unzip, shred, package managers, file managers, browsers, messengers and container/VM runtimes gave bursts on ordinary jobs in measurement, so they stay as before (`ransomware_allowlist_strict` set to `false` goes back). Not seen: writing to a temporary file and replacing the original with `rename`, writing the ciphertext to another folder, and output named `.zip`, `.gz` and the like. A Python script that compresses files and deletes the originals can be frozen (`roamswitch frozen resume` resumes it). `rclone sync` rewrites a file in place when the destination already has one of the same name, so a plain sync of changed files counted as rewriting existing files and caused a burst (48 changed files gave 48 overwrites and 2 bursts); `rclone` is again trusted for every write, on its name and location alone. In throwaway VMs, extracting tar files and debs, git, node_modules, apt, copies, and the ordinary use of borg, restic, duplicity and ffmpeg did not cause a burst.
- **The frozen-process record and the confirmation dialog** (1.15.0): the frozen-process record (`/run/roamswitch/frozen_processes.json`) was read and rewritten without a lock, so with several threads freezing at once the later write dropped the earlier entry and a freeze could be missing from `roamswitch frozen list` (a test that writes concurrently kept only 9 of 128). It is now written under a lock and replaced in one step, and a new freeze replaces a stale entry for the same pid. The "Dangerous file detected" dialog opened again right after Esc, "Later" or closing the window, because the confirmation is checked every 0.8 s. A request closed that way is not shown again for a minute (it stays pending; the daemon withdraws it after 3 minutes and the file is left alone). An approval dialog the daemon had withdrawn stayed on the screen; it now closes when the request is withdrawn.
- **Text that did not follow the display language** (1.15.0): the reason the SSH brute-force guard only detects (the "Reason" line of `roamswitch server bans` and the TUI), the reason for a broken hash chain in `roamswitch events verify` (the CLI in Japanese and English, the TUI in ten languages; the reason MCP returns stays in English), the record written when a risk acceptance runs out (timeline, notification history) and the "entropy analysis" sentence of the ransomware detection (`roamswitch-ransomware-rollback watch`) were in English even in a Japanese display. Honeytoken notifications came out in English without `LANG` in the service even when `language` was `ja` (the default), and `roamswitch-incident-capture` ignored the language in `server.conf` on the Server Edition. Input errors of `roamswitch server config set` and the like for `domain_check_domains`, `ssh_guard_log_source`, `agent_users`, `agent_setup_watch_secs`, `ebpf_heartbeat_interval_secs`, numbers, ranges and true/false are now in all 10 languages (the errors for the address lists and `protected_processes` are still English). The Server Edition follows `language` in `server.conf`.
- **Saying what was not checked, in diagnoses and scans** (1.15.0): the detection coverage list (`roamswitch coverage`, the MCP `get_detection_coverage`) showed T1562.001 and T1562.006 as on for a server with neither Falco nor Tetragon (Server Edition), because the mere existence of the heartbeat file counted as running. Now only a probe that came back (`ok`) is on; silent, waiting and disabled are off, and a state that is not yet known is unknown. `scan-packages <folder>` said "no match" for what it had not checked; a language with no CVE data (the Server Edition does not fetch it, by design) is now reported as "not checked" with a count, and a CVE listed twice is shown once. `scan-scripts` said "nothing added" for folders never marked as reviewed; it now says how many folders were not compared (CLI, window, and `uncoveredFolders` in the MCP result). The "backup freshness" check counted Ubuntu's dpkg-db-backup (a daily copy of the dpkg database) as a backup, so a host that had made no backup passed; it is no longer counted. The domain check (Server Edition) said the expiry was fine when it could not be read; it now says how many domains had no readable expiry. `config set domain_check_domains` said a restart was needed, but the key is read at every check. After a process-level isolation (`roamswitch server ebpf`, the TUI), the isolated process still looked as if it were running once it had ended; an ended process is marked "exited", with one line saying that the rule blocking traffic stays and that `sudo roamswitch emergency-restore` lifts it.
- **Risk acceptance (`vuln-accept`) and the agent-setup audit** (1.15.0): the `vuln-accept` file became root-owned when root rewrote it, so its owner could not read it and the list looked empty. A rewrite now keeps the original owner (mode stays 0600), and so does saving the reviewed states. A file that cannot be read or is corrupt is reported as such instead of "no finding is accepted". `vuln-accept` said "accepted" for a version or CVE that is not in the scan result without a word; the acceptance is still recorded, with a warning. The completion line of `audit-agent-setup --accept` now counts the settings files marked as reviewed. A new finding `agent_settings_writable` (high) is reported for a Claude Code settings file (settings.json) that every user can write: another user could add a rule that allows every command without asking, or a hook that runs each time. A group-writable mode (664) is the default and is not reported.
- **Protected-process checks** (1.15.0, Server Edition): the TUI containment check did not read `protected_processes`, so an ordinary user's `roamswitch server allowlist test` could not read server.conf (0600) and said "not protected"; it now says "cannot tell, run as root". A script that was merely named like a protected process (sshd and the like) got the real one's protection; when the executable is an interpreter such as bash or python, the name now protects it only if the running script is in a system location (/usr and the like), and any other script is protected by listing its absolute path in `protected_processes`.
- **Honeytokens, forwarding and notification history** (1.15.0): accesses to the honeytokens did not reach the `forward_*` destinations (SIEM, syslog, webhook), because the honeytoken watcher is a separate process and no forwarder ran in it; it now starts one from the same settings. A `forward_file_path` directly under `/var/log` passed the check but could not be written under `ProtectSystem=strict` (Server Edition); a location the service cannot write is now reported by `forward status` and at daemon start, and that destination is not used. The unit gained `LogsDirectory=roamswitch`, so `/var/log/roamswitch/` works. An autostart alert was recorded twice in the notification history; it is recorded once. Right after start on a new host, the history held a record of the daemon's own helper opening the honeytokens; reads by the daemon and its helper are not counted.
- **Network and output** (1.15.0): with DNS threat protection on, the resolver uses the protection provider's DNS (Quad9, for example), not the one DHCP handed out, yet a changed DNS in the lease raised "queries may now go to another server". When `resolvectl dns` shows only other servers for the link, the lease's DNS servers are not compared and not remembered (a change of DHCP server or gateway is still reported; once the protection is off and the resolver follows the lease, a DNS change is reported too; same idea as the Mac app). `roamswitch notifications | head` and similar commands printed a panic message when the reader closed the output early; the command now ends without a message, with exit code 141.
- **Publishing the CVE maps** (1.15.0): the build kept going after a failed EPSS fetch and published a map without EPSS. It now stops and raises an error in GitHub Actions, and checks that the finished map has `epssDate` and `epss`. The maps published then had no EPSS because they were built (2026-10-19 06:03 UTC) before the EPSS step was added; the next refresh carries it.
- **Web access guard and `web-guard-check`** (1.15.0, Server Edition): added `roamswitch web-guard-check [--hours N]` (root). It reads the access logs and the web server configuration and looks at the busiest addresses, the share of requests from private addresses (a proxy or a container network), the share from shared addresses (several browsers behind one address) and whether a setting restores the visitor's address (Apache `RemoteIPHeader`; nginx `real_ip_header` with `set_real_ip_from`), then reports fit, works for part only, not fit, or cannot tell (fewer than 200 requests). Exit code 1 when not fit; it changes nothing and uses no network. It does not recognise a CDN by name: with a CDN or a company proxy in front that does not pass the visitor's address on, everybody looks like one address and blocking would cut off many visitors. On a real VM, nginx with 400 visits from 40 addresses was "fit", the same visits through a proxy that does not pass the address on were "not fit", and adding the setting that restores the address made it "fit" again; with blocking on in the not-fit setup, the proxy's address was blocked and the next 40 visitors all received 504. The guidance in the alerts now appears only where the blocking state and the check disagree (the check is worked out from the logs and reused for six hours). The alert for probing of the site's structure (`path_walk` and the like) did not mention `recon=block` (this kind of detection is not blocked by `mode=block` alone); the guidance now names both (10 languages). `roamswitch unban` lifted only the SSH guard's bans, and now lifts the web access guard's too (a separate firewall table). A scanner that changed its User-Agent from request to request was treated as a "shared address with several browsers" and not blocked; an address where fewer than one answer in five (of 10 or more) was served (2xx and 304) is no longer treated as shared (on a real VM, the 12th of 60 requests was blocked and the remaining 48 never arrived).
- **Active diagnostics** (1.15.0, Client edition): added a reflected-XSS probe, `webvuln-xss`. GET only: a harmless marker without any script is put into parameters read from the root page's links and GET forms, and a finding is reported only if it comes back unescaped where markup or an attribute quote takes effect. At most 12 requests go to one port, and when nothing is reflected the result is "could not confirm". The Mac app has the same probe.
- **Bundled apps** (1.15.0): AccessScope 0.4.2 and PersonalSOC 0.2.1 (PathScope 0.2.0 is unchanged).
- (1.15.1) **Added: a record of random bytes** (Server Edition, off by default). A ransomware makes the keys of its files from random bytes it gets on the machine. With `prn_record=true` in `server.conf`, what `getrandom` returned and what reads of `/dev/urandom` and `/dev/random` returned are recorded for a limited time, as the material a decryptor made for that family needs to rebuild the keys after an attack. RoamSwitch does not decrypt. `sudo roamswitch-prn-recorder export --output FILE` writes them in `memento.prn/v1`, the format the Memento decryptor of KDDI Research reads.
  - **Deadline**: a record older than `prn_retention_hours` (1 to 168 hours, default 24) is overwritten with zeros and removed (up to 10 minutes late). Past `prn_max_mib` (16 to 4096, default 256) the oldest go first. A value outside the range is refused, not clamped. Turning `prn_record` off removes what was recorded when the daemon next starts.
  - **Cost**: it hooks only the kernel's random-byte functions, so reads of other files and every other system call cost nothing (measured: a million file reads differ within noise; a recorded call costs about 1.2 microseconds and about 67 bytes). The syscall-tracepoint way slows every system call of the machine, so it is not the default.
  - **The record is a secret**: it also holds the secrets of TLS and SSH sessions the host made. Only root can read it (0600), and it never appears in notifications, evidence bundles, MCP or event forwarding.
  - **Not seen**: a path that returns random bytes without a system call (glibc 2.41 and newer using the vDSO for `getrandom()`) and RDRAND executed in user space. `sudo roamswitch-prn-recorder status` warns about them. When the kernel has no fentry or lacks the functions needed, recording does not start and you are notified with the reason.
- (1.15.1) **Added: the Air-Gap emergency window now points to ways of getting encrypted files back when a ransomware encryption burst or a canary tampering is detected** (desktop edition). It shows links to No More Ransom, which collects free decryption tools, and to the Japanese National Police Agency's ransomware page, plus a QR code of the first, because this PC cannot open it while the Air-Gap is on. Decryption tools exist only for some families whose workings have been analysed, and RoamSwitch itself does not decrypt files. The text also says to remove the malware before using a tool, to keep the encrypted files and the ransom note, and not to pay. The Server Edition adds the same two addresses to the canary-tampering notification.
- (1.15.1) **Added: evidence capture can include the memory of a process** (`roamswitch-incident-capture snapshot --pid PID --memory`). The writable memory (heap, stacks, anonymous regions, data of loaded libraries; limit 512 MiB) is saved to `process/memory.bin` and the position of each region to `process/memory_index.json` (`memory.bin` is 0600 and covered by the manifest's SHA-256). A frozen (SIGSTOP) process stays readable until it is killed. Measured on a frozen perl process (about 77 MiB): 5.7 s, against 0.13 s without memory. It can hold passwords and keys, so it is off by default and the automatic capture on detection does not use it.
- (1.15.1) **Fixed: freezing (SIGSTOP) and killing (SIGKILL) a process now go to the process itself, not to a number.** If the PID passed to another process between the decision and the signal, an unrelated process could receive it. The process is pinned with a pidfd, so the protected-process check and the signal concern the same process. "Terminate and restore" in the Air-Gap window records the start time with the incident and ends only a process that matches it (an incident with no recorded start time ends nothing). The start time has a resolution of 10 ms, so a number reused within the same tick cannot be told apart by it; the pidfd covers that case.
- (1.16.0) **SELinux-enabled RHEL-family hosts (Fedora, RHEL, AlmaLinux, Rocky Linux)**: the unlabeled daemon ran as the generic `init_t`, and SELinux denied reading `authorized_keys` and setuid files, creating canary files and `setpgid`. The daemon kept running, so the SSH key audit, setuid audit, canaries and process freeze silently did not work. The RPM scripts now label the daemon; on Fedora 42 the denials went from 81 to 0.
- (1.16.0) **Decoy (honeytoken) detections reach notifications**: the monitor runs in a separate process and its detections appeared only in the timeline and event forwarding. They now also reach `roamswitch notifications` and Telegram, LINE and the webhook, with the process that opened the decoy (account, executable, command, pid, parent).
- (1.16.0) **Repeated tamper notice for critical paths**: after `useradd` or similar changed passwd, group and shadow, restarting the daemon announced the same "tampering" again. A per-file report no longer clears the notified record; only the full periodic scan and `fim update` do.
- (1.16.0) **Language gaps**: execution-rule (EXR-001 to 013) notifications on the client edition stayed in English with a Japanese setting (the language is now read at every notification; rule descriptions, severities and reverse-shell shape names are in 10 languages). The "detail" line of `audit-agent-setup` findings and the second half of `vuln-accept` failure messages are in 10 languages too. `detail` in `--json`, MCP and SARIF stays English for machines.
- (1.16.0) **`forward status`**: it said "the daemon is not forwarding" although forwarding was enabled. It now says forwarding has not started yet and gives the restart command.
- (1.16.0) **Ransomware freeze misses and duplicates**: file rewrites started right after a copy (`cp -r`) were not detected (the allowlisted-writer check compared creation time with a flat 50 ms margin; it now follows the kernel tick). On a loaded host the same process's "freeze" notice repeated many times; later writes of an already frozen process now count as the same burst and send no notice.
- (1.16.0) **`roamswitch server allowlist test <rule> <name>`**: with a name only it said "protected" although the process would really be killed. It now looks the name up in PATH and `/usr/sbin`, and says it cannot decide when nothing is found (same in the Server TUI, 10 languages).
- (1.16.0) **Added: PHP-FPM in the web-server worker crash notice, and on the client edition**. A pool worker that exits on a crash signal (SIGILL, SIGABRT, SIGBUS, SIGFPE, SIGSEGV, SIGSYS) three times in five minutes is reported (SIGKILL and SIGTERM are not counted). Notice only; nothing is stopped or blocked.
- (1.16.0) **Bundled apps**: AccessScope 0.4.3 and PersonalSOC 0.2.2 (PathScope 0.2.0 unchanged).
- (1.16.1) **Typosquatting check of the package CVE scan**: it reported `@eslint/js` as similar to `ws`. A scoped npm name is compared without its scope (`js` for `@eslint/js`). A two- or three-letter name is one edit away from some popular package, which made false positives, so names of three letters or fewer are no longer compared by edit distance. With several folders registered, a result did not say which folder's `package.json` held the name; the result now carries `project_dir`, and the window, the CLI and the CSV (first column) show the folder.
- (1.16.1) **CSV export**: to prevent CSV injection, a value that starts with `@` got a leading `'`, which also hit scoped npm package names (`@scope/name`). A value that is exactly `@scope/name`, made only of letters, digits and `. _ ~ -`, cannot be read as a formula and no longer gets one. A value that looks like a formula, such as `@SUM(A1)`, still does.
- (1.16.1) **EPSS columns stayed empty on some hosts** (`roamswitch-updater`): a host whose CVE maps were installed before the feeds carried EPSS holds a map without an EPSS date (`epssDate`), so EPSS stayed empty until the next periodic check. When the feed has a newer version and the installed map has no `epssDate`, the updater fetches it on the next daily pass instead of waiting. Once the newer map is installed, this stops.
- (1.16.1) **Changed: the automatic check for CVE data now runs about once a week instead of about every 30 days** (`cve_refresh_due`). The maps are rebuilt once a week on the publishing side, and the check now matches it. The daily manifest check, the threat feed and the web rules are unchanged. The text in the 10-language windows, the knowledge base, the README, the specification, the systemd unit descriptions and the man page were updated to match.
- (1.16.1) **Aliases on `lo`**: a socket listening on an address added to `lo` as an alias (such as `203.0.113.5`) was counted as reachable from the network. An address on `lo` cannot be reached from outside. Wildcard listeners (`0.0.0.0` and `::`) are still counted as reachable. Same idea as the Mac fix in 1.16.1.
- (1.16.1) **Comparing an AI agent's setup with the accepted state did not count a vanished hook as "weakened"** (`audit-agent-setup`, `agent_settings_weakened`): some hooks guard a tool call, so losing one weakens the setup. A hook of the accepted state that is gone with nothing replacing it is now counted and reported as a finding (medium). A hook that was only edited is still "added or changed", as before.
- (1.16.1) **Changed: changes to an AI agent's setup are reported in about 0.4 seconds** (Server Edition, `agent_users`): before, a periodic check every `agent_setup_watch_secs` (300 seconds by default) found a change, so it could take up to that long. The daemon now watches the folders that hold instruction files, skills, MCP definitions and settings with inotify, and compares with the accepted state about 0.4 seconds after the writes settle. `agent_setup_watch_secs` is now the interval of the periodic check that catches what file events miss (0 still turns the whole watch off).
- (1.16.1) **A frozen process could start running again with nobody releasing it** (Server Edition and client edition): the freeze used SIGSTOP, so when the parent shell exited and the process group became orphaned, the kernel sent SIGCONT and the process ran again. A `kill -CONT` did the same. On a host with cgroup v2, the frozen process is now moved into a cgroup of its own, `roamswitch-frozen-<pid>`, and frozen with `cgroup.freeze`. A process in that state does not run whatever signal it receives. The cgroup is a child of the original one, so service and container blocking rules apply as before. The daemon keeps `ProtectControlGroups=yes` and writes the cgroup through a separate unit started with `systemd-run`, running `roamswitch frozen-cgroup` (an internal command). Where cgroup v2 is not usable (cgroup v1, a container with a read-only `/sys/fs/cgroup`, a kernel older than 5.2), a watcher stops a frozen process again every 10 ms if it runs; after a daemon restart it takes over the processes that are still stopped. Release is `roamswitch frozen resume` or `roamswitch emergency-restore`, which remove the watcher and the cgroup first. A release from another process reaches the daemon through a mark on disk.
- (1.17.0) **Fixed: a protection that was switched on but not actually working showed up nowhere, neither on screen nor in a notification** (Client and Server Edition). A needed command was missing, the kernel did not allow it, a watcher process had died, or the log it follows never arrived, so the detection did nothing. The diagnosis and the screens said "enabled", so someone who believed they were protected could not tell. A protection that is not working is now reported with a stable reason code (19 of them, such as `tool_missing`, `worker_not_running`, `no_source`, `log_not_seen` and `no_baseline`) and a sentence in 10 languages. It shows in `roamswitch guards`, the MCP `get_guard_status` (`effectiveState`, `inactiveReason`), `roamswitch detection-coverage`, the diagnosis (`guard_effect:<guard>`), `roamswitch doctor`, the Server TUI defense screen and `roamswitch status --server`. The daemon checks from 2 minutes after start and every 5 minutes, and notifies once per 24 hours for the same guard and reason. The state is in `/run/roamswitch/guard_effect.json` (and `server_runtime.json` on the Server Edition). Facts of the installation (the app is not running, an update failed, off by default on a small host) are not notified.
