- (1.17.0) **Changed: the diagnosis score can drop**: a protection that is not working counts as a failure in the diagnosis. `entropy_ransomware_guard` (a failure while the daemon is stopped; it used to be a fixed pass), `server_xdp_boot_gate` (judged from the unit, the timer and the eBPF object) and `alertChannels` (a new Server Edition with no usable urgent-notification channel fails, which also happens if you run on the TUI alone without notifications) now follow the real state. The first two get an ordinary verdict in `verify_security_findings` instead of "inconclusive".
- (1.17.0) **Fixed: where decoys (honeytokens) are placed, their watcher, and how many were placed**: the client daemon runs as `User=root` with no `SUDO_USER`, so there was a path on which decoys were placed in `/root`. The home is now chosen in this order: the user who used sudo, the user who has the app's `config.json`, the user of a local logind session, the first user under `/home`. `/root` is used only when nothing else exists. The chosen place and the reason are written to `/run/roamswitch/honeytokens.json`. **On a machine with several users, decoys are placed for the one chosen user only.** The decoy watcher process could die without a word; a supervisor now restarts it and the reason it stopped appears in the state (both editions). The Server Edition counts the folders where decoys were placed and shows the number; when a folder could not be written because it lies outside the service's write paths, the message carries the steps.
- (1.17.0) **Fixed: ransomware freeze, port-scan detection, execution record, resource guard and SUID watch**: the diagnosis item `entropy_ransomware_guard` now follows the running state of the fanotify guard, and says so when pre-execution blocking runs in notify-only mode. The port-scan nft rule carries a named counter `syn_logged`; when the counter rises but no line reaches the journal for three windows in a row, it reports `log_not_seen` (the Server Edition port-scan detection has the same watch; the egress, metadata and agent-egress rules do not). `roamswitch exec status` gives the reason when the execution record is disabled or off by default on a small host. With `log_audit_enabled=false`, crash-loop detection does not run even if `resource_guard_enabled` is on, so it is reported as `part_depends_on`. In the SUID watch (Server Edition) the first scan could not finish within 300 seconds, so the baseline was never made. It now waits up to 30 minutes and doubles the wait (up to 4 hours) each time it times out, and reports `no_baseline` with progress until the baseline exists. The warning log is written once a day.
- (1.17.0) **Fixed: container exec guard, DNS tunnel guard and DNS sinkhole** (Server Edition): when the input could not be read, they still showed as running if the setting was on. An unreadable input is now counted. The DNS tunnel guard also counts it as a failure, and retries, when `nft -f -` could not load its table. The DNS sinkhole records the NIC the daemon configured in `/var/lib/roamswitch/dns_sinkhole_applied.json`, and turning it off and restarting restores DNS on that NIC only. A DNS setting someone else made later is left alone. When `resolvectl` is missing, the reason is shown. **A host that set the sinkhole with an earlier version has no record, so DNS is not restored by turning it off until it has been turned on once with this version.**
- (1.17.0) **Fixed: web detection, urgent-notification channels and threats that could not be quarantined** (Server Edition): when the Web Rules have 0 targets, the web access guard follows 0 logs while a web server is running, or the error logs for the web worker crash watch number 0, it warns once at start and reports `no_source`. A stopped Web gate went unnoticed because nginx still passes requests; the gate is now reported and notified when the `ts` in `webgate.status` is older than 60 seconds. The roots and logs being followed are shown by `roamswitch web-rules status`, the Server TUI, `roamswitch doctor` and `status --server`. At start, if no channel can be used or a channel lacks credentials, this is logged and reported as `alertChannels`; a permanent failure is kept in `/var/lib/roamswitch/notify_last_failure.json`. **With no channel, or with a channel that is enabled but has an empty token, destination or URL, an urgent detection reaches nobody and stays only in `journalctl` and `roamswitch notifications`.** Check delivery with `sudo roamswitch server test-notify`. When a file found by YARA or ClamAV could not be quarantined, only a count was logged; the file and the reason are now notified as a detection.
- (1.17.0) **Fixed: Link Guard, shared services, ARP, file scan, `server.conf`, the incident record and AI agent setup**: the Link Guard writes whether it could install its nft table to the state file and shows it in status and MCP. The automatic stop of shared services stops `ssh.socket` first. The GUI and fleet scores use the measured ARP result. The file scan keeps the text of its errors in the log and the state file. Unknown keys and invalid values in `server.conf` are logged at start, and an unreadable file is told apart from one hidden by `server.json`. A hand-edited value does not take effect until a restart, so when one of the 11 keys read at start has changed, "restart required" is shown. A broken incident record looked like "no incidents"; a broken file is now moved to `.bak` before writing, and the reader tells it apart from "no record" (an unreadable file is never overwritten). On the client edition, AI agent setup change detection does not watch continuously and compares only when you run `audit-agent-setup`; the state and the screen text now say so (10 languages).
- (1.17.0) **Added: `roamswitch doctor` checks per feature**: the commands a switched-on feature uses, the update timer and the latest update, mistakes in the config files, places the daemon cannot write (including the homes of `agent_users`), the size of the egress block list, and protections that are not working. The Server Edition also shows the number of Web Rules document roots, the number of logs the web access guard follows, the urgent-notification channels, the `agent_users` accounts and whether they have a baseline, whether the DNS sinkhole is applied, and the allowed UDP ports.
- (1.17.0) **Added: `allowed_udp_ports` to allow incoming UDP ports** (Server Edition, `server.conf`): the default-deny firewall passed only TCP and dropped every UDP packet that was not a reply, so WireGuard, DNS, NTP or HTTP/3 (443/udp) on the same host could not be reached. `allowed_ports` is TCP only. Ports written in `allowed_udp_ports` (comma separated, 1 to 65535) are passed in the input of the same nft table and in the forward that carries DNAT to containers. **The default is empty, and with it empty the nft content is exactly what it was before.**
- (1.17.0) **Changed: `sudo roamswitch server setup` and the after-install message now match what actually happens** (Server Edition): step 2 says only TCP is accepted and UDP goes in `allowed_udp_ports`. Step 7 stops asking about behaviors that cannot work without Falco or Tetragon and shows how to install them. Step 9 says only the directories you name are scanned, and asks for them first. Step 11 lists the protections that work by default from the current settings. **Step 13 (new) asks about the accounts for AI agents (`agent_users`)**; the default answer to the approval is N, and nothing is approved silently. It warns when there is no notification target, or when one is enabled but empty, and prints commands to check the result at the end. The first deb and rpm install says "next, run `sudo roamswitch server setup`" and lists what is not configured yet. **On an upgrade, it enables the signed-data update timer if it is not enabled and `cve_kernel_map_updates_enabled=false` is not set.** A host with `false` stays stopped.
- (1.17.0) **Fixed: guidance of `audit-agent-setup` and `roamswitch xdp-gate enable`**: when `audit-agent-setup` finds nothing, it lists the places it checks, how to make a baseline and what is watched after `--accept` (changes are announced within about a second). `~/.gemini/antigravity-cli/settings.json` and `~/.gemini/config/mcp_config.json` are now audited. `xdp-gate enable` stops without changing anything when the eBPF object (`/usr/lib/roamswitch/ebpf/roamswitch-xdp-gate.o`) is missing, and says the standard package does not carry it. The doctor item `server_xdp_boot_gate` is no longer a fixed pass and is judged from the real state. A pin path used to check the boot gate had a name the loader never creates; it is fixed. **The Server Edition deb and rpm do not carry the `roamswitch-xdp-gate` binary and unit, so on the Server Edition `xdp-gate enable` now always stops and says why.**
- (1.17.0) **Added: a mark on active-check results when the same check was clean last time** (`run_active_vuln_scan`, `previouslySafeAt` in the MCP output): it appears only when the last verdict for the same check on the same port was "no problem". It does not claim that a fix came back; it returns only the fact that is in the log. It has the same form as on the Mac.
- (1.17.0) **What you need to do**: a Server Edition that uses `agent_users` should audit each account with `sudo roamswitch audit-agent-setup --user NAME`, read the result, and make the baseline with `--accept`. Until then, changes in that account are not watched. Decoys cannot be placed in `/home`, so decoys for `agent_users` need `ReadWritePaths=-/home/<account>` in `/etc/systemd/system/roamswitch-server.service.d/agent-users.conf` (step 13 of `server setup` writes it after you confirm). Decoys and file-scan quarantine can write only inside `/var/www`, `/srv`, `/root`, `/opt` and `/etc/roamswitch`; for any other place, run `sudo systemctl edit roamswitch-server` and add `ReadWritePaths=-/path`. After editing `server.conf` by hand, run `sudo roamswitch server restart`. A host that needs incoming UDP must write the ports in `allowed_udp_ports`. Check the document roots watched by web shell detection with `roamswitch web-rules status`, and with 0 write them in `scan_dirs` of `web_rules.conf`.
- (1.17.0) **What was not checked**: nothing was checked on a real systemd, nft or Server host (only tests in Docker and a postinst check with a fake `systemctl`). The same holds for the time of the first SUID scan on a large file system (30 minutes is an unmeasured default), the DNS setting and restoring through `resolvectl`, UDP DNAT to containers on a real Docker, and the added text (translations are based on machine translation and have not been checked by native speakers, other than Japanese). The check that logs arrive for the egress block, metadata watch and agent-egress rules was not added. The after-install message of deb and rpm is in English only, because the shell has no language mechanism.
- (1.17.0) The bundled apps are now AccessScope, PersonalSOC and PathScope, all 1.0.0.
- (1.17.1) **Fix: on a host that runs Docker, the diagnosis warned that IP forwarding was enabled** (`server_ip_forwarding_disabled`). Docker turns `ip_forward` on to route container traffic, but the diagnosis reported it as router behaviour. When the Docker socket (`/var/run/docker.sock`) and a `docker0` or `br-*` network exist, forwarding now passes and the reason "needed by Docker for container traffic" is shown (10 languages). A host without Docker is warned as before.
- **PHP check and session files** (1.17.2): added `roamswitch php-check [--json]` (Client and Server Edition). For the two critical PHP vulnerabilities php.net fixed on 2026-10-21 (CVE-2026-109618 and CVE-2026-109619; fixed in 8.2.35, 8.3.36, 8.4.27 and 8.5.12), it reads, without changing anything, the PHP version (for OS packages, the CVEs in the package changelog), how PHP runs (mod_php, PHP-FPM, FrankenPHP), the settings `session.upload_progress.enabled` and `fatal_error_backtraces`, and the session files. The web rules gained the file type `session` and two rules (`php-session-upload-progress-code`, `php-session-code`), so the bundled rules number 20. The Server Edition watches the session save paths (`session.save_path` and others), and `roamswitch web-scan` without a folder also checks the same paths.
- **Files Python runs at every start** (1.17.2, Client): the autostart watch looks at `.pth` files in `site-packages`, `sitecustomize.py`, `usercustomize.py` and the module a `.pth` file imports (ATT&CK T1546.018). It notifies only about code that reads several kinds of credential stores, decodes data and runs it, downloads content and runs it, or has the shape of a reverse shell; ordinary `.pth` files are only logged.
- **Fixes** (1.17.2): the in-kernel SMB server `ksmbd.service` joined the sharing services stopped on an untrusted network. Shells started by a Node.js service are covered by EXR-001 (`node` and `nodejs` joined the list of service processes). `exec_rule_service_users` added to the default service accounts instead of replacing them, as the settings text says. The setting that refuses unprivileged user namespaces no longer stops systemd units that use `PrivateUsers=` (`colord`, `power-profiles-daemon`). A Server Edition alert now says which host it comes from.
- **A notification for connections to OAST servers, a daily summary of site-structure probing, NXDOMAIN burst detection in the DNS guard, MTA-STS in the domain check, cause links in the timeline** (1.17.3): link protection notifies connections to interact.sh, oast.*, burpcollaborator.net, oastify.com, dnslog.cn and ceye.io (client edition, notification only). `recon_notify` of the Web access guard turns the notifications about probing of the site structure into a daily summary by default (`summary_hour`, 9 by default; Server Edition; blocking, logs and incident records are unchanged). The DNS guard notifies when 40 or more different names under the same domain all return NXDOMAIN within 60 seconds (Server Edition, `dns_tunnel_detect_enabled`, notification only). `domain_check` also checks MTA-STS. Timeline lines gained `parent_event_id`, `root_event_id` and `evidence_refs`, and `roamswitch-incident-capture snapshot --trigger-event-id` links a detection line to its evidence bundle.
- **Changed: the heading of a Server Edition alert now also carries the IP address after the host name** (Telegram, LINE, webhook). The heading is `🚨 [RoamSwitch Server Alert · host name (IP address)] title`. When one chat receives the alerts of several servers, the address tells apart machines even if their names look alike. The address is the first one `hostname -I` returns. If it cannot be read, only the host name is written (no made-up value). If the host name cannot be read but the address can, only the address is written. If neither can be read, the heading is the old one. (1.17.4)
- **USB guard** (1.17.6): in Lockdown, unapproved USB drives and keyboards are held and an approval is asked for, whatever the setting (`usb_storage_guard_enabled`, off by default). Before, unless it was on, a new drive stayed unauthorized with no request and no notification, and even a drive on the allow list could not be used. When you leave Lockdown, held storage devices are authorized. `get_usb_guard_status` reports the guards that are really working (`storageGuardEffective`, `keyboardGuardEffective`) and a note when they are stronger than the setting. The allow list is now one list, `allowed_usb_devices` (the IPC `add_allowed_usb` wrote to a separate list that nothing read, so allowing a device changed nothing). A device is identified by `vendor:product` plus the fingerprint taken at approval (serial number included), so another unit of the same model stays held; a device without a serial number is accepted only with `accept_no_serial`. USB storage is recognized by its kind (interface class 08), not by its name (it was decided only by whether the product name contained `flash`, `disk`, `storage`, `mass` or `cruzer`, so DataTraveler, Ultra and others were not held). The notification for a blocked USB keyboard, which showed only the approval request, is fixed too.
- **ClamAV scan of USB drives, and the Lockdown network** (1.17.6): the diagnosis said "malware on USB drives is scanned automatically", but the implementation was not connected anywhere. It now finds the mount, scans read-only (lowest CPU priority, idle I/O, one at a time, 30-minute limit) and notifies as critical when something is found (it deletes and ejects nothing). If files could not be scanned because of ClamAV's limit (25 MB on Ubuntu), the notification gives their number (ClamAV skips them silently and answers "clean"). It follows `usb_mount_scan_enabled` (on by default) and `clamav_enabled`. The Lockdown firewall dropped IPv6 neighbor discovery and router advertisements, so the host got no SLAAC address and no default route. It now lets through neighbor solicitation and advertisement (hop limit 255) and router advertisements from a link-local source only (other ICMPv6 stays dropped; a fake router advertisement is watched by the network settings monitor). UDP ports in `allowed_udp_ports` now also pass for ports published by Docker (`-p 51820:51820/udp`); before, only the host's input chain let them through and forwarding to containers dropped them.
- **ATT&CK technique IDs, MCP, internal notation in text** (1.17.6): the IDs in detection coverage and the Navigator layer are IDs of v19 or later (`T1562.001` and `T1562.006` are `T1685`, `T1562.004` is `T1686`, `T1562.012` is `T1685.004`, `T1070.002` is `T1685.006`; the log-clearing detection writes the same ID to the timeline; the Navigator layer is v20). If you filter or count by the old IDs, read them as the new ones. MCP `search_exec_events` has a new `chainStatus` (a fixed word, `ok` / `not_chained` / `broken`). The old `chain` (a Rust internal notation) stayed for compatibility, deprecated, and was to be removed in the next version. Internal notation in notifications and command output (`Some(1)`, `exit status: 1`, `Some(1234)`, Rust names such as `LinkGuard`) became sentences about exit code, signal or unknown, and product names; a check now prevents them from coming back.
- **Ransomware entropy watch** (1.17.6): added to the Server Edition (`entropy_watch_enabled`, on by default). It watches places where decoys cannot be put (folders that applications keep data in, document roots): it reads the first 256 KiB of files written in `entropy_watch_dirs` (when empty: web document roots, `/var/www`, `/srv`, and the standard data folders of MariaDB, MySQL, PostgreSQL, MongoDB and Redis) and notifies when one writer writes 20 high-entropy files in 5 seconds (the 4 KiB and 256-byte window checks are included). It also catches "encrypt and delete the original" and the pattern of renaming a temporary file over the original name. The default is notification only; only with `entropy_watch_freeze=true` is the writer frozen with SIGSTOP (`protected_processes` and the system's protected programs are never stopped). It uses no blocking fanotify and reads at most `entropy_watch_max_files_per_sec` files per second (50 by default). Measured (Ubuntu 24.04 VM): a full encryption of 150 files and a replacement of every 2 KiB were detected at the 20th file with one notification; `cp -r`, `tar`, log append and gzip, a SQLite with random BLOBs and `dpkg-deb` gave 0 notifications; at 434 file writes per second it used about 2% of one core. The entropy detection of the client and the Server also covers renaming a temporary file over an existing name (there is no write to the original file, so it was invisible from the write side; it follows `ransomware_allowlist_strict`, jobs that rewrite random files are not counted, and about 30 ordinary rename-over jobs gave 0 detections).
- **Web rules and web shell accounts** (1.17.6): Web rules read more of each file. Only the first few MiB were read, so a web shell appended or placed in the middle of an image or a large file was invisible. Files up to 64 MiB are read in full, larger ones at the head and the tail (the thread runs at lower priority, the amount read is capped, and the write monitor counts what is over the cap in `health.oversize`). The rule scan became 6 to 17 times faster. Locking a web shell's account (`account_action=lock` in `web_rules.conf`) now also stops its outgoing traffic (a running web shell, and a reverse shell it opened, were not stopped by revoking the login shell; for an account that listens on a public address only new outgoing connections are stopped). `account_stop_processes=on` (off by default) stops the account's processes with SIGSTOP, and `unlock-account` restores everything.
- **Auto-start, log clearing, SUID / SGID, network settings, metadata** (1.17.6): `.git/hooks` of working copies are watched as auto-start places (`core.hooksPath` in the global and system git config, submodules and linked worktrees included; searched up to 6 levels under the home, 12 with `git_hook_scan_depth`, hidden folders such as `~/.config` too; a notification comes only when a suspicious line is newly added; a one-liner using zsh `ztcp` is reported as a reverse shell). Log-clearing detection finds rewrites of `wtmp` and `btmp` (even with the same size), edits of `lastlog`, and changes made while the daemon was stopped (up to 256 MiB is remembered as a hash per 1 MiB in `/var/lib/roamswitch/log_tamper_baseline.json`, root-owned, 0600; a lost journal cursor is recorded for a real `rotate` / `vacuum`). SUID / SGID changes are seen at the moment they are made, between scans, with the process that made them; the notification for a change of AI agent settings names the writing process; the exec record and the decoy notification read `/proc` when the event arrives, so processes that end in a millisecond, such as `cat` and `curl`, no longer vanish before they are read. The network settings monitor also sees accepted IPv6 router advertisements, DHCPv6, RDNSS and DHCP option 252 (WPAD), and reports a change of the DNS servers handed out by DHCP while DNS threat protection is on. Connections to the cloud metadata address (`169.254.169.254` and others) are reported with process, PID, parent and command, root included (Falco rule `95-roamswitch-metadata.yaml`). The critical-path file integrity monitor sees a file moved onto a watched name from another folder at once (Server Edition; before, a file built in `/tmp` and moved with `mv` onto `/usr/sbin/sshd` stayed unseen until the periodic scan, about 280 seconds; with `IN_MOVED_TO` it is reported 8 ms after, a same-folder replacement 126 ms after; a move does not tell which process wrote the file, so the "who" says it could not be identified).
- **Diagnosis and data** (1.17.6): package CVE matching shows the CISA KEV mark and "no fixed version" (the Server Edition fetches the signed maps of language dependencies only when both `package_cve_map_languages_enabled`, false by default, and `cve_kernel_map_updates_enabled` are true; about 5 MB, once a week, anonymous receive only; the default outgoing traffic does not grow). The Client Edition has a light periodic check that keeps a history of results (`finding_history_enabled`, on by default: once an hour, no network, lowest priority, kept in `/var/lib/roamswitch/finding_history.json`, so MCP `verify_security_findings` can tell "fixed" from "did not apply from the start"). `audit-url` now checks the on-device threat feed (read only, no network, IP addresses not matched; the Server deb / rpm carry the initial feed). Freezing is stronger: descendants of a frozen process and the parent and sibling workers of the found worker are stopped too, the v1 freezer holds them on cgroup v1 and hybrid hosts, and a repeat detection of a process a person has just released does not redo the Air-Gap, notification and evidence saving.
- **Fixes to the check of protection and to notifications** (1.17.6): the check of whether protection is working reads the settings chosen in the app (with the Fleet overlay applied); the root daemon read `/root` or the defaults, so it did not report protection you had turned off in the app and missed protection that was on but whose dependency was off (`forward status` / `forward test` also reflect the organization's destination, and the language of helper programs started by the daemon follows the app). The "protection is on but not working" notification was repeated at every daemon restart (now held for 24 hours; "nothing to watch" on a host without a web server is not reported as a fault). The client protection-level table (`inet roamswitch`) is rebuilt in about 20 seconds when removed by `nft flush ruleset` or a firewall reload (until then input stayed open on an untrusted network). Port anomaly detection excluded interpreters in `/usr/lib` and `/usr/libexec` (such as OpenJDK's `java`) as system listeners; it now looks at the name, not the place. TLS syslog forwarding checks the certificate and host name before it writes the event (on a slow handshake a failed verification was counted as sent and dropped). The Webhook `content` is cut before Discord's limit (2,000 characters); beyond it Discord answered 400 and the notification was dropped without a retry.
- **Distribution** (1.17.6): the Server Edition carries the `roamswitch(1)` man page, and an Arch Linux tarball and PKGBUILD are placed at `lafine.net/linux/dl/server/` (the files the page pointed to returned 404). The Linux packages carry `pathscope-diff` and `pathscope-fleet`, and the deb and rpm declare, as `Recommends`, the commands the features use (`dig`, `sudo`, `bubblewrap`, `rfkill`, `pkexec`, `resolvectl`). The unused `udisks2` was removed from the client deb's `Depends`. `scripts/check_published.sh` checks after publication that the packages the instructions name are there.
- **php-check and CVE-2026-115560** (1.17.6): `roamswitch php-check` also checked CVE-2026-115560, which has no fix yet (Client and Server Edition; the remote code execution path that PHP announced on 2026-10-23, whose affected versions are not published, so the PHP version cannot decide it). With `session.serialize_handler` set to `php_binary` or `php_serialize`, application code that switches it back to `php` with `ini_set()` before `session_start()`, and `session.upload_progress.enabled` still on, all at once, it gave a High finding (exit code 1); only the configuration or only the code was Info. The code was read from the `.php`, `.phtml` and `.inc` files in `/var/www`, `/srv`, `/usr/share/nginx/html` and `/usr/local/apache2/htdocs` (limit 40,000 files and 2 MiB per file). `php-check` was removed in 1.17.9 and the judgment is part of the periodic diagnosis.
- **Checks and bundled apps** (1.17.6): the workspace tests without the eBPF XDP crate (3835 tests) passed, `cargo audit` showed 0 vulnerabilities and `scripts/audit_no_network.sh` passed. What was checked in a disposable Ubuntu 24.04 virtual machine: USB holding and allowing, the ClamAV scan of a USB drive, IPv6 in Lockdown, the entropy watch, overwriting by `rename`, and the log-clearing detection. The bundled apps were AccessScope 1.0.2, PersonalSOC 1.0.1, PathScope 1.0.1.

### 1.9.39 - 1.9.94 (summarized)

Everything in these releases, grouped by topic (Client and Server Edition unless marked). The full text of each entry is in the git history of this file.

- **Isolation (Air-Gap) reworked** (1.9.90, 1.9.91): an isolation caused by a high-confidence detection (a tampered canary, suspected ransomware, a critical eBPF detection) no longer releases itself on a timer. After a cap (1 hour by default) without a verified-cleared re-check it moves to a "degraded" mode that never re-opens on a timer, and it is released only by `roamswitch emergency-restore`, the GUI, the server's `roamswitch server ack`, or a verified-cleared re-check. Detections prone to false positives still release on the short timer. Connections that were already open no longer survive an isolation (the server keeps only the maintenance SSH and allowed IPs; the client's degraded mode allows loopback only). The ARP re-check sends a direct ARP request when the gateway has no neighbour entry (`arp_recheck_active_probe`), so ARP-triggered isolations stop getting stuck.
- **Process-execution record, forwarding and Server TUI** (1.9.90): a continuous record of which programs ran, with parents, hashes and containers, on the machine only (default 200 MB / 14 days, hash chain), and seven notify-only correlation rules; event forwarding (off by default: syslog, CEF, JSON Lines, HMAC-signed webhook); and a full-coverage TUI for the Server Edition (`roamswitch server tui`). Also `--json`/`--export`, and a notification when another program opens credential files (off by default).
- **Containers and network** (1.9.43, 1.9.44, 1.9.90): the Docker firewall-bypass guard works with Docker's native nftables backend and IPv6 and heals itself within a minute; Container Exec Guard (Server) marks each container's rootfs with fanotify's notify-only `FAN_OPEN_EXEC` and captures even a self-deleting attacker binary; the Egress Guard covers container traffic; per-process CPU-saturation detection; a wider FIM scope (`authorized_keys`, `ld.so.preload`, new files in watched directories).
- **Investigation agent** (Server, 1.9.45 to 1.9.56): automated first-pass triage of eBPF, FIM and lockfile detections (false-positive likelihood, reasons, next commands, Markdown report) and an optional, off-by-default handoff to an agentic CLI (Claude Code, agy, Codex CLI, OpenCode, custom), skipped right before a host isolation; fixes for it never working under root, for the sudo wrapping, and for the recursion where an agent's own investigation looked like a new incident.
- **Security fixes** (1.9.84 to 1.9.90): a local user could send a forged critical event to Falco's socket and trigger a full Air-Gap (the socket is now mode 0600 with a peer-UID check); look-alike names such as `sshd-x` escaped the protected-process list; allowlist entries could be bypassed by renaming a process (entries can be bound to the real executable with `--exe`); Telegram and LINE tokens and webhook URLs were visible in the process list; the port-scan auto-block could be abused with a forged source address (it now refuses only new inbound connections, never blocks the gateway, DNS resolvers or the host's own addresses, and checks the SYNs' source MAC); a flood of forged SYNs could push scan records out of the log (200 per second limit first, and a notification when detection is saturated); a scan claiming the Sensor's address escaped the block (the Sensor's MAC is recorded at pairing); unknown-listening-port detection could be evaded with a system daemon's name (`/tmp/sshd`) or by an interpreter script from a temporary directory; the dev-server block rules were briefly lifted while being re-applied; log-frequency detection went blind after one large burst; the root daemon created root-owned directories under `~/.local`.
- **Port scans, listeners and vulnerability checks** (1.9.39 to 1.9.94): stronger port-scan detection (memory bounded under a flood, a 24-hour window, IPv6 per /64), unknown-listening-port detection for non-wildcard binds and temporary, in-memory or deleted binaries, UDP listeners from interpreters, alerts that name the real program (`wsdd (python3)`); active checks for Elasticsearch, CouchDB, Jenkins, VNC, RDP (NLA) and SMB (SMBv1, signing); CSV export that explains safe and inconclusive results, scan IDs and locked logs; typosquat detection for npm packages (Pro); the kernel-CVE check no longer flags distro backports that only bump the ABI number; the nmap NSE scan is always on and the CVE maps update monthly.
- **RoamSwitch Sensor** (1.9.55 to 1.9.91): pairing moved from mDNS to a pairing code; an active audit can be requested from a Sensor and its result fetched later (MCP tool `get_sensor_audit_results`); TLS 1.3 with certificate pinning (`roamswitch sensor pair --fingerprint`, `sensor repin`; new pairings need Sensor 0.3.9 or later, existing plaintext pairings keep working with a warning); the setup wizard offers a preset when a Sensor runs on the same host; a failed pairing now says why (30 seconds of waiting, likely causes, a command for the Sensor host).
- **Network control review** (1.9.71 to 1.9.72, 1.9.85 to 1.9.87): firewall settings not re-applied right after a daemon restart, services stopped by shared-service control never restored, and a manual security-level change racing the daemon's patrol; ARP false positives triggering Air-Gap under VirtualBox NAT and in NAT environments; the Frag Gap mitigation (denying user namespaces) became opt-in (`auto_enable_userns_restriction`) because it broke browsers, Flatpak, Electron and rootless containers on the client.
- **Notifications, screens and languages** (1.9.40 to 1.9.94): every notification is in ten languages (FIM, lockfile, eBPF, kernel exploit, all Server alerts, the second-instance message, health-check lines), and the Server Edition's language is set with `language`; the notification history no longer freezes (bodies capped at 2,000 characters, newest 200 drawn); "suspected malware" notifications no longer repeat for a file you keep saving; screens split into tabs (package CVE scan, Ports & DevIsolator); the onboarding wizard defaults to standard protection instead of "open"; several GTK layout, CSV-header and translation fixes.
- **Packaging and updates** (1.9.75 to 1.9.88): the periodic nmap NSE database update and the CVE map updates were failing silently in systemd's sandbox or held back by a shared marker; RoamSwitch did not start at boot on some systems; the health check advised apt on Arch-based systems; an administrator password was requested every time Air-Gap was released; and the apt and rpm repositories held every past version until the publishing job failed, so they were cut to the latest version (1.10.5 keeps the latest three).

### 1.0.0 - 1.9.38 (initial release through the stable era, summarized)

- **Initial release (1.0.0)**: ported the macOS edition's zero-trust
  architecture to Linux (systemd + nftables). Followed by the core
  defense capabilities — VPN tunnel + killswitch (WireGuard/Tailscale),
  unknown-port auto-blocking, passive link protection, daily threat feed,
  BadUSB/USB storage authorization, autonomous ARP-spoofing detection
  (1.0.1 - 1.0.66).
- **Official launch of RoamSwitch Server Edition (1.1.0)**: Falco eBPF
  runtime threat detection & auto-isolation, Critical Path FIM, a 25-item
  server security health check, Webhook/PagerDuty alerts. Followed by
  event-driven FIM, Egress/C2 containment, Tetragon sensor support, and
  fine-grained `guard.yaml` policy configuration (1.1.1 - 1.3.2).
- **Package CVE Scan (1.5.0)**, **Log Audit template anomaly detection +
  automatic secret masking (1.6.0)**, **incident history for Air-Gap
  trigger guards exposed via MCP/CLI (1.7.0)**, **File Scan Guard &
  Docker risk detection (1.4.1 - 1.8.0)**.
- **Evil-Twin SSID warning, BadUSB keystroke-timing anomaly detection,
  Ransomware Canary blast-radius evidence (1.9.0 - 1.9.5)**; **notification
  history, ClickFix detection, and the log audit's background schedule
  added to the client edition (1.9.6 - 1.9.15)**.
- **Critical Path FIM extended to the client edition, Resource Exhaustion
  / Process Anomaly Guard (Server Edition), a root-cause fix for Log
  Audit's masking regex, and fixes for several Server Edition
  success-without-verification bugs (1.9.16 - 1.9.26)**.
- **Four npm-install supply-chain-risk features (Lockfile FIM,
  install-script inventory, npm signature verification, bubblewrap
  sandboxing), typosquat detection, crypto-wallet seed-phrase leak
  detection, and a critical fix for the Default-Deny firewall blocking
  100% of Docker containers' outbound traffic (1.9.27 - 1.9.38)**.

---

## RoamSwitch for Mac

## 1.17.11

- **Added: the diagnosis item "Vulnerabilities likely to be exploited (installed software)" is checked every hour, and a notification is sent when it newly fails** (the first check is 5 minutes after launch). It notifies once when a new vulnerability is added, or when 24 hours pass with the same contents. Pro gets a push notification; on Free it stays in the notification history. Nothing is sent when an update brings the item back to pass, or when it could not be measured. What was installed with Homebrew can be updated with `brew upgrade`. There is no switch in the settings.
- Note: the Mac already ran a diagnosis every four hours, but it notified only when the score dropped, and nothing was notified when this item alone failed.
- Checks: all 2665 tests of the Mac app pass.

## 1.17.10

- **Added: `run_active_vuln_scan` takes a `format` argument** (`json` by default, or `sarif`). It returns SARIF 2.1.0 in the same form as the Linux edition. The rule ID is the probe name, and the log carries `evidenceKind`, the checks that could not be made (`inconclusive`) and whether the scan was complete (`complete`). A rule that SARIF rule IDs are never renamed is written down, with a test that fails when a published ID is removed or renamed.
- **Added: the dependency source scan (the "Dependency sources (Pro)" tab and MCP `run_dependency_source_scan`) reports `release_age_gate_missing`.** For a project with an npm, pnpm, yarn Berry, bun or uv lockfile, it reports with Low severity when neither the project nor the home folder sets a rule that keeps out versions published a moment ago. The setting names and the way of reading them are the same as in the Linux edition (npm `min-release-age` or `before`, pnpm `minimumReleaseAge`, yarn Berry `npmMinimalAgeGate`, bun `minimumReleaseAge`, uv `exclude-newer`). It does not say that a bad package is installed. pnpm 11 and later is on by default, so it is not reported. Environment variables and settings elsewhere are not read. It uses no network (10 languages).
- **Added: `get_incident_timeline` returns `incidents` (events put together per incident) and `openIncidentCount`.** Events in the same chain of cause, or about the same process within 15 minutes, become one incident. `events` stays the record, and the grouping is not proof of one attack. A saved evidence bundle and an expired risk acceptance do not count as detections, and an incident made only of them is `recorded`. Values are spelled in camelCase, as in `events`.
- **Fixed: when ClamAV exits with code 2 and checks nothing, the app now shows a failure instead of "No threats found (0 files)"** (10 languages). This covers a signature database that cannot be loaded and the like. Homebrew's ClamAV 1.5.4 combined with OpenSSL 4.1.0 cannot load any signature database, so it checks 0 files (a fix is under way in the upstream pull request #1873 of Cisco-Talos/clamav). Before, the app showed "No threats found" in this state too.
- **Fixed: the probe `webvuln-xss` was missing from `probesRun` of `run_active_vuln_scan`.**
- Checks: the full Mac app tests (2651 tests) pass, with 0 skipped.

## 1.17.9

- **Changed: the "Check PHP settings" button in the Web rules settings is removed** (added in 1.17.1). The PHP judgment is now part of a new item of the diagnosis.
- **Changed: the text of the diagnosis item "Automatic security updates" now matches what is checked.** The item looks at whether Software Update's automatic check is on. It does not check whether macOS itself is installed automatically. It used to say "latest patches applied automatically" and "the configuration is optimal" (10 languages).
- **Added: the diagnosis has a new item, "Vulnerabilities likely to be exploited (installed software)"** (22 to 23 items). It compares the packages installed with Homebrew with the known-CVE data on this Mac. It fails when a vulnerability that CISA has confirmed as exploited (KEV), or whose probability of exploitation (EPSS) is 0.9 or higher, is still unfixed. PHP installed outside Homebrew is checked by reading its version and configuration directly. It uses no network.
- **Added: a signed KEV list (about 30 KB) is fetched every day and added to the KEV marks in the data.**
- **Fixed: the generation of the Homebrew vulnerability data did not cover php, php@8.x, tailscale, node, nginx, openssl, python, postgresql, brotli and about 25 other packages.** They are now covered (the data itself is published separately).

## 1.10.0 - 1.17.8 (summarized)

The contents of these releases, grouped by theme. The full text of each entry is in this file's git history.

- **Bundled app update** (1.17.8): updated to AccessScope 1.0.3, PersonalSOC 1.0.2 and PathScope 1.0.2 (see the entry of each app). PathScope.app and PersonalSOC.app are in the DMG. AccessScope can also be updated from "Update" in the settings of its window app. The features of RoamSwitch itself were unchanged from 1.17.6; the version was aligned with the Linux edition.
- **Bundled app update** (1.17.5): AccessScope is now 1.0.1. There are fewer false High and Medium findings on a site that answers every URL with the same page, and the IPs and domains in the LLM's text are written with plain dots. The window app can also be updated from AccessScope's own settings ("Update"). The features of RoamSwitch itself were unchanged from 1.17.4; the version matched the Linux edition. Bundled: AccessScope 1.0.1, PersonalSOC 1.0.0, PathScope 1.0.0.
- **Bundled apps** (1.11.0 to 1.11.2): 1.11.0 bundled AccessScope 0.2.0 and PersonalSOC 0.1.9, 1.11.1 AccessScope 0.3.0 and PathScope 0.1.7, and 1.11.2 AccessScope 0.3.1. AccessScope's detections became larger, stronger and quieter, and it reads more kinds of logs (0.2.0); it now finds the behavior of the same address across several logs, logins from an unfamiliar source and differences from the usual, and the "Diagnose logs" screen became easier to follow (0.3.0); 0.3.1 unified the Korean and Chinese terms for "severity" and "confidence" in the screens and reports. PathScope refreshed its known-vulnerability data (0.1.7). PersonalSOC gained the import of known vulnerabilities in dependency packages and a ledger of confirmed facts (0.1.9). See each app's entry. RoamSwitch itself arrives by the in-app update (Sparkle), and the bundled apps can be updated from inside each app (Settings → Update).
- **MCP tools and the config audit** (1.11.0 to 1.11.1): added the MCP tool `verify_security_findings` (31 MCP tools in all). It re-evaluates the items of `get_security_report` (18 checks) in their current state and returns, per item, still present (stillPresent), fixed (resolved) or cannot confirm (inconclusive), with a language-independent reason; it is read-only, and is meant for checking that something is really fixed. `checkIds` takes at most 50, and omitting it or an empty array means all items. An item that could not be evaluated or is not reliable becomes inconclusive and is never treated as safe. ARP becomes fixed only when it matches the gateway MAC of a registered trusted network. For the host firewall, stealth mode, gateway ARP pinning, malware protection, DNS and USB items, the result notes that they reflect the app's settings and do not re-measure the real OS state. Each item of `get_security_report` carries `inconclusiveReason` only when needed. `get_guard_status` gained port-scan detection and lock-file tamper monitoring (28 items). The MCP config audit (`audit_mcp_configs` and the screen "Audit MCP settings (AI integration)…" in the Malware Protection menu) checks the MCP config files of AI tools for free, without any network access and without printing secret values (auto-approve, plain http, shell one-liner launch, a connection to HexStrike, writable configs, `enableAllProjectMcpServers`, launchers without a pinned version, container host privileges). It also fixed the detail showing "0 tool(s)" when `autoApprove: true`. In 1.11.1 a non-loopback `http://` is also flagged as plain HTTP (`remote_plain_http`) when the destination is passed as a launch argument such as `--server http://host` or `--url=http://host` instead of the `url` field (as in the HexStrike AI sample config); that form was not flagged before. Loopback and `https://` are out of scope, and an `http://` inside a shell or interpreter one-liner is not a server address, so only the one-liner finding is shown, as before. One server gets one finding even if both the `url` field and an argument carry it, and a URL with credentials shows only the host name. The MCP skill `closing-out-roamswitch-findings` was added too (it ends each finding as confirmed, denied with a reason, or undetermined, and lowers the severity without deleting the finding even when there are mitigating circumstances).
- **Diagnosis fixes** (1.11.0): the diagnosis reported "enabled" or "disabled" without having measured anything. `fdesetup`, `csrutil`, `spctl` and `softwareupdate` were judged enabled or disabled even when they returned an error message or empty output; now a verdict is made only when the exit code and the output exactly match a real machine, and anything else is "could not be measured" (SIP's `enabled (Custom Configuration)` counts as partially enabled). A failing `lsof` was also judged "no public ports"; "no listeners" now requires exit code 1 with no output, and anything else is a failure (standard output and standard error are read at the same time so it cannot hang). Items that could not be measured are removed from the score and the failure count, and the app's own view and the dashboard show "not confirmed" instead of "disabled" (when the port list has not been measured yet it is shown apart from an `lsof` failure, and the menu shows a stale list as stale). The diagnosis through MCP read the MCP process's own settings (UserDefaults) instead of the app's (`malware_scanning`, `dns_threat_guard`, `usb_zero_trust`); fixed. `sshd_config` is parsed by its real rules (`ssh_hardening`: `Include`, the first value wins, everything after `Match` is out of scope). The MCP tool descriptions now match the real network behavior (the flat claim "does not touch the network" was corrected: the diagnosis may send an ICMP ping into the LAN to find the gateway MAC, normally once and up to 3 times when the MAC cannot be resolved; `get_exposed_ports` sends one HTTP GET to 127.0.0.1 per public port; the only tool that contacts an outside host is `run_npm_audit_signatures`; the MCP knowledge base (10 languages) was updated too).
- **New and stronger detections** (1.11.0): the active vulnerability check gained detection of an API that accepts execution by AI without authentication (HexStrike AI, `hexstrike-noauth`); it sends a single `GET /api/processes/list` to a Python listener, and since that applies to every Python listener it is not shown by the passive diagnosis. The process execution log gained two rules that find chains of attack tools (notification only): when one process looks up 8 or more kinds of attack tools with `which` or similar within 30 seconds (`exec.offensive_tool_inventory`), and when a script runtime or an AI agent starts 3 or more kinds within 2 minutes (`exec.offensive_tool_burst`); a person typing in a terminal is excluded. Link guard treats numeric hosts such as `2130706433` or `0x7f.1` as a direct IP, and also detects URLs that put a fake destination before an `@`, such as `https://apple.com@evil.example/`. Listener detection no longer reports the interception proxy Caido when it listens on loopback only, reports it when it is reachable from the network, and knows the MCP Inspector ports (6274 and 6277).
- **Detection rules for web shells and injected code (Web Rules)** (1.11.11): finds PHP, JavaScript, JSP and ASP web shells and injected code in files of local web development (`~/Sites`, MAMP, Herd, Local, `/Library/WebServer/Documents`, and the project folders registered for package-CVE scanning). The 18 rules are shared with the Linux edition; none is extracted from a malware sample and they describe publicly documented techniques only. The rules come in three layers: built into the app, delivered once a day by the signed receive-only update, and your own files in `~/Library/Application Support/RoamSwitch/rules.d/*.json` (custom rules are read only from files owned by root or you that group and others cannot write). Quarantine is `critical` only by default. `high` findings leave the file alone and show up as a notification and in a list in Settings (with "do not detect again"). The level can be set in Settings to "do not quarantine", "critical only" or "critical and high", and the list of restored files that are not detected again is separate from the ClamAV exclusions. In folders the app already watches (for example Downloads), detection is right after a write. Web development folders are checked once a day and with "Check the web development folders now" in Settings (the daily check skips `node_modules` and `.git` for time; a check of a folder you pick does enter `node_modules`). The Mac cannot stop anything before it runs (as before, all detection is after the fact). A post-detection hook: an executable at `~/Library/Application Support/RoamSwitch/hooks/post-detection` is called with the hits as JSON on standard input (only a file owned by root or you that nobody else can write runs; it is stopped after 30 seconds). An account response (off by default) needs the administrator password to turn on; it revokes the login shell of the owner of a quarantined file, but never touches root, UIDs below 501, or anyone who is logged in (the previous shell is recorded and can be restored in Settings). 0 findings in 114,501 files of a development folder. Verified in a macOS 26 virtual machine, as root under the helper's own sandbox: the real dscl shell change and restore, and the exclusion of the logged-in user, root and low UIDs; the Settings screen was checked in the same virtual machine, which also found and fixed a defect where the screen could not be opened without ClamAV installed (the Malware Protection menu now always shows "Web shell detection rule settings…"). Not verified: the administrator-password confirmation and the app-to-helper call path (they need a signed helper). The new text is in 9 languages (machine-translated; not reviewed by native speakers). Linux-specific changes (`kernel-modules`, `bluetooth-audit`, write monitoring in the Server Edition and so on) are in Linux 1.11.23.
- **The MCP configuration audit has an item for environment variables** (1.11.11, `code_loading_env`): it reports `LD_PRELOAD`, `DYLD_INSERT_LIBRARIES`, `BASH_ENV` and `PYTHONSTARTUP` in `env`. For `NODE_OPTIONS` and the Java-family variables, only when the value has a code-loading option such as `--require` or `-javaagent`. Variable names are matched case-insensitively, and values are not shown in the result (10 languages).
- **Default protections with Pro** (1.11.0): fixed protections that should have turned on with Pro but did not. Inbound port-scan detection was not wired to turn on by default when Pro is activated, and it now is (the source is blocked automatically for 10 minutes only; the gateway, DNS and this Mac itself are never blocked; only the blocking can be turned off in the menu). Deactivating and re-activating the license did not bring the default "on" back; fixed (settings you turned off yourself are respected; on a Mac that had already deactivated once, the "off" left by an older version cannot be repaired automatically, so turn it on once from the menu). The free edition also ran web and mail protection and the autostart monitor and showed the folder permission dialog; those two now run only with Pro. The protections that are on by default with Pro are: automatic blocking of unknown ports, inbound port-scan detection, ransomware decoys and entropy detection, credential honeytokens, automatic log audit, tamper monitoring of important files and lock files, and automatic blocking on ARP spoof and XProtect link. ClickFix, Docker, USB, Bluetooth, gateway pinning, process execution log, browser credential monitoring, VPN and Sensor stay off by default because they need permissions or settings, or could interrupt normal use.
- **Wording about network use** (1.11.0): the in-app "Zero Telemetry" wording flatly said "nothing is sent to an external server" and "no data is sent at all", although the minimum necessary connections are made (license activation, receiving update data, expanding shortened URLs). The security policy in the overview, the link safety diagnosis sheet, the help (10 languages) and the knowledge base now say that there is no telemetry that sends usage or diagnosis results, and that the necessary connections are published in the design document. "No other tab connects at all" on the package CVE match screen now says that the matching itself does not connect. It is also stated that expanding a shortened URL in the link safety diagnosis accesses the target URL directly. The description of the update frequency of the package CVE map and the vulnerability diagnosis CVE map was corrected too (the expiry is checked daily and the data is fetched only about once every 30 days; the knowledge base and help (10 languages) said "once a day"; the link protection threat feed is still daily).
- **AccessScope bundled** (1.10.28): AccessScope 0.1.0 is now in the DMG, next to RoamSwitch.app. A separate app that finds attack attempts in web server and SSH access logs and shows the evidence (see AccessScope 0.1.0). You can update it from inside the app (Settings → Update).
- **MCP security report** (1.10.28): fixed the MCP `get_security_report` always reporting the gateway ARP lock as "disabled", even when it was on. The report was generated without the ARP lock setting. `get_guard_status` already returned the correct value.
- **Links to the product page and others** (1.10.28): the links to the purchase page, help and the MCP knowledge base inside the app now point to roamswitch.com.
- **Bundled PathScope** (1.10.22 to 1.10.26): Even with "unrestricted", private key files and API keys are no longer sent (see PathScope 0.1.2). 1.10.23 fixes one German label (0.1.3). 1.10.24 fixes the update section text and the unreadable drop-down text on Linux, among other things (0.1.4; the Mac screens hardly change). If 0.1.0 or later is already installed, you can update from inside the app (Settings → Update). 1.10.25 adds checking installed software versions against known vulnerabilities (0.1.5). In 1.10.26, an explanation is added when PathScope itself is the subject of a finding (0.1.6). 1.11.11 bundles PathScope 0.1.8 (it adds CVEs with a high EPSS and keeps them; see the PathScope 0.1.8 entry).
- **False ransomware detection** (1.10.26): Fixed a false ransomware detection when copying many compressed files. When a lot of files were copied, already-compressed files such as `.gz` and `.jpg` (which have high entropy by nature) were counted as "encrypted file writes", and this could even trigger the full network cut-off (air-gap isolation), naming Spotlight's indexer as the culprit. A file whose first bytes are a genuine compressed or media format (gzip, zip, JPEG, PNG, mp4 and so on) is now left out of the check. Encryption destroys those first bytes, so detection of real encryption is not weakened. Folders such as `.venv`, `node_modules` and `.git` are skipped, and Spotlight's worker processes (`mdworker_shared` and the like) and any process started from macOS's own locations (`/System`, `/usr`) are treated as safe.
- **Tailscale reconnect loop** (1.10.26): Fixed the exit node reconnecting and disconnecting about every 40 seconds after a Tailscale disconnect. While the network service reconnected after the disconnect, the gateway's MAC address could not be read for about 40 seconds; the 30-second grace period ran out first and the network was judged to be an unknown one (Lockdown). So even at a Standard-protection home network, it briefly went to Lockdown and the VPN connected, then disconnected when the MAC came back, and reconnected again, with traffic stopping on and off. The grace period is now 90 seconds and ends as soon as the gateway is back.
- **App quitting after an isolation release** (1.10.26): Added protection against the app quitting after an isolation is released. When the app reached its limit of open files (256), starting ClamAV raised an exception and the app quit. The crash report does not prove that this limit was the cause. The limit is now raised, and the pipe for ClamAV's output is always closed, even when the launch fails.
- **Privileged helper hardening** (1.10.5): the helper now runs inside a sandbox from launch and self-checks it. It only talks to the genuine app, and Mach-O, `eslogger`, `tcpdump` and Sensor traffic are parsed outside root. Snapshot restore runs with the user's own privileges.
- **Quarantine and port scans** (1.10.5, 1.10.6): ARP-spoofing quarantine is lifted automatically once the cause is confirmed gone. Fixed quarantine release trusting a spoofed gateway and ARP pinning locking in a forged MAC. Automatic port-scan blocking now verifies the source MAC, and IPv6 scans are detected. A manual quarantine that failed no longer keeps showing "quarantined".
- **Ransomware defense and recovery** (1.10.0 to 1.10.8): added generic entropy-based detection and reworked decoy files so they no longer break real tools. "Ransomware Recovery" lets you pick deleted files from snapshot contents, and detects deletion of recovery snapshots by a third party. Added a separate "Recovery and Uninstall" menu.
- **Tailscale and VPN** (1.10.1 to 1.10.5): the Exit Node is now checked against the real OS route rather than tailscaled's self-report. VPN tool upgrades are announced, and a pinned folder with files outside the manifest is rejected.
- **Link guard and download guard** (1.10.1): fixed brand-impersonation (homograph) detection that never worked for Apple and PayPal.
- **Notifications and UI** (1.10.0 to 1.10.9): identical notifications stay silent for 10 minutes (history keeps them all). Fixed a sound with no banner. Added explanations and confirmations when toggling features, showed block state in the exposed-port list, and separated "cannot verify" from "failed" in the overall diagnosis.
- **Security fixes** (1.10.4, 1.10.5): Sensor control traffic never falls back to plaintext. The license signing key was rotated (both old and new public keys are accepted). Out-of-range settings (ports, block durations) can no longer break firewall rule rebuilds.
- **Privileged-helper resource leak and busy loop** (1.10.10, 1.10.11): fixed pipes for external-command output not being closed, so open file descriptors reached the limit (256) and the app showed "helper not connected"; the firewall and DNS being rewritten roughly every 2 seconds; the helper possibly stalling on large output; and the gateway pinning being deleted and re-added on every re-evaluation even when already pinned.
- **NDP pinning and diagnostics** (1.10.10, 1.10.11): pinning the IPv6 router's NDP entry did not actually work (`ndp -s` without an interface returns exit code 0 but does not pin). It now pins with `fe80::…%en0` and verifies afterwards. Also fixed: critical-file tamper monitoring reporting "deleted" for a file that was merely unreadable for a moment because of a race, and the ordering of the "proven vulnerability check" status.
- **Activity log and false-positive audit** (1.10.12): a Security Activity Log screen to search and filter past detections. An alert-fatigue audit fixed 27 findings: the ransomware emergency-containment banner now names the affected file and the suspected process, ClickFix protection only notifies when a command matches a known installer's pattern, the risky-Docker-container notification has a 24-hour cooldown, link protection states its triggering signal, and the Pickle-format AI model download warning shows where the file came from.
- **PersonalSOC bundled** (1.10.13): a separate app that gathers this machine's logs and defense status read-only into a report is now bundled in the DMG. 10 languages, Apache-2.0. Its LLM integration is optional and off by default. It is not updated by the in-app updater (Sparkle) but by a new DMG (from 0.1.1 it also has its own updater).
- **PersonalSOC in-app updater and more** (1.10.14): PersonalSOC got an in-app updater (Mac): it fetches the latest version information only when you press "Check for updates" in Settings, and verifies the signature before installing. The fixed "Response" section was removed from the reports, and chart legends and axis labels that overlapped in English and other languages were fixed.
- **Link guard and threat feed** (1.10.17): hostnames with a trailing dot (`evil.com.`) no longer slip past (they are normalized before the decision). The privileged helper now verifies the threat feed's signature before publishing it; before, a program running as the same user could empty the feed to disable the link guard or add hosts. When verification fails the previous feed is kept and protection is not removed, and a swap to a feed older than the recorded manifest is refused. A TLS ClientHello split across several records is now inspected up to 16 KB.
- **Quarantine and malware scanning** (1.10.17): a scanned file name containing line breaks and a fake detection line could make RoamSwitch quarantine an arbitrary user file; only the detected path is now scanned again before quarantine. Scans no longer hang on large output.
- **Secret masking in the execution record** (1.10.17 to 1.10.18): MCP execution-record search runs on the masked content, so secrets in command lines cannot be guessed from search hits, and the app itself applies output masking and the search rate limit. Masking now covers API keys such as `sk-svcacct-`, GitHub, GitLab, npm, Slack and Google tokens, `mysql -ppassword`, `DB_PASS=` and more. In 1.10.18 wrappers such as `sudo`, `env` and `nohup`, interpreters such as `sh`, `python`, `ruby`, `perl` and `node`, and the contents of `sh -c '…'` are seen through so the real command's rules apply. The integrity chain detects rewriting of a sealed segment and deletion of the oldest segment.
- **Data protection and state saving** (1.10.17 to 1.10.18): state saving (kill switch, port protection, Sensor pairing and more) is atomic, so an interruption cannot restore a state with the kill switch off. Fixed the stored endpoint being overwritten when a WireGuard import failed, and traffic staying blocked when `wg-quick up` failed and the previous kill switch was not restored. When the trusted-Sensor list cannot be read, other pairings are no longer erased by overwriting with an empty list; a copy is kept in `trusted_sensors.json.corrupt-<time>` (0600, latest 3) and the operation fails with a clear error (shown in 10 languages).
- **Working with Sensor** (1.10.17 to 1.10.18): pairing sends a proof of key possession (PoP). The control signature version is remembered at the highest confirmed level and is not downgraded on connections without a pinned certificate. The reason "Sensor's own storage file is corrupt, so pairing is impossible" returned by Sensor 0.3.15 is shown in 10 languages. The calmer warning during a Sensor audit returns to the normal warning once the report arrives (1.10.16).
- **Other fixes** (1.10.17): SSH remote-login state detection, DNS restore (disabled services and the like), resuming port-scan detection, and symlink handling in the process-execution record's plist check.
- **Decoys and notifications** (1.10.15): added `~/.pypirc` and `~/.env.backup` as decoys (honeytokens). A real access to a decoy places and watches one related decoy (the chain stops after one step). The process-execution record (Pro) gained a rule that detects commands naming a decoy file directly (11 rules). Log-audit notifications are limited to security-relevant anomalies such as authentication failures and XProtect detections. Warnings in the roughly 25 minutes after requesting a Sensor audit use calmer wording. Full Disk Access guidance was unified (the permission is decided by "RoamSwitch" in the System Settings list).
- **Bundled PersonalSOC** (1.10.15 to 1.10.27): 0.1.2 to 0.1.8 in turn (see each PersonalSOC release). In 1.10.27, the details of a CVE (known vulnerability) finding began to show the package name and version (0.1.8). In 1.10.21, the unreadable drop-down text on Linux was fixed (0.1.7; the Mac screens do not change). In 1.10.19, Settings is split into tabs by function, the message after a report is created is shorter, and the white margin around the app icon is gone (0.1.6).
- **Bundled PathScope** (1.10.19 to 1.10.20): 0.1.0 and 0.1.1 (see each PathScope release). In 1.10.20, 0.1.1 added reading config files with an LLM to find risks, 10 languages in the app, and in-app updates on Linux.
- **Bundled PersonalSOC** (1.11.4): PersonalSOC 0.1.10 is now bundled. From the command line, `personalsoc consent` sets consent to automatic log collection (see PersonalSOC 0.1.10). PersonalSOC can update itself from inside the app (Settings > Update). RoamSwitch itself reaches your Mac through the in-app update (Sparkle). There are no other changes.
- **Network isolation (Air-Gap) guide screen** (1.11.3): the "ℹ️ Why you're isolated & how to release…" screen gained a button to the "First Hour After an Incident" page ("See the first hour after an incident"). Pressing it opens the page in the app's display language (10 languages) in your browser. The page explains the order of checking, preserving, deciding, releasing and watching, for the Mac, the Linux desktop edition and the Linux Server Edition. It opens only when you press the button; RoamSwitch never connects to this page by itself. There are no other changes.
- **Four weaknesses of the privileged helper fixed** (1.11.5): snapshot restore no longer reads and hands over, as root, anything other than the user's own files. An ordinary user can create a hard link in their home to a file only root can read (for example `/private/etc/master.passwd`), and `O_NOFOLLOW` does not stop a hard link, so restoring from a later snapshot copied what root had opened into the user's folder; a file whose owner is not the console user is now refused. Unmounting a snapshot-browse mount could unmount an unrelated mount through a name containing links or `..`; the name is now matched exactly and checked as a real root-owned directory before unmounting. Pinning VPN tools (copying a tool from a user-writable place into a root-only place to run as root), uninstalling, and changing Sensor trust now require the administrator password (releasing quarantine does not, because it lifts automatic containment when nobody is present). The paths that succeed after entering the administrator password need on-screen interaction and were not checked on a real Mac. New text was added in 9 languages by machine translation.
- **Bundled apps** (1.11.5): AccessScope 0.3.2 (tidier terminal output) and PersonalSOC 0.1.11 (the "consent" to reading logs automatically was removed). The button name in the Air-Gap guidance screen was aligned to "View the first hour after an incident".
- **Bundled apps** (1.11.6): PersonalSOC 0.1.12 (a `sudo` example was added to the message shown when logs cannot be read). PersonalSOC can be updated from inside the app (Settings, "Update"). There are no other changes.
- **URL audit: shortened URLs and look-alike domains, and untranslated text**(1.11.7): Fixed: shortened URLs and domains that add words like "secure" or "login" to a brand name were judged safe. The URL audit now shows a notice, "Shortened URL (the destination is not visible)", for links such as `bit.ly` (penalty 25). A domain such as `paypa1-secure-login` or `microsoft-security-alert`, which adds words like "secure" or "login" to a brand name (including one written with digits or look-alike characters), is judged a severe risk (penalty 45). Official domains and names without a brand stay safe. What the link guard blocks or warns about does not change. Fixed: some text stayed in English after changing the language. 13 items (the Wi-Fi and ARP monitor states, the protection level names Maximum Lockdown, Balanced and Trusted, and the rename prompt) were English in German, Spanish, French, Italian, Portuguese and Korean (Simplified and Traditional Chinese: the three protection level names). They are now translated. These translations are based on machine translation and have not been checked by native speakers. The package vulnerability check (Homebrew) does not cover Ubuntu packages, so the Linux fix does not apply.
- **Bundled AccessScope 0.3.3**(1.11.8): The bundled AccessScope is now 0.3.3 (it gained detection of clients that behave very differently from the other visitors; see the AccessScope 0.3.3 entry). AccessScope can be updated from inside the app (Settings, "Update"). RoamSwitch itself reaches your Mac through the in-app updater (Sparkle). There are no other changes.
- **Bundled apps and the link protection seed data** (1.11.9): the bundled AccessScope is now 0.3.4 (the command line can run LLM analysis as root too, and the terminal output is now Markdown tables; the window app still does not run LLM analysis as root). The source of the link protection's bundled seed data changed. It used to be host names (about 280) from OpenPhish's free feed; to match OpenPhish's terms of use (commercial use and passing the data to third parties need its written consent), OpenPhish is no longer used, and the seed is now 2,000 randomly chosen currently active phishing domains from Phishing.Database (MIT licence). The seed only bridges the time until the feed's first update. The full threat feed received with the daily update never contained OpenPhish, so it is unchanged.
- (1.11.10) **New: the "Why you're isolated & how to release" window now shows a QR code for the first-hour guide page.** While the Mac is isolated, it cannot open the guide. A phone on its own connection can, so the window shows a QR code and one line, "Scan this QR code with your phone to read the guide on another connection." (10 languages), next to the link. The code holds only the public, fixed address (the page in the app's language): no host name and no query string. Nothing is sent over the network, and it uses macOS's built-in CoreImage, so there is no new component. It is drawn on white even in dark mode. I checked that the address in each of the 10 languages reads back from its QR code, and that the view, drawn to an image in both light and dark mode, can be read.
- (1.11.10) Linux (Ubuntu and others) gets the same display (Linux 1.11.20).
- (1.11.12) **Changed: the clipboard watch now checks every 0.1 s instead of every second.** When you copy a ClickFix-style malicious command, the app clears it from the clipboard, but with a once-a-second check it could be pasted until then. The check now runs every 0.1 s, and also while a menu is open. I did not measure the time from copy to clear on a real Mac (only the build and `ClickFixGuardTests` passing).
- (1.11.12) **Improved: the dependency CVE scan now says which project (repository) each finding comes from.** The scan walks the folders you point it at, but the results did not say which project a finding belonged to, so you could not tell which repository it was about. The results gained a "Project" column (the folder of the lockfile and the lockfile's name), and the first column of the CSV and the result of the MCP tool `run_package_cve_scan_languages` now carry the lockfile's path. Results are grouped by project; the same package in two projects is shown as two findings (10 languages).
- (1.11.12) **Changed: the "suspicious Terminal command" detection now waits less for changes to the shell history file** (the FSEvents latency from 1.0 s to 0.1 s, and the wait that merges consecutive changes from 0.5 s to 0.1 s). This detection reads the history after the command has run; it does not clear the clipboard.
- (1.11.12) Linux (Ubuntu and others) gets the same review (Linux 1.11.26).
- (1.11.14) **Changed: the bundled AccessScope is now 0.3.6 and PersonalSOC 0.1.13.** AccessScope includes the automatic decision of the real client for logs behind a reverse proxy, the tidying of chains and the fix for wrong web shell verdicts. PersonalSOC includes the new web log sources on Mac (the Apache that ships with macOS, and nginx and httpd from Homebrew). Both also arrive through the in-app update. There is no change to RoamSwitch's own features.
- (1.11.13) **Added: local AI service diagnosis, AI agent setup audit, an SSRF check in the active scan, and blocking of traffic with the 3 attack-server IPs published by JPCERT/CC using pf.** The AI services (Ollama, Jupyter Server, MLflow, Gradio) get one read-only GET to the listener on 127.0.0.1 and are reported only when reachable without authentication. The AI agent setup audit (MCP tool `audit_agent_setup`) checks instruction files, skills and OpenClaw settings read-only. The attack-server block puts its rules right after the anchor call and reads them back with `pfctl -sr` after applying (the read-back as root on a real Mac was not checked).
- (1.11.13) **Updated: the bundled AccessScope is now 0.3.5** (detection of XSS in headers and OAST call-back targets).
- (1.11.13) Linux (Ubuntu and others) has the AI service diagnosis, the AI agent setup audit and the SSRF check too (Linux 1.11.27).
- **SSH brute force blocking** (1.12.0): on a Mac with Remote Login (sshd) enabled, the unified log is followed, failures are counted, and pf drops only new connections to the SSH port (by default 6 failures in 10 minutes; the first ban is 1 hour, a repeat offender gets 4 times as long, the cap is 7 days). Established connections are not cut. The gateway, DNS, the Mac itself and loopback are never blocked; the exclusion list, sources that succeeded with a public key (30 days) and sources of authenticated SSH sessions are exempt; private IPs are not. With Remote Login disabled the log is not read. On by default when Pro is activated; when fail2ban or similar is active it only detects and notifies. Turning off the guard or blocking releases all current bans, and the guard is part of the emergency recovery steps. **Brute force coming from IPv6 on the same LAN is not blocked automatically, so as not to cut off the whole LAN** (it is only recorded). Notifications only for a ban and for a login that succeeds after failures (critical). Read-only MCP tool `get_ssh_guard_incidents` (the Mac tools became 33). Measured on a disposable macOS 26 VM: about 0.25 seconds from the 6th failure to the block. The sshd log cannot be read by a standard user or the sandboxed helper (measured), so it is read by a small root process (`--ssh-log-reader`) that starts before the sandbox and only launches `/usr/bin/log` with fixed arguments; the lines are interpreted by a sandboxed parse worker running as `nobody`, because an attacker can write them.
- **FIM notifications carry the SSH logins of the same period** (1.12.0): the owner and last modification, the SSH logins that started from 30 minutes before the change to about 1 minute after it (source, user, authentication method, time, still connected or not), and sessions that had been running since earlier. **macOS does not tell which process wrote a file, so this is a coincidence in time, not proof of cause** (the notification says so too).
- **Log audit fix on macOS 26** (1.12.0): the periodic log audit did not pick up SSH logins and failures, because OpenSSH 10 writes the login lines as `sshd-session`, not `sshd`. `sshd-session` is now covered, specified by name (matching by prefix would pick up unrelated `sshd-*` helpers).
- **Advisory score from a rewrite classifier in ransomware detection** (1.12.0, a port of the Linux edition's classifier): when a burst happens, 22 features are taken from the first, middle and last 4 KiB of up to the 20 most recent files, and a logistic regression computes a "ransomware-likeness" from 0 to 1. **On Mac it is advisory only**: the score is kept in the log and in the saved alert, the response does not change, a model that fails verification is not used, and bursts that touched canaries or honeytokens are not scored. FSEvents does not tell which process wrote, so the meaning of the inputs differs from Linux and the Linux numbers do not carry over.
- **AI service diagnostic fix** (1.12.0): "usable from the network" was shown even for services listening only on 127.0.0.1; only listeners reachable from the network are now diagnosed (the same construction as the bug confirmed on a real Linux machine).
- **MCP config audit** (1.12.0): a new finding `injection_exposure` (medium) in `audit_mcp_configs`, once per file where the same config has both a server that makes third-party text readable (web fetch, browser, search, chat, email, GitHub and so on) and a server that can run commands or change files, the arrangement that indirect prompt injection (ATT&CK T1698) depends on. It is only a match against known package and image names; combinations across files are not added up, and arguments, environment variables and tokens are not included in the result.
- **Bundled apps** (1.12.0): AccessScope 0.3.7, PathScope 0.1.9 and PersonalSOC 0.1.14 (see each app's entry), also delivered through the in-app update. Of the PersonalSOC changes, the ones that concern Mac are the new notice for an OS package CVE matching that could not be completed, and the order of the Settings tabs. The added wording is in 10 languages (based on machine translation, not reviewed by native speakers).
- (1.12.1) **Fixed: the honeytokens (decoy credential files) silently dropped every detection of a read.** To watch a decoy, the app itself holds the file open. On each detection it runs `lsof` to find who has it open, but the reader has usually exited by then, so only the app itself is left, and it was taken for the reader. A process of our own bundle counts as an "expected access", so the detection was dropped without a notification or a record. I installed the published 1.12.0 on a real Mac (a macOS 26 VM), confirmed that reading all five decoys produced no event at all, and then fixed it. The app's own process is now excluded, and when no reader is found it is reported as "could not be identified".
- (1.12.1) **Fixed: the honeytokens missed every read of a decoy after the first.** macOS (APFS) updates a file's access time only on the first read after a write (a second `cat` of the same file leaves it alone), and kqueue reports no reads. A detection that looked at the access time every 30 seconds saw only the first read of a decoy, and when the app had read an existing decoy itself after a restart (to check it), it saw none of the attacker's reads. The access time is now set back to one second before the modification time at start, when a related decoy is added, and after every detection, so the next read moves it again. The kqueue notification caused by that is ignored for 2 seconds.
- (1.12.1) On a real Mac (a VM), with the fixed build, the first read of a decoy, a second read of the same decoy, and a read of another decoy were all detected within 30 seconds.
- (1.12.1) The Linux honeytokens use a different mechanism (fanotify) and were not affected by this (Linux 1.12.1 fixed a different missed-read problem).
- (1.12.1) The bundled apps are unchanged (AccessScope 0.3.7, PathScope 0.1.9, PersonalSOC 0.1.14).
- **Lockfile tamper notification repeating** (1.12.2): the record of already-announced changes lived only in memory, so the same notification came at every launch. The baseline is deliberately never advanced on its own, so even the change from your own `npm install` was announced each time.
